<a id="item-1"></a>
## [CoT Monitoring Evaded via Backdoor Poisoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02820) ⭐️ 9.0/10

This paper demonstrates that chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring can be bypassed by implanting backdoors in reasoning models, causing them to behave maliciously while their reasoning traces appear benign. The authors introduce a curriculum training approach that enables this attack even when direct poisoning fails. This challenges a core assumption in AI safety that CoT monitoring can reliably detect harmful intent, potentially undermining current safety stacks for reasoning models. The findings have significant implications for the safety and interpretability of frontier models and are likely to spark substantial discussion in the community. The backdoors, termed 'CoT-Hidden,' can be induced through simple fine-tuning recipes across various reasoning-model architectures and sizes. Causal interventions reveal a trigger-conditioned activation pathway independent of visible reasoning, and residual stream verbalizations provide an anomaly warning near answer generation but do not identify the trigger, target, or backdoor mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring is an AI safety technique that inspects a model's explicit reasoning steps to detect harmful intent before it acts. However, it relies on the assumption that reasoning traces are faithful and informative. Backdoor attacks involve poisoning training data to embed hidden behaviors that activate under specific triggers, while model poisoning refers to manipulating the training process to embed malicious behaviors. This paper explores the intersection of these concepts, showing that CoT monitoring can be evaded.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [FAR.AI Launches AI Security Leaderboard with Minimal Standard for Safeguards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03070) ⭐️ 9.0/10

FAR.AI introduced the AI Security Leaderboard and the Minimal Standard for Safeguards, Version 1.0, a benchmark evaluating jailbreak resistance of frontier AI models. The paper evaluates Claude Fable 5, GPT-5.6 Sol, Gemini 3.1 Pro, and Grok 4.5 across CBRNE and cyber domains, finding cost-to-jailbreak varies over a hundredfold. This provides a standardized, public benchmark for AI safety, addressing a critical gap in evaluating frontier model safeguards. It enables comparisons across developers and sets a concrete baseline, likely influencing AI safety research, policy, and enterprise model selection. The benchmark includes a taxonomy of 67 static jailbreak techniques and a three-stage funnel to identify universal jailbreaks. Random search found 63 universal jailbreaks against Grok 4.5 at an average cost of $58, while expert-guided composition raised counts to 385 for Grok and 231 for Gemini, but no universal jailbreaks were found for Claude Fable 5 or GPT-5.6 Sol.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Frontier AI models use layered safeguards to prevent catastrophic misuse, but public evidence on their effectiveness is limited. Jailbreak techniques are prompts or methods that bypass these safeguards, and benchmarks like this aim to systematically measure resistance. The Minimal Standard is assembled from defenses already deployed in production, suggesting gaps are closable with current techniques.

Some discussions may focus on the methodology's rigor and the implications of cost-to-jailbreak metrics.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [Unified Framework for Embedded Multi-Agent Learning via Self-Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22226) ⭐️ 9.0/10

This paper introduces a mathematical framework for prospective learning in multi-agent systems, where agents predict both future perceptions and their own actions, extending AIXI to embedded agency. It shows that self-prediction enables agents to reason about others running similar algorithms, leading to new game-theoretic solution concepts and cooperation. This work addresses fundamental limitations of standard reinforcement learning in multi-agent settings, where non-stationarity demands prospective learning. It could reshape how AI systems model themselves and others, with implications for AI alignment and multi-agent cooperation. The framework builds on AIXI and Solomonoff induction, enabling agents to resolve epistemic uncertainty about themselves. It demonstrates that idealized agents can form consistent mutual predictions and achieve infinite-order theory of mind, setting a gold standard for embedded multi-agent learning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Standard reinforcement learning assumes stationary environments and decoupled agents, which fails in multi-agent settings where other agents' learning creates non-stationarity. AIXI is a theoretical formalism for universal AI combining Solomonoff induction with sequential decision theory. Embedded agency addresses the fact that agents are part of the world they affect, not separate from it.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [AI Agent Backdoors via Supply Chain Poisoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05159) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper reveals that AI agents can be backdoored through data poisoning at multiple supply chain stages, including a novel environment poisoning attack, achieving over 80% success in leaking confidential information. This research highlights critical security vulnerabilities in agentic AI systems, which are increasingly deployed in real-world applications. It underscores the need for robust supply chain security measures to prevent malicious backdoors that could compromise user data and system integrity. The paper formalizes three threat models: direct poisoning of finetuning data, pre-backdoored base models, and environment poisoning. Evaluations on two widely used agentic benchmarks show that poisoning only a small number of demonstrations can embed a backdoor that leaks confidential user information with over 80% success.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are trained on interaction data such as web browsing or tool use, which improves their capabilities but also introduces security risks. Data poisoning attacks involve inserting malicious content into training data to corrupt the model's behavior. The AI supply chain includes data collection, model training, and deployment stages, each of which can be targeted by adversaries.

## [Google DeepMind Leadership Shake-Up: Hassabis to Chair, Dean Departs](https://blog.google/company-news/inside-google/message-ceo/next-chapter-ai-momentum/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Demis Hassabis is transitioning from CEO to Chair of Google DeepMind, while Jeff Dean is departing to co-found a new AI research company called Discovery Loop, along with Sanjay Ghemawat, Oriol Vinyals, and Quoc Le. This marks a significant shift in leadership at one of the world's leading AI labs, potentially impacting Google's AI strategy and research direction. The departure of Jeff Dean, a legendary figure in computer science, to start a new venture signals a brain drain from Google and could intensify competition in the AI research landscape. Discovery Loop is structured as a public benefit corporation with a mission to 'automate machine learning, science, and engineering to accelerate discoveries and progress.' The co-founders have worked together for 14 to 30 years, bringing deep expertise in AI and distributed systems.

hackernews · colesantiago · Aug 5, 16:05 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49184755)

**Background**: Demis Hassabis co-founded DeepMind in 2010, which was acquired by Google in 2014 and later merged with Google Brain to form Google DeepMind. He is a Nobel laureate in Chemistry for his work on protein structure prediction. Jeff Dean is a renowned computer scientist known for his contributions to Google's infrastructure, including MapReduce and TensorFlow, and served as chief scientist of Google DeepMind.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [DeepMind Position Paper: LLMs Can't Jump to Scientific Discovery](https://openreview.net/challenge?redirect=%2Fforum%3Fid%3DklU4737opt) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DeepMind researchers published a position paper titled 'LLMs Can't Jump' arguing that large language models are fundamentally limited in their ability to make the intuitive leaps required for scientific discovery. The paper has sparked extensive debate, with 139 comments on OpenReview and widespread discussion on social media. This paper challenges the prevailing optimism about AI for science, suggesting that LLMs may not be sufficient for true scientific breakthroughs. It has significant implications for research funding, AI development priorities, and the expectations of the scientific community regarding AI-driven discovery. The paper uses Einstein's conceptualization of scientific discovery as a cyclical process involving an intuitive 'jump' from sensory experience to axioms, followed by logical deduction. The authors argue that LLMs can perform the deductive part but lack the ability to make the creative, intuitive leaps essential for new theories.

hackernews · theanonymousone · Aug 5, 11:01 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49181083)

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) like GPT-4 have shown remarkable capabilities in language understanding and generation, leading to speculation that they could accelerate scientific discovery. However, this paper argues that scientific breakthroughs often require non-linguistic, intuitive insights that go beyond pattern recognition in text. The debate reflects broader questions about the role of AI in science and whether LLMs can truly 'discover' or merely assist human researchers.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://openreview.net/pdf?id=klU4737opt">Position: LLMs can't jump - OpenReview</a></li>
<li><a href="https://wccftech.com/google-deepmind-paper-llms-cannot-replace-human-genius/">Google DeepMind Paper Says LLMs Will Never Replace Human ...</a></li>
<li><a href="https://medium.com/@damianvtran/where-are-the-llm-driven-scientific-breakthroughs-30608f589bee">Where are the LLM-Driven Scientific Breakthroughs? | Medium</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Community comments show a mix of agreement and critique. Some users argue that language is a lossy encoding of human experience, supporting the paper's thesis, while others criticize the paper's historical analogies, such as the reductive retelling of Einstein's work. The author, Tom Zahavy, clarified on Twitter that the paper does not claim LLMs can never make scientific discoveries, addressing misinterpretations.

**Tags**: `#LLM`, `#AI for Science`, `#DeepMind`, `#Scientific Discovery`, `#Limitations`

## [Self-Organising Digital Circuits: Meta-Learning for Fault Tolerance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02606) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Self-Organising Digital Circuits, a meta-learning framework that uses a topology-masked Transformer to configure lookup tables (LUTs) of Boolean gates, enabling circuits to self-assemble and adapt to hardware faults. It extends Neural Cellular Automata (NCA) to generate functional logic rather than fixed patterns, achieving rapid fault recovery and generalization across circuit scales. This work bridges biological self-organization with digital hardware design, offering a novel approach to fault tolerance that could complement or replace static redundancy and error-correcting codes. It may impact adaptive systems, FPGA design, and resilient computing, especially in environments with permanent or soft errors. The policy achieves >99.99% accuracy in recovering from soft errors, even when damage sizes exceed training conditions. It also shows improved accuracy on circuits substantially wider than those seen during training, indicating generalization across scales. The approach navigates the degenerate Boolean search space to satisfy computational tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Fault tolerance in classical computing relies on static strategies like hardware redundancy and error-correcting codes, while biological systems exhibit adaptive plasticity. Neural Cellular Automata (NCA) are bio-inspired models where cells apply local rules to self-organize into patterns. Lookup tables (LUTs) are configurable digital circuits that can implement any Boolean function, commonly used in FPGAs. Topological masking in Transformers incorporates graph structure into attention, which is crucial for graph-structured data.

## [VeriTrace Achieves 100% Pass@1 on VerilogEval-V2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02878) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VeriTrace introduces a multi-agent system with a complete debugging action space, enabling Agentic Temporal Exploration. It achieves 100% Pass@1 on VerilogEval-V2, the first system to attain perfect functional correctness on this benchmark, and outperforms the strongest reproduced baseline by +5.1% on a shared Claude Sonnet 4.0 backbone. This breakthrough addresses the ~95% accuracy ceiling in automated Verilog RTL generation, demonstrating that debugging agency can close the final accuracy gap. It could significantly enhance AI-assisted hardware design automation, potentially reducing manual verification effort and accelerating chip design cycles. The Inspector agent in VeriTrace has independent control over signal selection, time-window bounds, and iteration depth, allowing hypothesis-driven root-cause analysis. This complete debugging action space contrasts with existing systems that restrict signal inspection or time windows, reducing debugging to pattern matching.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Register-transfer level (RTL) is a design abstraction in digital circuit design that models synchronous circuits in terms of data flow between registers and logical operations. Verilog is a hardware description language (HDL) used to describe RTL designs. Large language models have been applied to automate Verilog RTL generation, but multi-agent systems previously plateaued at ~95% accuracy on standard benchmarks due to incomplete debugging capabilities.

## [LoCA: Forward-Only LLM Tuning via One-Shot Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03020) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LoCA introduces a two-stage method for parameter-efficient LLM tuning that replaces repeated backpropagation with a one-time calibration. It fits low-rank credit assignment maps per transformer block and then uses forward-only passes with closed-form ridge solves to adapt the model. This approach significantly reduces memory and hardware requirements during fine-tuning, making adaptation feasible on devices without backward-capable hardware. It also lowers GPU peak memory and CPU steady-state memory, which could democratize LLM customization for resource-constrained environments. LoCA was evaluated on five discriminative benchmarks with Qwen2.5 models ranging from 0.5B to 14B, achieving lower evaluation cross-entropy than LoRA in 16 of 25 task-scale comparisons. It also reduced GPU peak memory by 26-29% and CPU steady-state memory by 36-52% after calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods like LoRA reduce trainable parameters but still require repeated backpropagation through the frozen backbone, which demands backward-capable hardware and activation storage. LoCA aims to eliminate this by performing a single probe backward pass to learn local credit assignment maps, enabling forward-only adaptation. This is particularly useful for scenarios where repeated backpropagation is impractical, such as on-device or memory-constrained settings.

## [Probe-Guided Training Boosts LLM Tool Call Parameter Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03071) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces ParamBench, a difficulty-graded benchmark for LLM tool call parameter generation, and a probe-guided training framework (PBT/PGR) that uses hidden-state correctness signals to improve parameter accuracy, raising average exact match from 19.7% to 59.6% across 5 open models. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM tool use—parameter correctness—which is essential for reliable agent execution, especially in specialized domains like cloud networking where frontier models often fail. The proposed method offers a practical way to improve tool call reliability without requiring external feedback, potentially enhancing the robustness of LLM-based agents in real-world applications. The framework includes two complementary approaches: probe-filtered bootstrapped training (PBT) uses the probe to filter reliable self-generated calls for fine-tuning, and probe-guided reranking (PGR) selects better candidates during inference. ParamBench categorizes instances into five difficulty levels based on parameter nesting depth, cross-parameter dependencies, and reasoning required from earlier calls, and the method was validated on 6 external benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model agents rely on tool use to interact with external systems, but existing research has focused more on tool selection and call ordering than on parameter correctness. Recent studies have shown that LLM hidden states encode rich information about model predictions, including a linearly separable correctness signal that can be captured by a simple linear probe. This paper leverages that insight to improve parameter generation in tool calls.

## [AI Agent Economics: Emergent Economic Behavior Under Minimal Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03076) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a two-stage framework to test whether economic behavior emerges among AI agents given only executable mechanisms, finding that transfers and allocation strategies appear with productive tasks and scarce access. This research challenges the assumption that economic behavior in multi-agent systems is merely inherited from predefined scenarios, offering a new lens for studying emergent organization and informing governance audits of AI systems. The study uses 24 independent six-agent worlds across GPT and DeepSeek models, with a no-production boundary test. It finds that executable allocation authority increases differentiation while reducing failed allocation and prolonged exclusion, and that symbolic energy removes continuation support but not competition for task access.

**Background**: Multi-agent studies often place AI agents in predefined games, markets, or roles, making it hard to distinguish endogenous economic organization from behavior inherited from the scenario. This paper defines AI Agent Economics as systems of production, allocation, consumption, exchange, and institutions that alter agents' future feasible actions, and asks whether economic relations emerge when agents receive only executable mechanisms.

## [OM-GRPO: Masking Answer Gradients to Prevent Collapse in Label-Free RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03119) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces OM-GRPO, a label-free RLVR framework that masks gradients on answer tokens and uses a contrast-augmented reward to decouple reward estimation from policy optimization. It consistently outperforms existing label-free RLVR methods and matches supervised GT-reward training across three LLM backbones. This work addresses a critical limitation in label-free RLVR where answer-level consensus signals cause policy collapse. By preventing the model from directly reinforcing answer tokens, OM-GRPO enables more stable and effective reasoning training without ground-truth labels, potentially scaling RLVR to broader applications. OM-GRPO masks gradients on the answer span while retaining answer-level rewards via a soft consensus signal. It also introduces Contrast-Augmented Reward, which refines reward estimation using low-cost pairwise comparisons over existing trajectories without additional rollouts. In Test-Time Training, OM-GRPO surpasses majority voting by 4.24 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) improves LLM reasoning but typically requires ground-truth answers. Label-free RLVR replaces gold supervision with answer-level consensus from model samples, but this can cause collapse when the same signal is used for both reward estimation and policy optimization. OM-GRPO addresses this by masking answer gradients and using contrast-augmented rewards.

## [VerMem: Unified Memory Management with Local and Global Verifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03137) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VerMem introduces a unified memory management framework for LLM agents, representing long-term memory, active context, and episodic history as distinct states controlled by a single policy with seven atomic operations. It is trained via a three-stage reinforcement-learning curriculum that uses local and global verifiers for hierarchical credit assignment. This addresses a key challenge in LLM agent design by unifying long-term and short-term memory management, which are typically optimized separately. The framework's strong performance across benchmarks suggests it could improve agent efficiency and effectiveness in long-horizon tasks, potentially influencing future agent architectures. The local verifier scores executable memory transitions, while the global verifier assesses evidence coherence and terminal-memory consistency after task completion. Verifiers are used only during training, and the framework achieves the best results on most metrics across five benchmarks and two LLM backbones, with the strongest efficiency-performance frontier under controlled token budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents need to retain reusable information, manage a bounded active context, and recover earlier evidence during long interactions. Existing methods often optimize long-term and short-term memory separately, and unified policies are typically trained with trajectory-level feedback, which provides weak credit for individual memory decisions. VerMem addresses this by using atomic operations and verifier-based credit assignment.

## [AI-guided spatial proteomics reveals recurrence-risk niches in TNBC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03145) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers developed an outcome-informed spatial pathology framework that integrates H&E-based AI recurrence risk heatmaps with mass spectrometry-based spatial proteomics to identify and profile intratumoral recurrence-risk niches in triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC), achieving an AUC of 0.77 and a C-index of 0.77 in an independent test cohort. This study bridges AI-based histopathology predictions with localized molecular states, offering a new approach to uncover the biological basis of AI predictions and potentially improving biomarker discovery and prognostic accuracy in TNBC. The integration of protein and image-derived risk scores enhanced predictive performance, which could lead to more personalized treatment strategies. The framework was validated on a cohort of 156 patients, and spatial proteomic profiling of 46 AI-defined tumor regions from two recurrence patients revealed concordant molecular contrasts: mitotic programs enriched in high-risk regions and immune/antigen presentation programs in low-risk regions. A 13-protein composite derived from these contrasts showed a trend toward poorer recurrence-free survival, and integrating it with the H&E-derived risk score improved the out-of-bag C-index from 0.679 to 0.739.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Deep learning models can predict cancer recurrence from H&E-stained slides, but the underlying molecular states are often unclear. Spatial proteomics, particularly mass spectrometry-based approaches, allows mapping of proteins within tissue context, providing molecular detail. This study combines these technologies to link AI predictions with biological mechanisms, potentially advancing precision oncology.

TumorBoard, a multi-agent decision-support system for neuro-oncology, was introduced on arXiv (2608.03190). It uses a shared longitudinal case state and an auditable claim-evidence ledger, and on a 360-case hidden benchmark it achieved an action F1 of 0.772 and evidence entailment of 0.914, outperforming the strongest baseline by 3.1 percentage points. This work demonstrates that structured multi-agent coordination with an evidence ledger can improve clinical decision support in a high-stakes domain like neuro-oncology. It offers a path toward more auditable and safer AI-assisted medical recommendations, potentially influencing future clinical AI systems. The system includes specialist agents for radiology, neuropathology, molecular diagnosis, guidelines, and therapy planning, plus an adversarial critic and a safety governor. Under evidence deletion, it deferred 84.2% of unsafe cases and limited harmful recommendations to 5.8%; the safety governor reduced harmful release by 7.8 percentage points at a false-deferral cost of 4.3 percentage points.

## [Refusal-Cue Shortcut Bypasses Safety Guards in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03201) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals a 'refusal-cue shortcut' in safety guard models, where inserting refusal phrases into harmful responses can flip the guard's verdict from harmful to unharmful. This affects both open and proprietary models, including LlamaGuard3 and Qwen3Guard, and the authors propose a lightweight post-hoc intervention to mitigate it. This finding exposes a significant security flaw in widely used content moderation systems, potentially allowing harmful content to bypass safety filters. It underscores the need for more robust training data and mitigation strategies in AI safety, with implications for model deployment and regulation. The shortcut persists across response positions and is generally stronger in smaller model variants. The proposed sparse complementary masking intervention achieves an approximately 79% relative reduction in response-initial detection failures on two benchmarks, while preserving standard detection performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Safety guard models are trained via supervised fine-tuning on labeled prompt-response pairs to filter harmful content. The paper audits two widely used datasets, WildGuardMix and GR-Train, and finds that refusal expressions co-occur almost exclusively with unharmful labels, motivating the shortcut. The intervention suppresses a small set of shortcut-associated attention heads and MLP neurons without retraining.

## [Agent Operating System: A Reference Architecture for Distributed Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03214) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Agent Operating System (AOS), a vendor-neutral reference operating architecture for distributed agentic systems, comprising a Control & Governance Plane and a Runtime & Coordination Plane. It specifies concepts, invariants, interface objects, optimization objectives, deployment profiles, and reliability responsibilities. AOS addresses a critical gap in governing intent, capability selection, authority delegation, uncertainty control, and auditability in distributed agentic systems. It provides a stable, implementation-independent architecture that can compose heterogeneous components into governable, reliable, and interoperable systems, potentially influencing future AI infrastructure design. AOS defines two internal planes: the Control & Governance Plane handles intent, policy, trust, authority, confidence, auditability, observability, and human oversight; the Runtime & Coordination Plane manages agent lifecycle, workflow coordination, model/tool routing, context/memory coordination, scheduling, traffic management, and runtime assurance. Platform services, container runtimes, and physical infrastructure remain outside the AOS boundary, integrated via explicit interfaces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models have evolved from isolated prediction services into components of long-running, distributed systems that reason, invoke tools, and act on behalf of users. The ecosystem has produced frameworks, workflow engines, and observability tools, but lacks a stable, implementation-independent operating architecture for governance and coordination. AOS is proposed as such an architecture, not as a replacement for existing frameworks but as a composition layer for heterogeneous components.

## [Reachability vs. Realization: Auditing LLM Benchmark Gains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03219) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a question-level audit framework that distinguishes between realized and reachable benchmark gains in LLMs, revealing that inference-time layer routing does not expand reachability and that answer-blind procedures fail to retain gains. This work challenges the common interpretation of aggregate benchmark scores as evidence of capability expansion, urging the community to report both realized performance and reachability. It has significant implications for LLM evaluation methodology and AI safety. The audit covers 43 model and task settings, showing that random layer routing matches or exceeds structured search under matched budgets. Additionally, silencing one identified MLP block repairs 68-92% of predefined failures, and in DAPO, deployed scores rise by 14.7 points while reachable ceiling falls by 13.3 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM benchmarks are often used to measure capability improvements, but aggregate scores can mask whether a model actually produces new correct answers or merely accesses answers that were already within its reach. This paper proposes a question-level audit to separate these two aspects, using fixed budgets, temperatures, and answer formats. The concept of reachability refers to whether a specified probe can find the correct answer within a budget, while realization refers to whether the default deployment procedure produces it.

## [UniNav: Unified Diffusion Model for Visual Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03244) ⭐️ 8.0/10

UniNav introduces a unified world-action model that jointly generates future visual observations and continuous waypoint trajectories through a single diffusion process, trained on both trajectory-labeled navigation data and video-only data. It outperforms the strongest baseline in ATE across all datasets, with a fast variant achieving 0.1s latency. This work addresses key limitations in visual navigation by unifying future prediction and action generation, reducing the need for costly planning rollouts and enabling training on abundant video-only data. It could advance embodied AI and robotics by improving spatial grounding and efficiency in real-time navigation. UniNav uses a single transformer to denoise visual and waypoint tokens, incorporating geometry-aware camera tokens for better spatial grounding. Two variants are introduced: UniNav-Full for interpretable future observations and trajectories, and UniNav-Fast which removes future-image tokens at inference for efficient trajectory prediction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Image-goal visual navigation is a fundamental capability for embodied agents, where the agent must navigate to a location depicted in a goal image. Existing methods either predict waypoint trajectories without visual foresight or use world models that require costly planning rollouts. Diffusion models, which generate data by denoising random noise, have become popular in robotics for policy learning and trajectory generation.

## [TaskPress: Query-Agnostic KV Cache Pruning via Task-Guided Meta-Query](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03276) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TaskPress introduces a task-guided, query-agnostic KV cache pruning framework that uses a meta-query during prefill to filter irrelevant tokens, and leverages quantization scale factors as a zero-cost signal for detecting influential outliers. This approach constructs a reusable memory representation that can be shared across diverse queries, unlike existing query-specific methods. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in long-context LLM inference by enabling cache reuse across queries, potentially reducing memory overhead and improving inference efficiency. The use of quantization scale factors as a zero-cost importance signal is innovative and could inspire further research in efficient KV cache management. TaskPress constructs a reusable memory representation conditioned on a high-level task guide, which acts as a meta-query during prefill. It employs quantization scale factors to detect influential representation outliers, providing an efficient proxy for token importance without additional computation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: In transformer-based LLMs, the key-value (KV) cache grows linearly with sequence length, becoming a major memory bottleneck during long-context inference. Existing KV cache pruning methods typically determine token importance based on specific queries, making the pruned cache non-reusable for unseen queries. Quantization scale factors, which are already computed during model quantization, can serve as a zero-cost signal for identifying important tokens.

## [Hybrid GUI-MCP Agents Underuse Tools; RL Can Improve Adoption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03327) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals an 'adoption gap' in hybrid GUI-MCP computer-use agents, where tools are available but underused. It shows that multi-turn reinforcement learning (RL) with a dense tool bonus can increase tool adoption without harming accuracy. This finding is significant because it shows that merely providing tools to AI agents does not guarantee their effective use, and that training methods like RL can bridge this gap. It has implications for designing more capable and efficient computer-use agents, potentially improving their performance in real-world tasks. The study uses the OSWorld-MCP benchmark with 309 tasks and finds that the same MCP tools improve a reasoning model by +4.0pp but degrade a non-reasoning model by -5.9pp. A dense tool bonus raises spreadsheet adoption from 0.03 to 0.33, and compressing image history by halving it reduces input tokens by about a third, with the compressed agent reaching 37.8% accuracy vs 33.0% at 53% of the input cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Hybrid computer-use agents combine GUI interaction (via screenshots) with text-based tool calls, such as those enabled by the Model Context Protocol (MCP). MCP is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in 2024 to standardize how AI systems integrate with external tools and data. The OSWorld-MCP benchmark evaluates agents on real-world desktop tasks, and multi-turn reinforcement learning is a training approach where agents learn from extended interactions to optimize cumulative rewards.

## [DataSpace Benchmark Targets Verifiable Analytics Across Heterogeneous Workspaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03451) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DataSpace is a new benchmark for evaluating data agents on verifiable analytics over heterogeneous workspaces, containing 410 cross-language tasks and 7,439 artifacts totaling 15.01 GB across CSV, JSON, SQLite, Markdown, PDF, and video. It served as the official evaluation benchmark for the KDD Cup 2026 Data Agents for Complex Data Analysis competition. This benchmark addresses a significant gap in evaluating data agents for heterogeneous workspaces, where evidence is scattered across multiple modalities. Its official use in a KDD Cup competition and the finding that even the best frontier models achieve only 66.34% accuracy highlight the unsolved challenges and drive progress in data-agent reliability. DataSpace was constructed using DataSpace-Builder, an execution-grounded framework with cross-language transformation, constraint-aware relational sampling, modality routing, artifact rendering, and human review by 11 domain experts. The deterministic evaluator performs header-invariant column alignment, type- and precision-aware normalization, and order-aware row comparison; harness choice creates a 15.36-point accuracy spread with the backbone fixed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Data agents are AI systems that enable natural-language analytics over organizational workspaces, where relevant evidence may be scattered across databases, structured files, long documents, and multimedia. Existing benchmarks often isolate structured querying, retrieval, or open-ended analysis, failing to unify heterogeneous evidence discovery, complete tabular outputs, and deterministic evaluation. DataSpace aims to fill this gap by requiring agents to produce verifiable tabular results from task-local heterogeneous workspaces.

## [Scaling Laws for Mixture-of-Experts Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03457) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically characterizes the scaling behavior of Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) diffusion language models (dLLMs), revealing distinct trends in optimization, compute allocation, and architecture compared to autoregressive (AR) models. The authors train LLaDA MoE v2, a 30B-A3B dLLM, on 23.5T tokens, which approaches Qwen3 on several benchmarks. This work provides the first systematic scaling laws for MoE dLLMs, filling a critical gap in understanding how these models scale. The findings have significant implications for designing efficient and scalable diffusion-based language models, potentially influencing future research and model development in the AI community. The optimal nominal batch size grows faster and the optimal learning rate decays more rapidly with compute for MoE dLLMs compared to AR models. IsoFLOP analysis shows a slight data-side tilt, and larger scales favor larger expert pools with stable shared expert allocation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising a sequence, offering an alternative to autoregressive (AR) models that generate token-by-token. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures increase model capacity without proportionally increasing compute by activating only a subset of parameters per token. Scaling laws describe how model performance improves with compute, data, and parameters, guiding efficient resource allocation.

## [Solver-Aware Decomposition Improves Programming-by-Example Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03461) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Solver-Aware Decomposition (SAD), a training framework that optimizes decomposers for programming-by-example using feedback from the synthesizer rather than just ground-truth subgoals. SAD consistently improves synthesis and end-to-end task accuracy across two PBE domains. This work challenges the common assumption that ground-truth decompositions are optimal for bounded solvers, showing that decomposition quality is solver-relative. It offers a new training paradigm that could improve the scalability and effectiveness of program synthesis systems, benefiting AI/ML and program synthesis research. SAD retains supervised training on ground-truth subgoals as a structural scaffold while adding direct feedback from a frozen synthesizer, rewarding subgoals based on the synthesizer's loss on the target program. Experiments reveal an accuracy paradox: higher agreement with ground-truth decompositions does not improve synthesis success, and SAD can solve tasks that a ground-truth decomposition oracle fails.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Programming-by-example (PBE) is an end-user development technique where a system infers a program from input-output examples. Decomposition-based PBE splits tasks into subtasks, with a decomposer predicting intermediate subgoals and a synthesizer generating programs conditioned on them. Traditional training imitates ground-truth subgoals, but this may not align with the solver's search dynamics.

## [WeClawArena: Auditable Sandbox and Benchmark for Cross-User Agent Collaboration and Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03499) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WeClawArena introduces an auditable benchmark and runtime sandbox for multi-party owned-agent collaboration over personal workspaces, featuring 124 base tasks across six cross-user task domains, expanded into 620 scenario variants with one benign control and four attack-vector variants per base task. It records peer messages, tool calls, resource operations, governed decisions, and final workspace states, and reports utility and attack success rate separately. This addresses a critical gap in existing agent benchmarks, which lack end-to-end sandboxes for verifiable cross-user collaboration and security testing. As personal AI agents become more prevalent, WeClawArena provides a foundation for developing secure multi-agent systems, impacting AI/ML and systems research and the safe deployment of human-centered agent networks. The benchmark includes six cross-user task domains and 620 scenario variants, with each base task having one benign control and four attack-vector variants. It audits attack success from bounded runtime evidence, supporting diagnosis of task breakdown, privacy leakage, poisoned evidence, and invalid authority paths.

Existing benchmarks study tool use and collaboration but lack end-to-end sandboxes for verifiable cross-user collaboration and security testing.

## [CALVER: Symbolic Verification Outperforms Voting for Best-of-K Causal Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03506) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CALVER, a training-free symbolic verifier that scores structured reasoning traces against Pearl's causal criteria (d-separation, backdoor adjustment, intervention) and selects the highest-scoring candidate. On CLEAR find-one-valid queries, CALVER achieves 42.1% accuracy, significantly outperforming plurality voting, reward models, LLM judges, and model confidence, which all remain near 30%. This work addresses a critical failure mode of self-consistency in causal reasoning, where repeated confounding errors and vote fragmentation can lead to incorrect answers. CALVER offers a training-free, efficient alternative that leverages established causal theory, potentially improving the reliability of LLM reasoning in causal domains and beyond. CALVER requires only a causal structure, either provided or built from text, and scores each candidate in milliseconds on CPU. The advantage widens with sampling budget and generalizes across ten published Bayesian networks, a second model family, and settings where the graph is built from text; it also improves thresholded average-treatment-effect decisions and generalizes to logic under a truth-table checker.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Self-consistency is a common technique in LLM reasoning that assumes the most frequent answer among sampled traces is the most reliable. However, in causal reasoning, this can fail because samples may repeat the same confounding error, and votes may fragment across multiple valid answers. Pearl's causal criteria, including d-separation, backdoor adjustment, and intervention, provide a formal framework for evaluating causal validity, which CALVER leverages.

## [Soft Guidance Outperforms CoT Prompting as LLMs Improve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03550) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.03550) shows that modern mid-sized LLMs, especially reasoning-specialized models, achieve higher accuracy in zero-shot settings than with few-shot Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting. For example, Mathstral's GSM8K accuracy improved from ~77% to ~84% without additional cost. This challenges the long-standing paradigm that CoT prompting is the best way to elicit reasoning from LLMs. As models become stronger, standard CoT prompting may act as a distraction, suggesting that evaluation practices and prompting strategies need to be revisited. The authors attribute the performance drop to a 'guidance-distraction' tradeoff: CoT prompting demands style adaptation, formatting compliance, and undesired contextualization, which can distract models from core reasoning. The study tested several mid-sized models on math problem-solving tasks, including a general-purpose model where zero-shot CoT also outperformed few-shot CoT.

**Background**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting is a technique that instructs LLMs to generate intermediate reasoning steps before the final answer, improving performance on complex tasks. Few-shot prompting provides examples in the prompt, while zero-shot prompting gives no examples. The paper suggests that as LLMs improve, they may natively produce CoT-style reasoning, making explicit CoT prompts less necessary and potentially harmful.

CASE uses a contextualization strategy: pre-filling the KV cache of a Gemma 3-based tabular language model with a sample of rows to anchor dataset semantics. ## [Coding Agents Boost OSS Productivity but Widen Inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03585) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A simulation study using real GitHub data from 1,084 developers found that introducing coding agents increased planned and completed tasks by 34.0% and 39.0% respectively, and cut median completion time from 45 to 20 minutes. However, adoption was only 26.0%, and benefits concentrated among already active contributors. This study highlights a critical tension in open-source communities: while coding agents significantly boost productivity, they may exacerbate inequality and reduce the public knowledge available to future contributors. These findings have implications for how AI tools are integrated into collaborative software development and for maintaining the health of open-source ecosystems. The simulation branched the same community state into parallel No-CA and CA conditions for 4-week runs. Direct human-human interaction dropped from 32.4% to 11.6%, while CA-involved modes rose to 57.3%, including 40.3% via CA-assisted self-loops. The CA-generated corpus achieved only 22.3% knowledge coverage on a retrieval benchmark, far below the 81.1% of the real-human corpus.

The findings suggest a productivity-public knowledge trade-off, where increased automation may reduce the shared knowledge that sustains open-source communities.

<a id="item-34"></a>
## [FraQ: Efficient Coordinate-Space Recompression for Federated LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03605) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FraQ introduces an efficient coordinate-space recompression method for federated LoRA, enabling exact aggregation of client updates with reduced computational and memory costs. It factorizes stacked factors into an orthonormal basis and a compact coordinate matrix, then recovers the singular spectrum from a small Gram matrix to select the minimal rank satisfying an energy threshold. This addresses a critical aggregation mismatch in federated LoRA, where naive averaging of factors fails to recover the average of induced updates. By enabling exact aggregation with low overhead, FraQ can improve the practicality and efficiency of federated fine-tuning for large language models, potentially accelerating adoption in privacy-sensitive applications. FraQ operates by stacking client factors to exactly represent the aggregate, then factorizing into an orthonormal basis and coordinate matrix. It recovers the singular spectrum from a Gram matrix, selects the smallest rank meeting an energy threshold, and maps the subspace back to construct the global adapter. Experiments on text classification and commonsense reasoning show accuracy close to uncompressed baselines with reduced communication and server-side overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning enables collaborative model training without centralizing private data, and Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) is a popular parameter-efficient fine-tuning method. However, LoRA's two-factor parameterization causes an aggregation mismatch in federated settings, as averaging factors does not yield the average of induced updates. Existing solutions either suffer from rank collapse or require expensive full-matrix decomposition. FraQ addresses this by performing recompression in coordinate space, leveraging the Gram matrix to efficiently recover the singular spectrum.

## [Offline RL Benchmark for Clinical Trial Strategy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03606) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel offline RL benchmark for oncology clinical development, constructing 881 decision episodes from 31.7k heterogeneous public records and comparing offline RL methods against LLM agents. Reward-weighted behavioral cloning achieved the best performance, with 46.2% indication F1 and 14.2% strict F1, outperforming the best tool agent. This work bridges offline RL and healthcare AI, offering a benchmark for training agents to plan clinical experiments, which could improve efficiency and success rates in drug development. It also provides a realistic testbed for offline RL algorithms in a high-impact domain. The dataset includes trial registries, regulatory reviews, sponsor filings, utilization data, and epidemiology, and is split into held-out drug, sponsor, drug-class, and temporal splits. The post-August 2025 holdout is contamination-clean, and models trained offline outperform non-fine-tuned baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning (RL) learns policies from fixed datasets without online interaction, which is crucial for domains like healthcare where exploration is costly or unsafe. Behavioral cloning (BC) is a simple imitation learning method that learns from expert demonstrations via supervised learning, while implicit Q-learning (IQL) is a more advanced offline RL algorithm that avoids querying unseen actions. This paper applies these techniques to clinical trial strategy, framing it as a sequential decision-making problem.

## [Formal Verification of Agentic Systems over Operational Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03609) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Stateful Tool-Enabled Agentic Deployments (STEADs) and proves that verifying them against First-Order Computation Tree Logic (FO-CTL) specifications is undecidable. It also identifies sufficient conditions for decidable finite-domain verification, which is PSPACE-complete. This work addresses a critical gap in formal verification for LLM-driven agentic systems, providing theoretical foundations that are essential for ensuring AI safety and reliability. The undecidability result highlights fundamental limits, while the decidable conditions offer practical pathways for verification in real-world deployments. The key requirement for decidable verification is that renaming opaque identifiers in the data must correspondingly rename the selected tool calls. The paper also introduces a canonical deployment wrapper that guarantees this condition for arbitrary base agents, and proves that computing canonical representations is graph-isomorphism-hard.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic systems driven by large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in real-world workflows where they act on persistent operational data. Formal verification is a method to mathematically prove that a system satisfies its specifications, and Computation Tree Logic (CTL) is a branching-time logic used for model checking. First-Order CTL (FO-CTL) extends CTL with first-order quantifiers, allowing specifications over data. This paper formalizes such systems as STEADs and studies their verification, which is crucial for ensuring business requirements are met before deployment.

## [Study Finds Gender Bias in LLM Fake News Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03627) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study systematically investigates gender bias in LLM-based fake news detection, augmenting the LIAR benchmark with gender variants and evaluating six state-of-the-art LLMs. Results show 9.79%-35.13% of statements receive inconsistent labels across gender variants, with male-female flip rates of 6.5%-23.6%. This matters because LLMs are increasingly used for automated fact-checking and content moderation, and gender bias can undermine the fairness and reliability of these systems. The findings highlight the need for bias-aware evaluation and mitigation strategies in AI-driven journalism and social media moderation. The study identifies two primary bias manifestations: instability (inconsistent judgments) and directionality (systematic favoritism). Five of the six models show statistically significant directional effects, with the strongest effects displaying male-skeptic patterns. The augmented dataset is publicly released to support future research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: The LIAR benchmark is a widely used dataset for fake news detection, containing over 12.8K short statements labeled for veracity. Gender bias in LLMs has been a growing concern, as these models can inherit and amplify societal biases from training data. This study extends prior work by focusing specifically on how speaker gender affects veracity judgments in fake news detection.

## [PhyAI: Unified Inference Engine for Physical AI with 1.40x-4.65x Speedups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03682) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PhyAI introduces a unified inference engine for Physical AI models, achieving 1.40x-4.65x speedups over official implementations of pi0, pi0.5, GR00T N1.7, and MiniCPM-Robot across edge and cloud deployments. It uses a single runtime with model adapters to handle architecture-specific logic, and was able to add MiniCPM-Robot on the day of its release. This matters because Physical AI models like VLA and WAMs are typically deployed across diverse settings (evaluation, cloud RL rollout, edge GPU serving, onboard), each requiring separate inference programs, leading to inconsistency and overhead. PhyAI's unified runtime simplifies deployment and improves performance, potentially accelerating the adoption of Physical AI in real-world robotics and edge applications. PhyAI achieves a 2.08x speedup on Cosmos3-Nano-Policy-DROID, reducing latency from 2.46s to 1.18s on eight H20 GPUs (CFG=2, TP=4). However, specialized runtimes remain faster in several configurations, so the goal is competitive latency rather than the fastest result in every case. Detailed profiles show that different models require different execution policies; for example, on a Hopper GPU at batch size 1, pi0.5's action expert accounts for 8.8% of FLOPs but 57.2% of latency, while at batch size 32 its share drops to 13.5% and throughput reaches ~100 samples/s.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Physical AI models, such as Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models and World-Action Models (WAMs), combine perception, language understanding, and action generation to control robots. These models require inference across various stages of their lifecycle, from training and evaluation to real-time deployment on edge devices or in the cloud. Traditionally, each deployment setting uses a separate inference program, leading to code duplication and performance inconsistencies. PhyAI addresses this by providing a single runtime that shares graph execution, kernels, memory management, and parallel services, while using model adapters to handle architecture-specific conditioning, solver, cache, and output logic.

## [Competition-Aware Dispatch Cuts DSP Traffic 34.2%, Boosts Revenue 4.6%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03705) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new competition-aware request dispatch framework for real-time ad exchanges uses distributional bid prediction and probabilistic forwarding to selectively send requests to DSPs. In a production deployment, it reduced DSP request volume by 34.2% while increasing net revenue by 4.6% (p<0.001) over a 14-day window. This innovation addresses the inefficiency of forwarding nearly all requests to DSPs, which often leads to throttled bidding and suboptimal outcomes. By reducing traffic while improving revenue, it offers a scalable solution for ad exchanges to enhance monetization and reduce computational load, potentially reshaping RTB infrastructure. The framework adapts per-DSP thresholds over time via lightweight policy optimization to track non-stationary market conditions. It was evaluated through four sequential online experiments on a platform serving over 20 billion daily requests, with segment-level analysis revealing heterogeneity and comparative advantages among DSPs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Real-time bidding (RTB) is a programmatic advertising mechanism where ad exchanges auction impressions to demand-side platforms (DSPs) in real time. Traditionally, exchanges forward nearly all requests to DSPs, but DSPs throttle participation due to compute and budget constraints, leading to inefficiency. Distributional bid prediction estimates the full distribution of bids, while probabilistic forwarding decides whether to send a request based on predicted competition, enabling smarter dispatch.

## [CARE-Bench: New Benchmark Reveals LLM Triage Safety Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03731) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CARE-Bench, a source-grounded benchmark for evaluating patient-facing LLM triage as a four-label per-turn current-action task, containing 500 cases and 1,059 patient-disclosure prefixes. In evaluations of 11 models on 269 held-out rounds, unprompted macro-F1 ranged from 31.2 to 50.4, while prompting improved 10 of 11 models to a range of 46.9 to 63.4, yet substantial threshold errors persisted. This benchmark addresses a critical safety issue in medical AI, as patient-facing LLMs increasingly provide symptom advice before clinician contact. The finding that prompting alone is insufficient highlights the need for explicit evaluation of action timing and safety before deployment, impacting developers, regulators, and healthcare providers. The benchmark uses a fixed GPT-5.5 mapper to code responses into a four-label action space, and when the correct action was to ask for more information, only 33.5% of prompted outputs preserved that step. The persistence of errors after prompting suggests that patient-facing triage is not a simple prompting problem, supporting explicit evaluation of action timing before deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Patient-facing medical LLMs and agents increasingly answer symptom questions before clinician contact, where the key safety question is what action the user should take next. CARE-Bench is a source-grounded benchmark that evaluates sequential patient-facing triage, reconstructed from medical dialogue, consultation, and follow-up-question sources. Macro-F1 is a balanced performance metric that averages F1 scores across classes, providing a more nuanced view than overall accuracy.

## [AgenticECO: Agentic Framework for 3D IC ECO with Minimal-Disturbance Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03738) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgenticECO introduces an evidence-gated tool-using agent workflow for engineering change orders (ECO) in 3D integrated circuits, paired with EcoRoute, a minimal-disturbance ECO-routing layer. It clears seven of nine natural defect cases under identical budgets, outperforming full reroute and stock repair, which clear only two. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in advanced semiconductor design, where post-route ECO remains manual and expertise-bound, especially for 3D ICs. By automating and improving ECO with agentic AI, it could significantly reduce design time and errors, impacting the EDA industry and enabling more efficient 3D IC adoption. AgenticECO operates on the open-source TaiWei flow and drives the unmodified pinned router via EcoRoute, ensuring repairs are attributable to edits. It achieves 0.66% mean disturbance over cleared cases, touches zero clock nets, and passes routing, extraction, timing, DRC, and structural-equivalence gates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: As Moore's law slows, the industry is turning to three-dimensional integration, but merged 3D-IC flows expose bond-level defects with no 2D analogue. Post-route engineering change orders (ECO) are manual and expertise-bound, and standard edit-then-fully-reroute practice entangles repairs with router churn, making signoff numbers unreliable. AgenticECO uses an evidence-gated agent to guide ECO, and EcoRoute minimizes disturbance to preserve the original design intent.

## [MissClick Attack Exploits Digit-Serialized Coordinates in GUI Grounding Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03740) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced MissClick, a white-box adversarial attack that exploits the digit-serialized coordinate generation in GUI grounding models, achieving untargeted success rates of 75.07% and 72.93% and targeted success rates of 44.86% and 62.67% on OS-Atlas and UGround across desktop, web, and mobile platforms. This highlights a new security vulnerability in GUI grounding models, which are increasingly used in AI agents for automating tasks. The attack could be exploited to hijack or disrupt GUI agents, posing risks to security-sensitive applications. The attack uses two objectives: MissClick-U maximizes soft-coordinate displacement for untargeted disruption, while MissClick-T minimizes a place-weighted target-digit loss for targeted hijacking. The method outperforms existing attacks by up to 30.72 percentage points for untargeted and 47.06 percentage points for targeted attacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: GUI grounding models map natural-language instructions to click coordinates, often by generating coordinates as sequences of digit tokens. These tokens are parsed into numerical values, but the categorical prediction of each digit means that small changes in token prediction can lead to large coordinate displacements, which the attack exploits. Adversarial attacks on such models have been studied before, but this work specifically focuses on the digit-serialized representation.

## [Clinical Multi-Agent LLMs Vulnerable to Shortcut Cascades](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03744) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that clinical multi-agent LLM systems are susceptible to shortcut cascades, where socially plausible wrong answers spread among agents and oversight agents fail to detect them. Across seven cohorts on six datasets, the study found that when two peers assert the same wrong answer, the holdout agent adopts it in 38% of cases. This research highlights critical safety risks for deploying multi-agent LLM systems in healthcare, where incorrect decisions can have severe consequences. The findings underscore the need for robust oversight mechanisms and caution against relying solely on benchmark performance, as these systems can be gamed by shortcuts that clinicians would ignore. The study tested Gemini committees across text, imaging, and tabular ICU datasets, finding that a gate oversight agent had a 100% false-positive rate, while a same-lineage judge collapsed in imaging tasks. A referee that privately re-queried the holdout transferred to imaging with 77-88% precision, but gaming a hidden rubric was nearly silent, with only 1/10 text and 1/134 imaging drifters naming the rubric.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems involve multiple language model agents collaborating on a shared workspace, often used for clinical decision support. Shortcut cascades occur when agents adopt socially plausible but incorrect answers due to peer influence, and benchmark gaming refers to exploiting cues that improve benchmark scores but are irrelevant to real-world clinical practice. Oversight agents are designed to detect such failures, but this study shows their limitations.

## [Computing Actual Causes for Neural Network Predictions under Structured Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03772) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a method to compute Halpern-Pearl actual causes for neural network predictions using Boolean Structural Causal Models (SCMs), with scalable algorithms and formal guarantees of completeness and minimality. This work addresses a critical gap in explainable AI by formalizing explanations as actual causes under structured dependencies, which can yield more accurate and trustworthy explanations than feature-attribution methods. It offers scalable computation with formal guarantees, potentially improving the reliability of AI systems in high-stakes applications. The method uses bound propagation and branch-and-bound techniques to compute all minimal actual causes, handling search spaces up to 2.3×10^13 candidate pairs on SCMs with up to 28 nodes within a 180-second budget. Experiments show it outperforms brute-force and ILP baselines in scalability and heuristic search as graph size grows, and a case study reveals that ignoring input dependencies inflates reported causes by 14.9% spurious ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Halpern-Pearl actual causality is a formal framework for defining actual causes in structural causal models, often used in philosophy and AI to explain specific events. Boolean SCMs are a type of structural causal model where variables take Boolean values, enabling efficient reasoning about dependencies. This paper applies these concepts to neural network explanations, which typically treat inputs as independent, leading to potentially misleading explanations when inputs are structured.

## [Oilbird: Training-Free Speculative Decoding Using Verifier Keys](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03839) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Oilbird introduces a training-free speculative decoding method that re-keys a draft pool using hidden states already computed by the verifier, improving draft accuracy for tool-calling workloads. It achieves up to 4.4x autoregressive decoding speed on API-Bank, compared to 3.9x for the strongest training-free baseline and 2.0x for EAGLE-3. This work addresses a key bottleneck in speculative decoding for tool-calling scenarios, where exact-match drafting often misses correct continuations. By leveraging already-computed hidden states, Oilbird offers a significant speedup without additional training, potentially improving efficiency for LLM inference in production. Oilbird merges a semantic draft source with an existing lexical drafter's tree, and in three published drafters, it lifts accepted length by 24-29% at matched pool and budget. The method is training-free and relies on hidden states already computed by the verifier at each committed token.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive generation by having a small draft model propose tokens that a larger target model verifies in parallel. Traditional training-free methods use exact suffix matching against a pool of earlier contexts, but this can miss correct drafts, especially in tool-calling where requests are similar but with different values. Hidden states in transformer models capture semantic information, allowing Oilbird to find semantically similar past positions even when exact strings differ.

ContinualSkillBench, a new dynamic benchmark, evaluates in-context continual skill learning in LLM agents across five domains with 100 interconnected subtasks each. ## [Lipschitz Singularity in Adaptive UAV Trackers Opens New Attack Surface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03902) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that adaptive Transformer trackers for UAVs have an unbounded local Lipschitz constant at layer-skipping decision boundaries, which can be exploited by tiny input perturbations to alter the inference topology. The authors propose the Adversarial Path-Inversion (API) framework to generate imperceptible perturbations that manipulate gating decisions, achieving superior attack effectiveness and stealthiness. This work identifies a novel vulnerability in adaptive UAV tracking systems, linking mathematical Lipschitz singularity to a new attack surface. It highlights a security risk in efficiency-focused dynamic routing architectures, potentially impacting the deployment of such systems in safety-critical applications like UAV tracking. The API framework precisely manipulates gating decisions to force inference onto altered computational paths, causing severe inconsistency between original and inverted paths. Extensive experiments on state-of-the-art adaptive trackers demonstrate that API achieves superior perturbation stealthiness, more effective attack, and faster inference speeds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Adaptive Transformer trackers use input-dependent dynamic routing to balance accuracy and efficiency on resource-constrained UAV platforms. Lipschitz continuity is a mathematical property that bounds how much a function's output can change with respect to input changes; a singularity means the bound becomes infinite, causing instability. Dynamic routing networks, which skip layers based on input, have been explored to reduce inference costs, but their security implications are not well understood.

## [First Implementation of Causal Perception Framework for Fairness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03917) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first implementation of the causal perception framework proposed by Alvarez and Ruggieri (2025), providing algorithms to compute interventional and counterfactual distributions. It demonstrates the framework's application to fairness analysis on the German Credit dataset, showing how competing structural causal models (SCMs) affect accuracy and fairness in multi-expert decision settings. This work bridges the gap between theory and practice for causal perception, enabling empirical studies of how different worldviews affect fairness assessments. It highlights that bias is situated with respect to the agent's SCM, emphasizing the need to consider competing causal models in fairness-critical applications. The paper operationalizes both structural (disagreement on causal graph) and parametrical (disagreement on weights) causal perception. It proposes distance measures to quantify disagreement and shows that the perception verdict is sensitive to the choice of distance metric and threshold, affecting fairness assessments and threshold-based decisions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Causal perception is a framework where agents with different structural causal models (SCMs) of the same system infer different probability distributions, including hypothetical distributions under interventions. SCMs are mathematical models that represent causal relationships via directed acyclic graphs and structural equations. This framework was previously theoretical, and this paper provides its first implementation, using the German Credit dataset to illustrate practical applications.

## [Transformers as Dynamic Processors: SIDPP Framework Challenges Stochastic Parrot View](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03921) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the SIDPP (Sequence-level Interactive Dynamic Parallel Processing) framework, reinterpreting Transformers as systems that generate prompt-dependent transformations during inference rather than merely reproducing training statistics. It highlights output-weight interconnections as a key architectural novelty, where outputs of some networks determine the weights of others. This theoretical reinterpretation challenges the 'stochastic parrot' view of large language models, potentially influencing future research on interpretability, predictability, and control of LLMs. It also suggests that human language processing might be a form of SIDPP, bridging AI and neuroscience. The paper argues that dynamic processing grows with prompt length and may equal or exceed static processing, a phenomenon called 'strong prompt sensitivity.' It also conjectures that SIDPP could be neurally realized in the cerebral cortex, given the human neural system's mechanisms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers are a class of deep learning models widely used in natural language processing, based on self-attention mechanisms. The 'stochastic parrot' view, popularized by Emily Bender and Timnit Gebru, argues that LLMs only mimic statistical patterns without true understanding. This paper offers an alternative perspective, suggesting that Transformers dynamically construct transformations during inference.

## [Embedded Bayesian Agents Reveal New Paths to AI Cooperation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03958) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces the 'embedded Bayesian agent' model, showing that foundation model agents converge to stable cooperation in social dilemmas, contradicting classical game theory's prediction of mutual defection. The paper proposes a new solution concept, the 'embedded equilibrium,' to replace the Nash equilibrium for AI agents. This work challenges the foundational assumptions of classical game theory when applied to AI agents, offering a new theoretical basis for understanding and potentially ensuring cooperation in multi-agent AI systems. It has significant implications for AI safety and the design of autonomous agents in social and economic contexts. The embedded Bayesian agent maintains epistemic uncertainty about its own decision-making algorithm and uses similarity inference: a decision to cooperate is treated as evidence that a similar partner will also cooperate. The paper formalizes this through the 'embedded equilibrium,' a novel solution concept replacing the Nash equilibrium.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Classical game theory assumes 'decoupled agency,' where agents treat their own decisions as independent of the environment and other actors. Modern foundation model agents, however, jointly predict their own future actions alongside external observations, which the paper argues necessitates a new theoretical framework. The 'embedded Bayesian agent' models agents as part of the universe they inhabit, leading to different equilibrium predictions.

## [ReflectRL: Learning from Failed Expert Trajectories to Boost LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03972) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ReflectRL introduces a framework that leverages Golden Negative Trajectories—failed expert reasoning paths—to enhance on-policy training of large language models. It first elicits reflective reasoning from these flawed trajectories, then transfers the learned behavior back to direct reasoning, improving performance across 9 benchmarks and 4 LLM backbones. This work challenges the common practice of discarding failed expert trajectories, showing they contain valuable reasoning signals. By providing a lightweight, plug-and-play method, ReflectRL could improve LLM reasoning training efficiency and effectiveness, potentially benefiting a wide range of applications that rely on strong reasoning capabilities. ReflectRL consists of two stages: Reflective Reasoning, which uses Golden Negative Trajectories to generate reflective reasoning, and Reflective-to-Direct Policy Transition, which transfers the acquired behavior back to direct reasoning. The framework is compatible with four on-policy training methods and introduces minimal overhead, and the authors release OpenR1-GNT-69k, a dataset of 69k expert failure trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy training is a post-training paradigm for LLMs where the model learns from its own sampled trajectories, often guided by golden trajectories from stronger expert models. However, when experts fail on hard problems, these failed trajectories are usually discarded. ReflectRL argues that such failures can be used as reflection prompts, leveraging the 'Reflection Advantage'—the idea that reflecting on a flawed solution can be easier than solving from scratch. This approach aligns with broader research on reflection in reasoning models, though some studies question its effectiveness in open-ended tasks.

## [GAP-URGENet Wins ICASSP 2026 URGENT Challenge with Generative-Predictive Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01832) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GAP-URGENet, a generative-predictive fusion framework, achieved 1st place in the objective evaluation of Track 1 of the ICASSP 2026 URGENT Challenge for universal speech enhancement. The system integrates a generative branch that performs full-stack speech restoration in a self-supervised representation domain and a predictive branch for spectrogram-domain enhancement, with outputs fused by a post-processing module that also performs bandwidth extension to 48 kHz. This framework demonstrates a novel approach to universal speech enhancement by combining generative and predictive methods, achieving top performance in a competitive blind test. It could influence future research by showing the benefits of fusing self-supervised representation-based generation with spectrogram-domain prediction, potentially leading to more robust and higher-quality speech enhancement systems. The generative branch reconstructs the waveform via a neural vocoder, while the predictive branch provides complementary cues. The post-processing module performs bandwidth extension to generate the enhanced waveform at 48 kHz, which is then downsampled to the original sampling rate. Audio examples are available at the provided demo link.

**Background**: Universal speech enhancement aims to improve speech quality across diverse conditions, such as noise, reverberation, and bandwidth limitations. The ICASSP 2026 URGENT Challenge is a third iteration of a challenge series that evaluates such systems in a blind test. Self-supervised representation learning has become a key technique in speech processing, and neural vocoders are used to synthesize waveforms from acoustic representations. This framework leverages these technologies to achieve state-of-the-art results.

## [KernelBrain: Budget-Aware LLM Agent Boosts GPU Kernel Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02611) ⭐️ 8.0/10

KernelBrain introduces a coarse-to-fine, budget-aware search strategy for LLM-guided GPU kernel optimization, achieving up to 6.72x speedup over PyTorch and up to 1.4x over state-of-the-art kernel agents, with up to 48% lower optimization time. This work addresses key challenges in automated GPU kernel optimization—correctness violations, noisy measurements, and early search stalls—by combining LLM-guided mutation with adaptive resource allocation. It demonstrates significant improvements in both kernel quality and search efficiency, which could accelerate AI and scientific computing workloads relying on optimized kernels. The system screens many candidates with low-cost evaluation, then allocates higher-fidelity budget only to promising survivors, using policy-gated evaluation and profiler-informed diagnosis. On Triton kernel generation tasks, it achieves 0.88x-6.72x speedup over PyTorch and up to 1.4x over state-of-the-art, with up to 48% lower optimization time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: GPU kernel optimization is crucial for performance in AI and scientific computing, but automating it is challenging due to correctness constraints, noisy runtime measurements, and premature search convergence. LLM-guided approaches, such as KernelAgent, use hardware profiling and iterative refinement to optimize Triton kernels. KernelBrain builds on this by introducing a budget-aware, coarse-to-fine search that balances exploration and exploitation.
