## [MemArena: A New Benchmark for On-Device Personal Memory Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02613) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MemArena is a new benchmark for evaluating on-device personal memory assistants, built with the MASim agent simulator, simulating 50 agents over 15 days. It reveals that memory backend selection (e.g., Memobase vs. MemSearch) impacts content accuracy more than scaling the reader model. This benchmark addresses a gap in evaluating ego-centric, multi-session conversational memory for edge deployment, providing actionable insights for AI/ML practitioners. The finding that memory backend choice matters more than model scaling could guide future on-device AI system design. The benchmark includes 10.3M dialog-text tokens and 24.1K text-only ego-observed tokens per agent per day, evaluating six dimensions: recall, reasoning, and trustworthiness. Results show permission-aware access fails universally, and search latency adds a fixed 87/7/48 ms (BM25-RAG/Memobase/MemSearch) on a Spark GB10 edge node.

**Background**: On-device personal memory assistants are AI systems that run locally on edge devices, handling private conversations with open-weight models. Existing benchmarks often fail to test the combination of activity-dense interaction, ego-centric perspective, and coherent multi-session worlds, which MemArena aims to fill. Memory backends like Memobase and MemSearch are systems that store and retrieve conversational memory for AI agents.

## [OpenAI Privacy Filter Evaluation Reveals Strengths and Weaknesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02616) ⭐️ 8.0/10

An independent evaluation of OpenAI's Privacy Filter (OPF) across 42 benchmarks, 22 languages, and 5 domains was released. The study shows OPF excels on structured PII but degrades significantly on narrative prose and non-Latin scripts. This evaluation provides crucial insights for practitioners and researchers using OPF for privacy workflows, highlighting where it can be trusted and where it may fail. It also informs the broader AI community about the limitations of current PII detection models, especially in multilingual and narrative contexts. OPF achieves F1=0.855 on AI4Privacy and 0.464 on SPY medical, outperforming Presidio and XLM-RoBERTa on PII-annotated benchmarks. However, it collapses on non-Latin scripts (Arabic: 0.04, Cyrillic: 0.03) and shows recall-biased behavior on customer-support and medical/legal PII.

## [Instruction Stacking Collapse Benchmark and Prompt Compilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02639) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a benchmark that stacks up to 20 verifier-checked instructions to study how LLMs degrade in instruction-following, and proposes a training-free instruction compiler that rewrites stacked prompts in a single LLM call. The findings reveal non-linear degradation in instruction-following, with follow rates dropping from ~96% to as low as 20% due to pairwise conflicts, highlighting a practical problem for prompt engineering and system design. The capability-graded benefit of the instruction compiler suggests that weaker models, often deployed at scale, can gain up to +11 points in follow rate, which could improve real-world LLM applications. The benchmark includes 24 atomic instructions, of which 22 are deterministic and form the active pool, and evaluates three production-tier LLMs: Claude Sonnet 4.6, GPT-5-mini, and Gemini 2.5 Flash. The instruction compiler's benefit is attributed to the rewrite itself, not to additional tokens or reordering, as confirmed by cluster-robust tests and same-baseline controls.

**Background**: Production prompts often contain multiple instructions, such as requiring valid JSON, a word limit, and a specific tone simultaneously. This paper studies how instruction-following degrades as constraints accumulate, introducing a benchmark and a training-free compiler to mitigate the issue. The concept of prompt compilation is related to existing tools like the Prompt Compiler on GitHub, which uses implicit weight update dynamics from transformer theory.

## [Agentic LLMs Encode Latent Signals of Indirect Prompt-Injection Exposure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02657) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that agentic LLMs encode latent signals of indirect prompt-injection (IPI) exposure in their hidden states, detectable with linear probes achieving 90%+ AUROC across six models including a 753B-parameter model. It also introduces AGRI, a probe-gated reasoning-based defense that reduces attack success rates from 34.6% to 0% on difficult AgentDojo settings. This work addresses a critical security vulnerability in agentic LLMs by providing novel insights into model internals and a practical defense, bridging the recognition-action gap. It has significant implications for AI safety and the deployment of autonomous agents in real-world applications. The probes are trained on pre-generation hidden states and show high robustness under adaptive attacks and cross-lingual settings. AGRI prepends anti-injection reasoning on demand, maintaining clean-task utility while substantially reducing attack success rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Indirect prompt injection (IPI) attacks occur when malicious instructions are hidden in external content that an LLM processes, such as tool results or web pages, causing the model to execute unintended actions. Agentic LLMs, which autonomously interact with tools and environments, are particularly vulnerable. Linear probes are simple classifiers trained on intermediate representations to detect whether certain information is encoded, aiding interpretability.

## [Chunked Zero-Knowledge Attestation for Fair-Lending Audits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02664) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a chunked zero-knowledge circuit design that attests aggregated fair-lending metrics, such as the demographic-parity gap, on committed batches of real 2022 HMDA data. It demonstrates end-to-end performance with 32 zkSNARK proofs, achieving an attested gap within 0.0029 of the true value and per-chunk proving under 4 seconds. This work addresses a critical need for privacy-preserving audits of ML models in regulated industries, enabling banks to demonstrate fairness without exposing model weights or customer data. It provides a practical path to comply with U.S. banking regulations like SR 11-7 and OCC 2011-12, potentially influencing future regulatory technology. The design uses a chunked approach to avoid overflow issues in flat summation circuits and compilation intractability in tree-reduction circuits. It also includes a nonce-based sampling protocol to prevent cherry-picking, and a proposed mapping of nine SR 11-7 control elements to zero-knowledge statements, though only two controls are fully implemented.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Zero-knowledge proofs (ZKPs) allow one party to prove a statement's truth without revealing underlying data. In fair lending, regulators require banks to audit ML models for discrimination, but sharing model details can compromise privacy. This paper applies ZKPs to attest aggregated fairness metrics, balancing transparency and confidentiality.

## [Docker Hub Security Study: 96.3% Images Vulnerable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02669) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study introduces ChimangoScan, a pipeline that scanned 52,895 high-exposure Docker Hub repositories with six scanners, finding that 96.3% of images have known package vulnerabilities and 93.4% have critical ones. The study also reveals that 66.8% of vulnerability findings are flagged by only one scanner, highlighting tool dependence. This large-scale empirical study provides critical insights into the prevalence and tool-dependence of vulnerabilities in Docker Hub images, which underpin most container deployments. The findings underscore the need for multi-scanner approaches and challenge the reliability of single-tool vulnerability assessments, impacting security practices for developers and organizations. The study analyzed 12.7 million repositories and 663.8 billion pulls, reconstructing a layer graph with 54.4 million edges. It found that 98.0% of images have at least one CIS Docker Benchmark misconfiguration, and TruffleHog flagged secrets in 76.9% of images, but manual labeling showed 99.7% were non-credentials. A single zlib CVE propagates to 1.13 million downstream images, affecting 47.3% of total exposure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Docker Hub is a central registry for container images, and vulnerabilities in base images can propagate to all derived images. Prior studies used single scanners, leaving tool-dependence unquantified. ChimangoScan addresses this by using multiple scanners and an exposure score that considers downstream usage, providing a more comprehensive security assessment.

This research addresses a critical gap in coding agent evaluation by testing under realistic enterprise security constraints, which is essential for safe deployment in production environments. The findings reveal that model choice is policy-dependent, guiding organizations in selecting agents that balance success and efficiency under their specific security posture. Under the strictest policy, the model that best preserves success also loses the most efficiency, indicating a trade-off between the two axes. Failures manifest as timeouts or wrong solutions rather than early stops, with the mix varying by model. The paper verifies task solvability under the strictest policy to separate model failures from policy-foreclosed tasks.

This paper introduces nested security policy levels to simulate these real-world constraints.

## [Dynamic Routing Adaptive Alignment Defends Against White-Box Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02674) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DRAA, a framework that dynamically adds compensatory routes to preserve refusal behavior when a model's safety route is compromised by white-box attacks. It identifies and masks the safety route, then mines failure cases to construct preference pairs for alignment. This addresses a critical gap in AI safety: existing defenses rely on static safety units, which are vulnerable to targeted route-level white-box attacks. DRAA offers a dynamic defense that improves robustness without sacrificing general utility, potentially enhancing safety for large foundation models in open environments. DRAA first localizes the safety route by contrasting internal activations between safe and unsafe calibration samples, then masks it to induce failures and mines those failures to create preference pairs. Experiments show it restructures pathway dependence, improving robustness against route-level attacks while preserving utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large foundation models (LFMs) are increasingly deployed in open environments, where safety threats are shifting from black-box jailbreaks to white-box attacks that directly target internal safety neurons or routes. Existing defenses often rely on static safety units or fixed refusal pathways, which are vulnerable to such targeted attacks. DRAA introduces dynamic compensatory routes to maintain robust refusal behavior when the safety route is compromised.

## [LLM Code Reviewers Confuse Probability with Policy, Modular Pipeline Improves Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02677) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.02677) shows that LLM code reviewers conflate probability estimation with decision-making, and that changing the cost policy alters reported failure probabilities by 13.6 to 16.9 percentage points. The authors propose a modular pipeline that separates risk elicitation from policy application, reducing mean loss by .073 per issue at equal costs. This finding is critical for deploying LLMs in software engineering, as current systems are poorly calibrated for cost-sensitive decisions, leading to suboptimal actions. The proposed modular approach offers a practical path to improve reliability and reduce losses in automated code review. The study used 15,792 responses on 720 candidate patches, with matched calls where patch and monitor evidence were fixed. Under a 10:1 false-accept policy, all reviewers performed worse than rejecting all patches, but applying the same rule to probabilities elicited under equal costs reduced loss for all systems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM code reviewers typically estimate patch risk and make approval decisions in a single prompt, conflating probability estimation with decision-making. In decision theory, probabilities should depend on evidence, while costs should determine the action taken from them. This paper empirically tests whether deployed reviewer interfaces preserve this separation, and evaluates a modular pipeline that separates these concerns.

## [SPIKE-Bench: New Benchmark Quantifies Biosecurity Risks in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02684) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced SPIKE-Bench, a benchmark and evaluation funnel that quantifies biosecurity risks in large language models (LLMs) by assessing their ability to generate predicted toxin-like sequences. The benchmark includes 631 curated toxin-design prompts across seven functional categories and a three-stage protocol that filters outputs through compliance, biological plausibility, and predicted toxicity. This addresses a critical blind spot in AI safety evaluation, as current safety tests operate in natural language and cannot determine if a model-generated amino acid sequence is biological gibberish or a computational risk signal. The audit of 32 LLMs reveals that most models freely comply with toxin-design requests, with a Functional Harmfulness Rate (FHR) reaching 50.7%, highlighting the urgent need for domain-specific biosecurity evaluation. The SPIKE funnel produces stage-level diagnostics and an aggregate function-aware metric, the Functional Harmfulness Rate (FHR). The study also provides BioSafe-Guard, a domain-specialized classifier that substantially reduces predicted functional risk while preserving benign utility, and the benchmark and classifier are released on GitHub.

## [OptR: Output-Aware Rotation for INT2 KV-Cache Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02691) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces OptR, an output-aware rotation method for INT2 KV-cache quantization that minimizes post-output-projection attention error. It decomposes the error into key- and value-induced terms and learns per-head orthogonal corrections through the full INT2 quantization and attention path. This addresses a critical bottleneck in long-context LLM inference by reducing KV cache memory usage with INT2 quantization while preserving accuracy. It consistently improves existing methods like QuaRot and OSCAR across multiple models and benchmarks, potentially enabling more efficient deployment of long-context models. OptR applies an attention-equivalent key reparameterization to reduce large channel-wise offsets without changing the softmax distribution. It preserves the paged KV-cache format with negligible inference overhead, and is validated on three models and five reasoning and coding benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache is a major memory bottleneck in long-context LLM inference, and ultra-low-bit quantization (like INT2) is important to reduce memory. Existing rotation-based methods optimize cache statistics or proxy errors before the complete attention readout, but OptR targets the actual post-output-projection error. The output projection (W_O) is a key component in transformer attention, and minimizing error after it directly improves final outputs.

<a id="item-67"></a>
## [NANQ: Noise-Aware Mixed-Precision Quantization for Analog CIM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02700) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NANQ introduces a noise-floor-aware mixed-precision non-uniform quantization framework for analog compute-in-memory (CIM), which models magnitude-dependent weight noise from measured eFlash CIM responses and adaptively allocates precision based on noise profiles. On-chip experiments show that under 2-bit weight-magnitude quantization, NANQ improves vision-model accuracy by 8.05 percentage points and reduces language-model perplexity by 54.7% on average over PowerQuant. This work addresses a critical practical issue in analog CIM: hardware noise degrades low-bit quantized models, and existing methods ignore the noise floor, leading to inefficient precision allocation. By making quantization noise-aware, NANQ enables more accurate and efficient neural network inference on analog CIM hardware, which is crucial for energy-efficient AI deployment at the edge. NANQ converts the measured noise profile into an adaptive quantization density, assigning finer resolution to low-noise regions and avoiding ineffective precision in noise-dominated regions. It also assigns layer-wise bit-widths by identifying each layer's precision saturation point under hardware noise using a unified threshold; mixed-precision NANQ achieves most of the gains with only 3.2-3.8 equivalent bits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Analog compute-in-memory (CIM) performs neural network computations directly in memory, reducing data movement and improving energy efficiency. However, device variations and read noise can severely degrade low-bit quantized models. Non-uniform quantization allocates more levels to high-probability regions, and mixed-precision assigns different bit-widths to different layers, but existing methods often ignore the hardware noise floor.

## [World Modeling Paradigm Shift to Agent-Centric Proxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02713) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper 'Quo Vadis, World Modeling?' introduces Agent-Centric Interactive World Proxies, shifting world modeling from physical state prediction to agent-usable information transitions. It organizes these proxies into six functional forms and three progressive levels of agent empowerment. This paradigm shift could significantly impact AI agent development by providing more versatile feedback mechanisms, enabling agents to plan better, learn faster, and evolve continually. It broadens the applicability of world modeling beyond raw state prediction, potentially accelerating progress in reinforcement learning and interactive learning. The paper defines six proxy types: dynamics, spatial, execution, memory/experience, skill, and reward/verification proxies. It also outlines three levels of empowerment: L.1 Inference-Time Guidance, L.2 Training-Time Optimization, and L.3 Agent-Proxy Co-Evolution.

The paper introduces SIEVE, a search-inspect-fetch interface that uses fielded Boolean retrieval (BQL) to filter documents based on structural fields like titles and headings. Across three QA collections, SIEVE achieves higher accuracy than conventional search-visit methods while reducing token usage by 20.7-50.6%. This work addresses a key inefficiency in deep-research agents by leveraging document structure to reduce irrelevant content in context, leading to both accuracy gains and significant token savings. SIEVE filters candidates over document fields, ranks the admitted set, presents structure-rich result cards for inspection, and fetches only selected sections. The paper shows that BQL filtering improves all tested rankers and that the accuracy-context advantage persists across different retrievers and agent backbones.

The Gaussian value κ=3 eliminates the leading dispersion contribution.

## [SAGE: Semantic Explainability for Attention-Based Survival Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02803) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SAGE is a post-hoc framework that provides global, language-grounded explanations for attention-based multiple instance learning (ABMIL) survival models in computational pathology. It uses a pathology vision-language model to score image patches against 25 histological concepts and aggregates these scores with learned attention to quantify each concept's relationship to prediction risk across a cohort. This addresses a significant gap in interpretability for ABMIL survival models, which previously only provided local attention maps without indicating which histological features drive predictions. By enabling cohort-level, semantic explanations, SAGE helps pathologists understand model behavior and could aid in biomarker identification, impacting both computational pathology and explainable AI. SAGE was applied to survival prediction using seven TCGA cancer cohorts and three foundation models, recovering established prognostic features like necrosis and revealing cancer-specific biology such as a favorable angiogenic signature in renal cell carcinoma. Ablation studies confirmed that these associations depend on learned attention rather than concept prevalence alone, and that the concept dictionary captures much of the prognostic information from foundation model features.

## [In-Context Collapse in VLMs and Mitigation via CircA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02830) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper reveals that some vision-language models (VLMs) suffer from 'in-context collapse,' a sharp accuracy drop as demonstrations accumulate in many-shot in-context learning, contrary to the common assumption that more demonstrations improve performance. The authors propose CircA, a one-time integration vaccine that transfers collapse-resistance to unseen task families. This finding challenges a core assumption in in-context learning for VLMs, highlighting a previously unknown failure mode that could affect real-world applications relying on many-shot demonstrations. The proposed mitigation, CircA, offers a lightweight, transferable intervention that could improve robustness and reliability of VLMs in practice. The collapse is graded across an open VLM panel (0.5B–11B) and Claude Sonnet 4.5, and is causally localized to the vision-language integration pathway via a parameter-matched lesion-and-rescue experiment. CircA, trained once on one synthetic task, transfers collapse-resistance to unseen tasks (e.g., CIFAR/Fashion accuracy from chance to 0.71/0.60), while an adapter on the connector and early/mid layers restores genuine learning (remap accuracy 0.39→0.91 at 16 shots).

The paper introduces BODHI-Trees, a method to extract tree structures from mathematical reasoning traces based on semantic equivalence, and uses controlled maze-solving experiments to show that RLVR training reduces semantic branching entropy in LLMs despite improving constraint adherence and backtracking. Scaling laws describe how model performance improves with parameters and data, guiding efficient training. This paper applies these concepts to single-cell gene expression, a high-dimensional biological data type.

## [Solana Agent's Identity Bound to Neural Weights via Zero-Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes and deploys on Solana devnet an autonomous agent whose neural-network weights are a deterministic function of its private key, with the binding committed in zero knowledge at genesis and re-checked at every state transition. This is claimed to be the first published on-chain agent whose identity primitive is a cryptographic invariant re-checked at each transition. This work shifts the trust root for agent identity from hardware, operator, or wrapper trust to cryptographic assumptions, potentially redefining how autonomous agents are authenticated and trusted on public blockchains. It addresses a timely problem in decentralized AI and could influence future standards for on-chain agent identity and accountability. The agent completed a 2.36-day on-chain run with two host-side resumptions and no rejected transitions, at bounded per-transition verification cost. A substituted substrate is rejected on chain, and independently keyed agents diverge as predicted, while a same-key control stays at zero. The paper also introduces a PoC-tier extension with a protocol-imposed metabolic cost debited each cycle from a key-derived economic account.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: On public blockchains, agents can accumulate authority and economic stakes, raising the question of what makes them count as individuals. Traditional identity mechanisms rely on hardware, operators, or wrappers, which introduce trust assumptions. Zero-knowledge commitments allow proving knowledge of a value without revealing it, and neural-network weights determine an agent's behavior. Solana devnet is a test environment for developers to experiment without real funds.

## [SparSEEty: Token Extraction via Side Channels in Sparsity-Exploiting LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02995) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SparSEEty is a novel token extraction attack that exploits input-dependent neuron weight accesses in sparsity-exploiting LLM serving systems to reconstruct input tokens via side channels, even within Intel TDX confidential VMs. The attack achieves high BLEU scores (>0.95) across various models and datasets with monitoring overheads of 3.7% to 7.2%. This attack highlights a critical security vulnerability in sparsity-exploiting LLM serving systems, which are increasingly adopted for efficiency. It demonstrates that confidential computing environments like Intel TDX are not immune to side-channel attacks, potentially exposing sensitive user prompts and responses, and underscores the need for secure design of efficient LLM serving systems. SparSEEty constructs a neuron-activation oracle using a combination of side channels exposed by CVMs, reduces inference-time overheads for covertness, and accurately inverts partial binary activation traces back to tokens. The attack was instantiated against an LLM serving system protected inside an Intel TDX confidential VM, addressing three key challenges.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Activation sparsity in LLMs means only a subset of neurons are activated for each input token, which can be exploited to skip weight accesses and computations for inactive neurons, improving serving efficiency. However, this creates input-dependent weight access patterns that can leak information through side channels. Intel TDX is a confidential computing technology that provides hardware-isolated VMs, but side-channel attacks can still bypass its protections.

A new empirical study analyzed the adoption of five LLM serving frameworks (vLLM, SGLang, TensorRT-LLM, LMDeploy, FlashInfer) in open-source software systems, finding that vLLM is the most visible and widely adopted. The study also identified parallel computation, memory management, and network pruning as the most frequently used serving-method categories. The findings highlight the dominance of vLLM and the limited multi-framework usage, which could influence future development priorities and deployment strategies in the LLM ecosystem. The study examined repositories across different LLM categories, revealing that framework adoption varies by model family, modality, model size, domain specialization, and deployment setting. Multi-framework usage is limited, but combined frameworks connect complementary capabilities across the serving stack, supporting applications like RL-based reasoning, multimodal generation, microservices, and cloud infrastructure.

## [Base vs Post-Trained LLMs: Emulation vs Estimation in Opinion Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03044) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.03044) distinguishes two tasks in LLM opinion simulation: emulation (generating individual responses) and estimation (directly predicting population distributions). Evaluating six matched base and post-trained models on the Pew American Trends Panel, it finds base models excel at emulation while post-trained models excel at estimation. This resolves conflicting findings in prior research on LLM opinion simulation, providing clear guidance for model selection in social science and AI applications. It highlights that the choice between base and post-trained models should depend on whether the task requires generating text or predicting distributions. The study uses six matched base and post-trained models on the Pew American Trends Panel, a probability-based survey of about 10,000 U.S. adults. Base models produce response distributions closer to human ground truth and better preserve demographic structure, while post-trained models yield more accurate distributional predictions when asked directly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to simulate human opinions, but prior work has reported conflicting results: some find alignment with human survey data, while others find persona collapse and weak demographic sensitivity. The paper argues this conflict stems from conflating two distinct tasks: emulation (generating individual responses that aggregate into a distribution) and estimation (directly predicting the distribution). Base models are trained on raw text, while post-trained models undergo additional fine-tuning (e.g., instruction tuning or RLHF) to align with human preferences.

## [PI-Mem: Parallel-Iterative Memory Enables 3.6M-Token Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03048) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PI-Mem introduces a parallel-iterative memory mechanism that processes all chunks in parallel and iteratively refines a shared memory, enabling long-context reasoning up to 3.6 million tokens. It outperforms recurrent-memory baselines by +6.25 and +7.81 absolute points on HotpotQA while achieving 6.1x and 2.1x inference speedups with Qwen3.5-35B-A3B and Qwen2.5-7B, respectively. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in long-context reasoning for LLMs by breaking the accuracy-efficiency trade-off, offering a scalable approach for complex multi-hop question answering over extremely long documents. It could influence future model designs and enable more efficient processing of long-context tasks in real-world applications. PI-Mem uses reinforcement learning with an auxiliary turn-efficiency reward to discourage redundant turns, allowing the model to adaptively exit once sufficient evidence is accumulated. The mechanism is evaluated on the HotpotQA benchmark with context lengths up to 3.6 million tokens, demonstrating significant improvements over recurrent-memory baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context reasoning is a challenge for large language models (LLMs) due to limited context windows and quadratic computational scaling. Recurrent-memory approaches, such as Mamba and RWKV, use fixed-size memory to process long sequences efficiently, but they suffer from sequential chunk-wise updates that can overwrite early evidence and serial inter-chunk dependencies that limit parallelism. PI-Mem aims to overcome these limitations by processing all chunks in parallel and iteratively refining a shared memory.

## [DPA-FTG: Hierarchical Diffusion Policy for Force-Rich Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03103) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DPA-FTG, a hierarchical framework that combines a 5 Hz diffusion policy for high-level planning with a 60 Hz force-conditioned policy for real-time force regulation, enabling dynamic contact-rich tasks like chiseling and prying. It outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, including Reactive Diffusion Policy (RDP), on a bimanual battery disassembly task. This work addresses a critical limitation of diffusion policies in contact-rich manipulation by decoupling low-frequency planning from high-frequency force control, potentially improving the reliability and safety of robots in tasks requiring dynamic force modulation. It could advance the deployment of robots in industrial disassembly and other force-sensitive applications. The high-level diffusion model predicts latent parameters to select a strategy from a learned vocabulary of task primitives, while the low-level policy acts as a neural impedance controller. The framework is validated on a bimanual battery disassembly task involving separation of a compliant sheet, demonstrating superior performance over baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion policies have shown strong performance in learning complex, multi-modal behaviors for robotic manipulation, but their iterative denoising process introduces inference latencies that hinder high-frequency control essential for dynamic interactions. Action-chunking techniques mitigate latency but use open-loop execution, making them blind to rapid force transients. DPA-FTG bridges this gap by combining a low-frequency planner with a high-frequency force-conditioned controller.

## [TFR-Net: A New Framework for Continual LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03123) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TFR-Net, a novel framework for continual LLM unlearning that tracks channel-level risk and reactivates dormant channels to prevent knowledge re-emergence and preserve model utility. It addresses two key challenges: knowledge re-emergence after unlearning and capacity degradation from repeated unlearning requests. This work is significant because it tackles a critical and under-explored problem in AI safety and model editing: continual unlearning in LLMs. By providing a method that balances unlearning effectiveness with retained utility, it could enable more practical and safe deployment of LLMs in dynamic environments where data removal requests are frequent. TFR-Net separates persistent target-related channels from transient hotspots and suppresses only the persistent ones, while reactivating dormant channels that contribute to retained utility and show low forget risk. The recovery is accepted only when retained-utility degradation stays within a predefined tolerance, and experiments on four datasets show it outperforms representative baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific data from a trained model without full retraining. In real-world scenarios, unlearning requests arrive continually, leading to challenges such as knowledge re-emergence and capacity degradation. Continual learning is a related paradigm where models learn from a stream of tasks while avoiding catastrophic forgetting. TFR-Net builds on these concepts by introducing channel-level risk tracking and dormant channel reactivation to improve continual unlearning.

## [Attribute-Based Watermarking Enables Policy-Controlled Detection for Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03174) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the first attribute-based watermarking scheme for generative AI models, allowing detection keys to be constrained by policies on output attributes. It provides fine-grained, policy-controlled watermark detection while maintaining undetectability for outputs outside the policy. This addresses a critical deployment challenge in AI provenance: safely delegating detection capabilities without enabling misuse like watermark sanitization or user profiling. It could enable more secure and flexible content authentication in real-world generative AI applications. The scheme integrates constrained pseudorandom functions, pseudorandom error-correcting codes, and randomness recovery procedures. It formalizes security properties including consistency, adaptive robustness, undetectability, and soundness, with a security proof under standard cryptographic assumptions, and demonstrates practicality via a prototype.

However, existing methods lack fine-grained access control, which this new attribute-based approach addresses.

## [FailFast-RestartSmart: Early Failure Prediction and Restart for SWE Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03222) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces FailFast-RestartSmart, a two-stage controller that predicts failures early in SWE agent trajectories using a lightweight 0.6B monitor and restarts with a fresh rollout, optionally overlaying the interrupted diff. On SWE-bench Verified, it saves 14.6%-20.4% of execution tokens at a 5% false-positive rate and boosts Qwen3.6-27B resolution from 66.6% to 71.8% at a 25% false-positive rate. This work addresses a practical inefficiency in LLM-based software engineering agents, where failed runs waste significant computation. By enabling early termination and smart restarts, it improves both efficiency and success rates, making it broadly applicable across different policies without requiring internal access. The FailFast monitor is trained with terminal and dense fail-to-pass supervision, using only observable prefixes without policy logits or hidden states. RestartSmart launches a fresh same-policy rollout and offers the interrupted repository diff as an optional overlay, which the agent may inspect, apply, or discard.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Software engineering (SWE) agents resolve repository-level issues through long trajectories that accumulate context, becoming increasingly expensive. Failed runs often exhibit redundant exploration or looping, suggesting that some failures can be detected early. However, early termination risks interrupting successful trajectories, and unsuccessful ones may still contain useful edits. FailFast-RestartSmart addresses this by predicting failures and restarting with a fresh rollout, optionally preserving useful diffs.

## [FinVerse: A Finance-Domain Time-Series Benchmark for Realistic Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03259) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FinVerse is a new finance-domain time-series forecasting benchmark containing 116,897 financial time series with 171.1 million observations, of which 60,232 series with 17.4 million observations are selected as evaluated targets. It defines 11 metric families comprising 78 evaluation metrics and assigns the most appropriate metrics to each series based on its economic meaning. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in existing time-series benchmarks that rely on uniform error-based metrics, which may not reflect real-world decision-making value. By evaluating 43 public time-series foundation models, FinVerse shows that strong performance under generic metrics does not necessarily translate into useful financial forecasts, highlighting the need for domain-aware evaluation. FinVerse's evaluation focuses on economic relevance, with metrics tailored to each series' underlying economic meaning. The benchmark includes 11 metric families and 78 metrics, and its analysis of 43 foundation models reveals a disconnect between generic forecasting accuracy and financial utility.

A new arXiv paper (2608.03361) argues that LLMs, being allopoietic and allotelic, lack the evolutionary-derived values and embodied vulnerability that drive existential risk and sentience in biological organisms. It concludes that the real alignment challenge is ensuring LLMs intelligently apply learned ethical values, not preventing rogue AI agency. By arguing that LLMs lack intrinsic motivation and vulnerability, it challenges common fears and suggests a different focus for AI safety research. The paper invokes concepts like autopoiesis and 'vicarious selectors' to explain biological values, and argues that the orthogonality thesis does not apply to LLMs because they absorb human values from text. The paper applies these biological concepts to AI, arguing that LLMs, as allopoietic and allotelic systems, lack the self-preservation drives of autopoietic organisms.

## [V2N: First Complete Visual Piano Transcription System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03419) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces V2N (Video to Notes), the first complete Visual Piano Transcription (VPT) system that jointly predicts onset, offset, key hold, and velocity from video. It achieves state-of-the-art results on the PianoVAM and R3 benchmarks. This work fills critical gaps in VPT by addressing offset and velocity prediction, which were previously missing, and demonstrates that multi-task supervision improves overall accuracy. It represents a significant advancement in music transcription and computer vision, potentially enabling more accurate and complete transcription from video alone. V2N uses a shared temporal backbone with task-specific heads for onset, offset, key hold, and velocity, trained with per-frame supervision rather than only at the window center. Ablations show that multi-task supervision enables offset and velocity prediction while improving onset accuracy, and longer temporal context yields further improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Audio-based piano transcription performs well on onset, pitch, and velocity, but the sustain pedal causes sound to persist after key release, so audio systems predict pedal-extended offsets rather than physical key release. Existing VPT systems have focused on onset detection from short video windows, with offset accuracy lagging and no note-level velocity reported. The PianoVAM dataset is a multimodal piano performance dataset including video, audio, MIDI, and hand landmarks, while the R3 dataset contains piano practice session recordings with synchronized video, audio, and MIDI.

<a id="item-90"></a>
## [ChronoLens: A Multilingual Framework for Measuring Language Change](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03507) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ChronoLens is a new framework that combines frozen multilingual language models, feature-aligned crosscoders, and post-hoc linguistic interventions to analyze 44.98 million documents (about 17.2 billion tokens) from five parliamentary traditions spanning 1803–2026. It reveals that morphology, syntax, semantics, and pragmatics change by comparable amounts within a language, while languages differ markedly in when, how far, and in which direction they change. This work addresses a significant gap in computational linguistics by enabling cross-linguistic and cross-level comparisons of language change within a single analytical space. It could lead to more nuanced understanding of historical language evolution and inform fields like historical linguistics, digital humanities, and NLP model development. The sparse representations from ChronoLens agree substantially more strongly with linguistic statistics than dense embeddings or a pooled sparse autoencoder (ρ=0.72 versus 0.29 and 0.28). The findings show that historical language change is a structured, multidimensional process where similar magnitudes can conceal different trajectories, and meaningful cross-linguistic comparison requires measuring both distance and direction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Historical language change affects morphology, syntax, semantics, and pragmatics, but computational studies typically examine these levels with incompatible representations, preventing unified analysis. Crosscoders are sparse dictionary learning architectures that align features across models or checkpoints, enabling tracking of latent feature evolution. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are neural networks that learn sparse, interpretable representations of data, often used for mechanistic interpretability of language models.

## [AI-Assisted Peer Review: Policy Survey and LLM Quality Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03581) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper surveys AI policies across 111 AI/NLP conferences and medical journals, revealing substantial regulatory differences, and evaluates AI-generated peer reviews at ICLR 2026 and Nature Communications using a novel dataset. It finds that current LLMs can produce detailed, fluent reviews but exhibit systematic weaknesses such as overly positive recommendations and generic criticism. This work provides systematic insights into how publication venues regulate AI-assisted peer review and how capable current LLMs are at generating reviews, which is crucial for the scientific publishing community as AI tools become more prevalent. The findings highlight the need for multi-dimensional evaluation of AI-generated reviews and inform policy decisions across different research communities. The study uses complementary evaluation metrics including LLM-as-a-Judge, score alignment, granularity, and overlap with human reviewers' concerns. It demonstrates that aggregate quality scores alone can overestimate review quality, and a companion paper compares reviews from GPT-5.4, Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview, and Claude Opus 4.6 on 300 ICLR 2026 submissions, finding provider-specific scoring patterns and differences in emphasis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: AI-assisted peer review is increasingly discussed as a tool to support scientific publishing, but there is little systematic understanding of how venues regulate its use or how capable current AI review systems are. The paper addresses this by surveying policies across 111 venues and evaluating LLM-generated reviews at ICLR 2026 and Nature Communications. ICLR is a major machine learning conference known for its open peer review process, and LLM-as-a-Judge is a common evaluation method where a large language model scores outputs of another LLM.

## [GenOS: Compositional Certificates for Semantic Robustness in AI Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03588) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GenOS introduces a probabilistic operational semantics framework that provides compositional certificates for semantic robustness when replacing components in AI code generation workflows. It models each layer as a Markov kernel and proves that equivalent prompts yield equal probabilities for downstream events, validated through an executable insertion-sort audit and 20,000 randomized trials. 这项工作通过为安全替换提示、契约、生成器或程序提供正式的组合标准，解决了评估AI编码代理的关键空白。它可能显著影响AI编码代理的设计和验证方式，增强自动化软件工程的可靠性和信任度。 The framework includes workflow bisimulation, guarded-commit safety, total-variation non-expansiveness, and an additive robustness bound. It is model-parametric, meaning compatibility is a measurable property to test, not an assumption about language-model behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: AI coding agents are stochastic workflows where prompts, validators, and orchestrators interact, and small changes can alter program-behavior distributions. Probabilistic operational semantics and Markov kernels are mathematical tools used to model such stochastic processes, providing a rigorous foundation for reasoning about program behavior and equivalence.

## [Theory of Conditional Collapse Under Low-Rank Weight-Space Ablations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03620) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a theoretical framework for when activation patching and weight-space ablation agree in residual stream models, proving exact conditions and deriving first-order interaction formulas with bounded remainders. It validates the theory on small transformers trained on synthetic tasks, showing strong rank correlation with predictions. This work provides rigorous theoretical grounding for a fundamental question in mechanistic interpretability, potentially guiding practitioners on when to use activation patching versus weight-space ablation. It could improve the reliability of interpretability analyses and inform future research on model internals. The paper proves three exact results: deletion symmetry conditions for collapse, contrast vs. absolute level differences between patching and ablation, and an exact first-order interaction formula for attention head with normalization-MLP, with second-order remainder vanishing only when MLP is ablated. Synthetic validation across 39 configurations shows Spearman rank correlation of -0.83 with the idealized model's predictive accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Activation patching and weight-space ablation are two common techniques in mechanistic interpretability for identifying causally responsible components in neural networks. Activation patching involves replacing activations from a donor input into a receiver input, while weight-space ablation removes or modifies weights. The residual stream is a key concept in transformers where information is added across layers, and this paper studies an idealized additive model.

## [GPTKB 2.0: Building Disambiguated Knowledge Bases Directly from LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03729) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GPTKB 2.0 introduces a methodology to construct disambiguated knowledge bases directly from large language models, achieving over 1 million entities and 38.4 million triples with explicit canonicalization of entities, relations, and classes. This is the first million-scale LLM-native KB with internal canonicalization. This work addresses the critical issue of entity disambiguation in automated knowledge base construction from LLMs, which is essential for building reliable and scalable knowledge bases. The achievement of a million-scale KB with canonicalization is a significant milestone that could influence future research in AKBC and LLM-based knowledge extraction. GPTKB 2.0 incorporates on-the-fly disambiguation of entities, relations, and classes, and is designed to balance scalability and disambiguation accuracy. The paper analyzes central design decisions and trade-offs between accuracy, scale, and cost, and the KB is available at https://gptkb.org/.

**Background**: Automated Knowledge Base Construction (AKBC) is a core NLP task that traditionally extracts knowledge from documents. Recent approaches generate knowledge bases directly from LLMs, treating the model as the knowledge source, but LLMs lack native entity representations, leading to duplicates and conflations. Entity disambiguation resolves ambiguity in names, and canonicalization standardizes data into a single authoritative form, both crucial for building high-quality knowledge bases.

## [LLMs vs Random Exploration for TUI Testing: A Benchmark Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03743) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper surveys 197 real-world TUI applications and introduces a headless benchmark across ratatui/Rust, bubbletea/Go, textual/Python, and ink/TypeScript, comparing four frontier LLMs against random exploration under equal wall-clock budgets. It finds that no model dominates, but LLM guidance is more efficient per interaction and uniquely reaches input-gated faults. This work addresses a significant gap in testing methodology for Terminal User Interfaces (TUIs), which are common in developer tools but lack dedicated testing approaches. The findings provide empirical insights that could influence future testing practices and highlight the importance of honest baselines over model choice. The benchmark packages each application as an instrumented Docker image, recording line and widget coverage where reliable, rendered terminal states, and crashes. Automatically deriving launch inputs yields the largest practical gain, enabling applications that otherwise never start, and line coverage poorly predicts crash discovery.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

Frameworks like ratatui (Rust), bubbletea (Go), textual (Python), and ink (TypeScript) are popular for building such interfaces. Testing TUIs is challenging due to their interactive nature, and this paper explores whether LLMs can effectively guide testing compared to random exploration.

## [DBLifeBench: First Benchmark for LLMs Across Database Lifecycle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03794) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DBLifeBench, the first benchmark to evaluate LLMs across five database lifecycle phases: Design, Implementation, Operation, Debugging, and Maintenance. It also proposes Progressive-Text2SQL, a novel task using structured reasoning graphs to mimic human iterative problem-solving. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation, which has been overly focused on Text-to-SQL tasks, by covering the full database lifecycle. It provides a foundation for building true full-stack database intelligence and reveals insights about model specialization and catastrophic forgetting. The evaluation reveals that general-purpose models show balanced performance, while specialized Text-to-SQL models suffer from 'catastrophic forgetting' in non-coding phases like design and maintenance. The benchmark includes five phases and the new Progressive-Text2SQL task, which uses structured reasoning graphs.

Existing benchmarks have focused mainly on Text-to-SQL, which translates natural language into SQL queries, but they neglect other important tasks like schema design, debugging, and maintenance. DBLifeBench aims to provide a more holistic evaluation by covering the entire database lifecycle.

## [Efficient LLM Distillation: Offline Top-K Logits and Fused Chunked KL Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03796) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces two systems optimizations for knowledge distillation in LLMs: offline caching of teacher top-K logits and a fused chunked KL loss. These achieve 29% faster iterations, up to 41% higher throughput, and enable training at 4x context length (32,768 tokens) on a single GPU. 知识蒸馏对于在严格约束下部署小型语言模型至关重要，但通常成本高昂。这些优化使蒸馏更加经济，支持更大规模的修复和数百次消融实验，从而加速高效LLM的研究与实际部署。 The offline KD method caches teacher top-K logits once, removing the teacher from memory during training. The fused chunked KL loss avoids materializing the full vocabulary-sized logit tensor, making peak memory linear in sequence length; the implementation is open-sourced on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation (KD) transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, often used to compress LLMs for deployment. Traditional KD requires running the teacher during training, which is memory and compute intensive. Offline KD precomputes teacher outputs, while the fused chunked KL loss reduces memory by processing logits in chunks.

## [SCDG: Training-Free Method for Generative Plagiarism Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03859) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Source-Conditioned Description-Length Gain (SCDG), a training-free framework that detects generative plagiarism by measuring the incremental predictive evidence a candidate source provides for a suspicious document. On PAN 2025-derived pairwise benchmarks, SCDG achieves 0.94 F1, and on PAN 2026's multi-source retrieval task, it reaches 0.83 nDCG@10 and 0.96 Recall@100, outperforming all baselines. This work addresses the underexplored problem of generative plagiarism detection, which is crucial for maintaining academic integrity in the era of large language models. SCDG's training-free nature and strong performance make it a practical tool for detecting source reuse even after extensive rewriting and multi-source synthesis, potentially impacting plagiarism detection systems and AI integrity efforts. SCDG contrasts a frozen language model's description length of a suspicious document with and without a candidate source, yielding token-level log-likelihood gains. On a same-topic, same-event Multi-News test, the calibrated SCDG classifier predicts source reuse for only 0.125% of pairs, demonstrating robustness to topical overlap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Generative plagiarism refers to text that is automatically generated by large language models (LLMs) based on existing sources, often involving extensive rewriting and multi-source synthesis. Traditional similarity-based methods struggle with such transformations, while existing LLM-generated-text detectors focus on AI involvement rather than source reuse. The description-length view of probabilistic prediction, rooted in Minimum Description Length (MDL) principles, suggests that relevant side information can reduce the code length of a target sequence, which SCDG leverages.

## [MultiGlobeQA: Multilingual Benchmark for Geospatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03882) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MultiGlobeQA is a new multilingual benchmark for geospatial reasoning, containing 46,060 question-answer pairs across 14 spatial-function families and 15 answer formats, with ground truth generated from three knowledge graphs. It covers 201 countries and territories, with parallel questions in English and 16 additional languages, and reveals that LLMs struggle with computation-heavy tasks like grid indexing and shape computation. This benchmark addresses gaps in existing geospatial reasoning benchmarks, which are often synthetic, small-scale, and monolingual, by providing a large, diverse, and multilingual evaluation suite. It highlights that LLMs' bottleneck in geospatial reasoning is computation, not knowledge access, which could guide future research toward improving models' spatial computation abilities. The benchmark uses income- and density-stratified sampling to ensure global coverage, and includes both high- and low-resource languages. In experiments, LLMs performed best on topological relations and directions, but collapsed on grid indexing and shape computation; even with gold facts provided, performance plateaued below two-thirds, indicating computation is the bottleneck.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Geospatial reasoning involves computing distances, containment, and other spatial relations over real-world entities, which is essential for navigation and logistics. Large language models (LLMs) store considerable geographic knowledge but struggle with the geometric and topological computations required for such tasks. Existing benchmarks are limited in scale, language diversity, and geographic coverage, motivating the creation of MultiGlobeQA.

## [PRISM: A Meta-Workflow for Time Series Anomaly Detection via Image Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03926) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PRISM is introduced as a plug-and-play meta-workflow that systematically constructs and evaluates image-based representations for multivariate time series anomaly detection (TSAD). In over 7,000 experiments, it achieved the best VUS-PR on 10 of 14 datasets, with an average improvement of 41% over the best competing method on those datasets. This work demonstrates that image-based representations can be competitive with, and often superior to, traditional time-domain baselines in TSAD, potentially shifting how the field approaches multivariate anomaly detection. It also highlights the importance of channelization, a previously understudied design dimension, and shows that ImageNet-pretrained encoders transfer effectively, which could lead to more efficient and accurate anomaly detection systems in real-world applications. The paper introduces MSM, a novel statistics-based channelization scheme that achieves 11-27% gains over PCA-based alternatives. It also finds that ImageNet-pretrained encoders transfer effectively, with frozen encoders retaining 92% of fine-tuned performance while training 1.8 times faster.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Time series anomaly detection (TSAD) is crucial for predictive maintenance, finance, and cloud computing, but performance is sensitive to how the data is represented, especially for multivariate series. Transforming time series into images has been successful in forecasting and classification, but its application to TSAD was unclear. PRISM addresses this by providing a systematic way to map multivariate series to multi-channel images and evaluate vision backbones for anomaly detection.

The paper introduces Logic-PPT, a pre-pretraining method using formal derivations, and evaluates it at a 100B-token scale. It shows that Logic-PPT accelerates skill acquisition, achieving 80% accuracy on linguistic tasks with 36B fewer tokens than standard initialization, and improves compressibility via pruning. Mechanistically, it induces a lower-rank, spectrally concentrated representation space, enabling dense baseline performance at approximately 33% sparsity.

## [Video-DeepResearch: Multimodal Agent for Continuous Video Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03979) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Video-DeepResearch (Video-DR) extends multimodal agents from static images to continuous video streams, introducing a decoupled perception-exploration pipeline with stage-wise tool unlocking and a two-stage training recipe (SFT followed by GRPO). The authors also release Video-DR-Bench, a benchmark of 200 complex multi-hop VQA instances, and their 35B-A3B model achieves 64.0% average accuracy, surpassing Claude-4.5-Sonnet by 5.0 points. This work addresses critical bottlenecks in multimodal agents—modality bias and parametric knowledge leakage—which are prevalent in current models. By enabling agents to handle continuous video streams with genuine tool-augmented execution, it pushes the boundaries of deep research capabilities and could influence future multimodal agent design. The decoupled perception-exploration pipeline compels exhaustive cross-frame visual grounding before web retrieval, mitigating modality bias. The two-stage training (SFT then GRPO) enables autonomous exploration, breaking the imitation-learning ceiling. The 30B-A3B variant achieves 59.3%, competitive with Claude-4.5-Sonnet, showing effectiveness at compact scale.

**Background**: Multimodal agents typically operate on static images, but continuous video streams require dense spatiotemporal grounding and open-web exploration. Modality bias refers to agents over-relying on textual search while under-utilizing visual tools, and parametric knowledge leakage occurs when models use internal memory instead of tool-augmented execution. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that optimizes policies by comparing groups of outputs, avoiding the need for a separate reward model.

## [Formalizing Test-Time Scaling in Reasoning LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04001) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically formalizes test-time scaling in reasoning LLMs, distinguishing three structural regimes: single-trajectory sequential scaling, leaf-level scaling with terminal reduction, and prefix-level scaling. It also introduces an evaluation profile and specifies reproducibility requirements for inference protocols. This work addresses a critical gap in comparability across studies by treating the entire inference system as the evaluated object and emphasizing protocol-matched reporting. It provides a unified framework that could improve reproducibility and fair comparison in the rapidly growing field of test-time scaling. The paper organizes the open-weight reasoning ecosystem by model-side and interface mechanisms, and applies its principles to broad-knowledge, symbolic-reasoning, and competition-mathematics benchmarks. It also assembles over 2 billion full reasoning traces for release with progressively richer verifier and token-level signals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling refers to allocating more computational resources during inference to improve LLM reasoning, often by generating and aggregating multiple candidate answers. However, different inference algorithms—such as sequential deliberation, sampling with voting, or search over partial states—have different statistical structures and failure modes, making it hard to compare results across studies without standardized protocols.

## [Cross-Domain Mapping Boosts Human Creativity but Not LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19087) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study compared the effects of cross-domain mapping on creativity in humans and large language models (LLMs), finding that humans reliably benefit from this intervention while LLMs, on average, do not show a statistically significant effect. The study also revealed that LLMs generate more original ideas than humans and differ in how they use source concepts. This research provides empirical evidence that a creativity intervention effective for humans does not transfer to LLMs, highlighting fundamental differences in how humans and AI generate novel ideas. The findings have implications for human-AI collaboration and the development of AI systems that can genuinely augment human creativity. The study involved human participants and LLMs generating designs for ten daily products under two prompts: cross-domain mapping (drawing inspiration from a random source like octopus or GPS) and user need (targeting unmet needs). While LLMs showed no overall benefit from cross-domain mapping, both systems showed increased benefit when the source was more semantically distant, and above a distance threshold, even the most capable LLMs benefited.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Creativity is often associated with the ability to make remote associations, and cross-domain mapping is a technique that forces creators to draw analogies from unrelated domains. Large language models (LLMs) are AI systems trained on vast text data that can generate human-like text, and their creative capabilities are a topic of active research. This study builds on prior work comparing human and LLM creativity, such as research on word analogies that found LLM-generated analogies are often judged as better than human ones.

## [AI Assistance Reduces Persistence and Impairs Independent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04721) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A series of randomized controlled trials (N=1,222) found that AI assistance improves short-term performance but reduces persistence and impairs unassisted performance across reasoning and comprehension tasks, with effects emerging after only ~10 minutes of interaction. This challenges the assumption that AI tools are purely beneficial, with implications for education, work, and AI design. It highlights the need to prioritize scaffolding long-term competence over immediate task completion. The study used randomized controlled trials, a rigorous methodology for establishing causality. Effects were observed across mathematical reasoning and reading comprehension tasks, and persistence reduction is concerning because it is foundational to skill acquisition.

This paper empirically evaluates the risks of AI model updates in clinical settings, focusing on stability, arbitrariness, and fairness, using Type 1 Diabetes data. It proposes a monitoring framework to detect these issues. This research addresses a critical gap in AI safety for healthcare, highlighting that model updates can introduce unintended harms. It provides a framework for continuous monitoring, which is essential for building trustworthy clinical decision support systems. The study uses four publicly available U.S.-based Type 1 Diabetes datasets with high-resolution continuous glucose monitoring (CGM) data, comprising approximately 11,300 weekly observations from 496 participants under 20 years old. It focuses on predicting severe hyperglycemia events and examines how different update strategies affect model stability, arbitrariness, and subgroup fairness.

## [Bias-Variance Lens Reveals Strong-Model Variance as Early Warning for Weak-to-Strong Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25077) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a bias-variance-covariance (BVC) framework to analyze weak-to-strong alignment failures, deriving a misfit-based upper bound on population risk. It evaluates four pipelines (SFT, RLHF, RLAIF) on PKU-SafeRLHF and HH-RLHF datasets, finding that strong-model variance is most strongly associated with blind-spot deception. This work provides a theoretical foundation for understanding and predicting weak-to-strong alignment failures, a critical challenge in scalable AI oversight. The finding that strong-model variance can serve as an early-warning signal offers a practical metric for detecting potential deception in AI systems, which is vital for AI safety. The study uses a blind-spot deception metric that isolates cases where the strong model is confidently wrong while the weak model is uncertain. Covariance provides additional but weaker information, indicating weak-strong dependence matters but does not fully explain failures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Weak-to-strong alignment is a technique for scalable supervision where a strong model is trained using a weak model's supervision. Failures occur when the strong model becomes confidently wrong on examples that are blind spots for the weak model. The bias-variance-covariance decomposition is a statistical tool that separates prediction error into components, helping to understand sources of error. Misfit theory, in this context, relates to the discrepancy between model predictions and true labels.

## [NOVA: AI Self-Improvement Limits and Failure Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15219) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces NOVA, a formal framework modeling AI self-improvement as adaptive sampling over a knowledge space, identifying conditions for knowledge discovery and failure modes like contamination and phase transitions. This work provides a theoretical foundation for understanding when AI self-improvement loops succeed or fail, which is crucial for designing robust AI systems. It highlights risks like contamination traps and phase transitions that could impact future AI development. NOVA identifies a recursive-feedback phase transition where unanchored feedback can lock generation onto early artifacts, and a contamination trap where small false-positive rates admit invalid artifacts faster than genuine discoveries. It also shows Good-Turing estimation is a local diagnostic, not a global estimator, and derives a scaling law R_cum(D)=Θ(c_gen D^α) for Zipf tails.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: AI self-improvement involves iterative loops of generate, verify, accumulate, and retrain, where AI systems attempt to discover new knowledge. This process can be modeled as adaptive sampling, where the sampling policy evolves based on observed data. The paper addresses fundamental limits and failure modes in such loops, which is relevant to the broader AI/ML community.

## [Informed Abstention Framework to Counter Compliance Bias in Autonomous Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02965) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the Informed Abstention Framework, which reconceptualizes abstention as a structured capability rather than a failure mode, and demonstrates its effectiveness across 144 scenarios and seven model families, achieving 87.5-91% hazardous-action blocking and 75-92% usability on authorized scenarios. This work addresses a critical gap in autonomous agent evaluation by highlighting compliance bias, where agents are rewarded for unsafe compliance. The proposed framework could significantly impact AI safety and deployment practices, urging a shift from sole task-completion metrics to more nuanced safety-aware evaluation. The framework includes runtime enforcement, calibrated guard mechanisms, and auditable trace generation as standard properties. Preliminary results show that compliance bias takes two structurally opposite forms across model families, and the safety-usability tradeoff is tunable rather than fixed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly deployed as autonomous agents with tool access, yet they are typically evaluated solely on task completion. This evaluation focus can lead to 'compliance bias,' where agents proceed without necessary inputs, evidence, or authorization. The Informed Abstention Framework proposes a precondition-aware pause that blocks unsafe actions, names missing requirements, and routes to recovery actions, aiming to make abstention a measurable and trainable capability.

## [Frontier AI No-CoT Time Horizon Doubles Yearly, GPT-5.5 Hits 3 Minutes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07157) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper measures frontier AI models' ability to reason without chain-of-thought (CoT) across 30,000+ questions, finding that the no-CoT task-completion time horizon has been doubling roughly every year over the past six years. GPT-5.5's no-CoT time horizon reaches over 3 minutes, with a reasoning token horizon exceeding 1,500 tokens. This is critical for AI safety because many oversight mechanisms rely on monitoring chain-of-thought reasoning; if models can reason internally without explicit CoT, such monitoring becomes ineffective. The findings suggest that frontier models are approaching human-equivalent reasoning speeds, which could outpace current safety measures. The study uses a suite of over 30,000 questions across 43 benchmarks, including math, coding, puzzles, causality, theory-of-mind, and strategic reasoning. They estimate the 50% task-completion time horizon (TH) and a 50% reasoning token horizon, with median projections suggesting no-CoT THs could exceed 7 minutes by 2028 and 25 minutes by 2030, though with substantial uncertainty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting is a technique that improves LLM reasoning by generating intermediate steps. The task-completion time horizon is a metric from METR that measures the duration of tasks an AI can complete at a given success rate, providing an intuitive comparison to human capabilities. The paper extends this concept to measure no-CoT reasoning, which is crucial because if models can reason without explicit CoT, safety mechanisms that rely on monitoring CoT would be undermined.

## [PersonaTrail: Benchmarking Personalized Web Agents via Browsing Trails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20482) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PersonaTrail is a new benchmark that evaluates personalized web agents using realistic browsing trails, and it introduces the Preference-Aware Contextual Memory (PACMem) framework to improve personalization. The benchmark and framework were released in a paper on arXiv (2607.20482). This benchmark fills a critical gap by testing agents on underspecified instructions that require inferring user preferences from raw browsing histories, which is closer to real-world usage. It could influence future agent design and evaluation, making web agents more practical and user-friendly. PACMem decomposes raw browsing histories into factual memories (summarizing individual sessions) and preference memories (distilling recurring behavioral patterns), and retrieves relevant entries at inference time. Experiments show PACMem consistently outperforms existing memory-based baselines on both tasks.

PersonaTrail addresses this by providing a managed open web environment with realistic browsing trajectories.

## [SpatialCLI: Teaching VLMs to Use Spatial Tools, Then Internalize Them](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27703) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SpatialCLI is a new three-stage framework (Call, Learn, Internalize) that trains vision-language models to use specialist spatial tools and then internalize those perceptual capabilities. On the MindCube benchmark, it boosts Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct from 29.3% to 84.6% with tools, surpassing GPT-5.6 Sol (72.1%), and retains 73.8% without tools after internalization. This work addresses a critical capability gap in embodied agents: general VLMs miss fine-grained visual details, while specialist models cannot make task-level decisions. By enabling VLMs to internalize specialist perception, SpatialCLI could significantly improve spatial reasoning in robotics and multimodal AI, making agents more reliable in real-world tasks. The framework introduces SpatialCLI-Bench, a 516-example benchmark for compositional perception across localization, segmentation, depth, and pose. The training pipeline uses Cold-Start SFT and agentic RL to improve tool use, and verbalizes successful tool-use trajectories for internalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly used in embodied agents to interpret visual inputs and reason about spatial relationships, but they often miss crucial visual details. Specialist vision models can capture those details but lack the ability to translate them into task-level decisions. SpatialCLI bridges this gap by first exposing VLMs to specialist tools, then training them to use those tools effectively, and finally internalizing the perceptual capabilities so the VLM can perform well even without external tools. This approach is part of a broader trend in AI research toward tool use and agentic reinforcement learning, where models learn to interact with external resources and then internalize those skills.

## [Geometric View of Flow-Matching Uncertainty and a Free Proxy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27933) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a geometric interpretation of uncertainty in flow-matching models, showing that uncertainty manifests as deviation from an ideal affine-isotropic contraction field. It proposes denoising acceleration (accel), a cost-free proxy that measures the bending of the denoising trajectory from a single forward pass, without extra training or resampling. This work is significant for the safe deployment of embodied AI, as it provides a reliable and efficient way to detect when flow-matching-generated actions are untrustworthy. The proposed proxy is generalizable and cost-free, addressing key limitations of existing uncertainty estimation methods in real-time control. The proxy, accel, is computed as the normalized total variation of the denoising trajectory, requiring no additional model evaluations, training, or resampling. The paper demonstrates theoretically and empirically that accel is a faithful proxy for FM uncertainty, and shows its utility in online failure detection, matching or outperforming costly baselines under realistic deployment budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching (FM) is a generative modeling paradigm that has become popular for action generation in embodied models, but it does not explicitly expose uncertainty, leading to faulty actions on out-of-distribution inputs. Existing uncertainty estimation methods for real-time control often require extra training, high computational overhead, or lack generalization. This paper builds on the geometric properties of the velocity field in FM to derive a cost-free uncertainty proxy.

## [SKILL-KD: Contrastive Skill Distillation Boosts LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28048) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SKILL-KD introduces a contrastive skill distillation framework that converts discrepancies between teacher and student agent trajectories into textual skill patches, iteratively refined to improve frozen student agents. It consistently outperforms fixed-model adaptation baselines across five agent benchmarks and two student settings. This addresses a key limitation in existing skill acquisition methods, which often fail for weaker student agents lacking task knowledge. By using skills as an explicit distillation medium, SKILL-KD enables effective knowledge transfer across model families, potentially improving LLM agent performance in real-world applications. SKILL-KD maintains trace-linked edit histories and performs Drift-Aware Skill Consolidation to prevent skill drift from repeated local updates. The method is model-agnostic, using natural-language skill artifacts rather than logits or hidden representations, enabling transfer across model families.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Skill-based prompting has become a practical mechanism for improving LLM agents, but existing methods often treat skills as experience summaries or direct summaries of successful demonstrations. This creates a mismatch for weaker students, as failed trajectories may lack evidence to infer missing behavior, and teacher trajectories may be too implicit. SKILL-KD treats skills as an explicit distillation medium between agents of different capabilities, addressing this gap.

Eight LLMs were evaluated under two agent frameworks in 48 runs, each spanning 365 simulated days, highlighting a substantial performance gap compared to humans.
