## [AdaMM: Analytic Memory Framework for Multimodal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29440) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces AdaMM, a framework that adds analytic memory to multimodal agents, enabling queryable structures for filtering, aggregation, ranking, and temporal comparison over multimodal observations. It jointly supports retrieval and analytic memory, improving performance on two benchmarks by up to 11.3% and 6.9%. This addresses a critical gap in long-term multimodal memory, which traditionally focuses only on retrieval. By enabling analytic operations, AdaMM allows agents to compute over accumulated observations, potentially improving decision-making in complex, long-horizon tasks. AdaMM extracts provenance-linked attribute-value observations from dialogue, images, and contextual metadata, discovering recurring field structures without application-defined schemas. At inference, a memory-aware planner decomposes queries into retrieval and analytic operations, routing each to appropriate tools.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term multimodal memory systems typically organize interaction histories through summaries and indexes to return query-relevant information, known as retrieval memory. Analytic memory is a complementary abstraction that organizes recurring observations into queryable structures for analytical operations. AdaMM builds on this concept, providing a framework that integrates both types of memory for multimodal agents.

## [Validity Loss in Agentic AI Evaluation: A Compounding Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00794) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a three-layer compounding validity model (V_total ≤ V_1 × V_2 × V_3) showing that agentic AI evaluation pipelines suffer multiplicative validity loss. A survey of 55 papers found that 82% misuse inter-rater reliability metrics, indicating systematic V_3 collapse. This framework provides a formal way to diagnose and correct validity degradation before deployment, which is critical for AI safety and regulatory compliance. It could influence future evaluation standards and improve the reliability of benchmark scores used for deployment decisions. The model estimates that a pipeline retaining 70% validity at each stage is at most 34% valid overall (range 0.22–0.54). It also identifies task validity flaws in 7 of 10 popular benchmarks and inter-simulator variance up to 9 percentage points, with demographic disparities for non-Standard American English speakers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI evaluation pipelines involve task generation, human-simulator calibration, and automated judgment. Validity refers to whether a test measures what it intends to, while inter-rater reliability (IRR) measures consistency among raters. The paper applies psychometric principles to AI evaluation, proposing thresholds like ICC ≥ 0.70 and alpha ≥ 0.67/0.70/0.80 by consequence level.

## [Deferred Trajectory Exposure for Verifiable AD Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01755) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces DEFT-RLVR, a method that defers exposure of future trajectories in autonomous driving VLMs to avoid trajectory anchoring bias, and proposes AD-MCQ, a multiple-choice formulation for verifiable planning decisions. This addresses a critical flaw in current CoT supervision for AD VLMs, improving reasoning faithfulness and reducing hallucinations, which is essential for safe and reliable autonomous driving systems. AD-MCQ casts planning as selection among explicit trajectory candidates, avoiding open-ended trajectory synthesis. DEFT-RLVR transforms future trajectories from pre-decision anchors into post-decision verification targets, improving AD reasoning while preserving general visual capabilities.

## [Mixed-Initiative Human-Robot Teaming with Online Bayesian Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16178) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a computational framework that uses online Bayesian inference to estimate a human's willingness to comply with robot assistance in sequential decision-making games, enabling robots to adapt to suboptimal human behavior. User studies demonstrate significant improvements in team performance and user trust. This work addresses a critical gap in human-robot teaming by relaxing the unrealistic assumption of near-optimal behavior, making the framework applicable to real-world scenarios where both humans and robots are suboptimal. It provides a validated approach to enhance collaboration and trust, which is essential for the deployment of autonomous agents in dynamic environments. The framework models asymmetric capabilities and incomplete environmental knowledge, and uses online Bayesian inference to update beliefs about human compliance. User studies show statistically significant improvements in objective team performance (p<.001) and subjective measures such as trust and willingness to comply (p<.001).

This work builds on Bayesian methods to infer human behavioral patterns and adapt robot actions accordingly.

## [AlphaZero Internalizes Tactical Priors from Search, Study Shows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14636) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces Cross-Phase Prior Intervention (CPI) to causally separate the online effect of a tactical prior from the learned residual in AlphaZero's policy network. Experiments in 9x9 Gomoku and 19x19 Go show that deleting the prior during evaluation still leaves a large residual response, rising from 13.8% to 26.3% in Gomoku and from 0.6% to 33.8% in Go. This work addresses a fundamental question in reinforcement learning and self-play systems: whether the network truly internalizes behaviors from search or merely relies on search at test time. The findings suggest that search acts as a training-time behavioral curriculum, with lessons that are real but partial and geometry-bound, which could influence future research on search and policy learning. The study uses a sealed one-shot final test and finds that soft reweighting teaches as well as hard action restriction, so pruning legal actions is not the mechanism. Re-enabling the prior restores nearly 100% response, leaving dependence gaps of 73.7 and 65.8 points, and a latched-position evaluation localizes the residual to trained geometry, worth +17.3 points on in-distribution defenses but only +1.3 on structurally novel ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: AlphaZero is a reinforcement learning algorithm that combines a policy-value neural network with Monte Carlo tree search (MCTS) for game playing. In self-play, the network is trained using data generated by MCTS, which uses a prior to guide search. This paper investigates whether the network learns to internalize the tactical priors used during search, or whether it simply relies on search at test time.

## [IPPRO: Scale-Invariant Structural Pruning via Projective Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14171) ⭐️ 8.0/10

IPPRO introduces a scale-invariant structured pruning framework that embeds filters into real projective space and defines PROscore, a directional collapse score measuring angular displacement toward zero under a single gradient step. It proves this one-shot criterion reliably predicts multi-step pruning dynamics and demonstrates consistent improvements across CNNs, Vision Transformers, and LLMs. This work addresses a fundamental flaw in importance-based pruning—scale invariance—which causes functionally identical filters to receive arbitrarily different importance scores. By providing a theoretically grounded, architecture-agnostic solution, IPPRO could significantly improve model compression across a wide range of deep learning applications, especially under high compression and no-fine-tuning regimes. IPPRO embeds filters into real projective space (RP^N) to resolve the singularity at the origin, placing all filters at equal angular distance from the zero filter. The method connects PROscore to exact L0 relaxation, and experiments cover ResNet, DeiT, and LLaMA, showing particularly striking gains under high compression and no-fine-tuning conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Structured pruning removes entire filters or neurons to compress neural networks, and importance-based methods typically rely on filter magnitude as a proxy for importance. However, due to scale invariance, rescaling filters can arbitrarily change their magnitude without altering the function they compute, making magnitude-based importance unreliable. Projective geometry treats points that differ by a scale factor as equivalent, which naturally addresses this issue by focusing on angular relationships rather than magnitudes.

## [ICL Mechanism Explained via Information Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21012) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that zero-shot language models encode queries into non-selective representations, and few-shot in-context learning (ICL) simulates task-oriented information removal via low-rank filtering. It identifies specific attention heads, termed Denoising Heads, that perform this removal, and ablation experiments show that blocking them significantly degrades ICL accuracy. This work provides a novel mechanistic explanation of ICL, which is a core capability of modern LLMs but remains poorly understood. The findings could inform future interpretability research and model steering techniques, potentially leading to more controllable and reliable language models. The paper demonstrates that zero-shot LMs produce near-zero accuracy due to non-selective representations, while a low-rank filter can steer LMs toward the intended task. The identified Denoising Heads are crucial for ICL, especially when the correct label is absent from demonstrations, as ablating them causes significant accuracy degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: In-context learning (ICL) is a few-shot learning paradigm where large language models adapt to new tasks without updating their weights, using only demonstrations provided in the prompt. Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the internal computations of neural networks to understand how they work. Low-rank filtering is a technique that approximates high-dimensional matrices with lower-rank ones, often used in model adaptation and compression.

MIMIC-MJX is a new framework that learns biomechanically grounded neural control policies from kinematic data, enabling physics-based simulation of animal behavior. It is demonstrated to be accurate, fast, and generalizable across diverse animal body models, requiring only modest amounts of motion data. This framework bridges neuroscience and biomechanics by providing a platform to model the generative process of motor control, potentially advancing motor control research and simulation-based behavioral studies. It could enable researchers to test hypotheses about neural control in realistic physics environments, impacting fields like neuroethology and robotics. MIMIC-MJX trains neural controllers to actuate biomechanical animal models in physics simulation to reproduce real kinematic trajectories. The framework is designed to be generalizable to diverse animal body models and can be trained with modest amounts of motion data, making it accessible for a wide range of research applications.

## [Layer Pruning Hurts Generative Reasoning More Than Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01997) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that layer pruning degrades generative reasoning capabilities in LLMs more severely than classification, and that simple fine-tuning recovery is insufficient. This finding challenges the viability of layer pruning as a compression technique for LLMs used in generative tasks, which are increasingly common in real-world applications. It highlights the need for more nuanced compression strategies that preserve algorithmic reasoning abilities. Under realistic post-training constraints (single 80GB GPU, no pretraining-scale data), supervised fine-tuning with self-generated responses recovers up to 90% of baseline performance on classification, but generative reasoning recovery remains limited. The gap persists even with task-aligned fine-tuning on GSM8K, and a depth-pruned model trained with nearly 100B post-pruning tokens still shows deficits on simple arithmetic.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Layer pruning is a model compression technique that removes entire layers from a neural network to reduce size and computational cost. While it has shown promise for classification tasks, generative reasoning tasks like math word problems (GSM8K) and code generation (HumanEval) require multi-step algorithmic capabilities that may be more sensitive to structural changes.

## [C-ICPE: In-Context Pure Exploration for Continuous Decision Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17976) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces C-ICPE, a theory-guided learned model for Bayesian fixed-confidence pure exploration with continuous recommendations. It meta-trains sequential architectures to jointly learn exploration, stopping, and recommendation strategies, enabling active evidence gathering and epsilon-optimal recommendation without parameter updates at inference. This work addresses a significant gap in pure exploration by extending to continuous decision spaces, which are common in modern applications like continuous-armed bandits and function minimization. It offers a learned, model-agnostic alternative to existing frequentist and model-specific methods, potentially improving efficiency and applicability across various AI/ML tasks. C-ICPE consists of three components: inference, stopping, and exploration. It meta-trains deep neural policies to map observation histories to the next continuous query action and a predicted hypothesis, enabling transferable learning across tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Pure exploration, also known as active sequential testing, involves adaptively acquiring information to identify an unknown ground-truth hypothesis with minimal queries. Traditional methods are often frequentist and model-specific, while learned approaches have been limited to finite recommendation spaces. This paper extends in-context learning to continuous spaces, where the hypothesis or recommendation space is continuous, such as in continuous-armed bandits or function minimization.

## [Neural Operator Framework Learns Stability and Receptivity from Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19465) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a data-driven framework that uses a neural network as a dynamics emulator and automatic differentiation to extract its Jacobian, enabling computation of eigenmodes and resolvent modes directly from observation data without governing equations. The method is demonstrated on chaotic models and high-dimensional fluid flows, successfully identifying dominant instability modes and input-output structures even in strongly nonlinear regimes. This framework bypasses the need for governing equations, enabling stability and receptivity analysis for nonlinear or poorly modeled systems, which is a fundamental challenge in physics and engineering. It could significantly impact scientific machine learning and fields like climate science, neuroscience, and fluid engineering by providing a broadly applicable tool for analyzing complex, high-dimensional datasets. The approach trains a neural network as a dynamics emulator and uses automatic differentiation to extract its Jacobian, from which eigenmodes and resolvent modes are computed. It is demonstrated on canonical chaotic models and high-dimensional fluid flows, showing effectiveness even in strongly nonlinear regimes, and provides a nonlinear representation of system dynamics while retrieving intricate dynamical patterns.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional stability and receptivity (resolvent) analyses rely on known governing equations and linearization, limiting their use in nonlinear or poorly modeled systems. Neural operators are a deep learning framework that learns mappings between function spaces, and resolvent analysis identifies the most responsive forcings and receptive states of a dynamical system in an input-output sense. This work combines these concepts to create an equation-free methodology.

## [Lean Refactor: Agentic Framework for Multi-Objective Proof Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20244) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Lean Refactor introduces a plug-and-play retrieval-augmented agentic framework that refactors Lean proofs with multi-objective control, achieving over 70% token compression on competition benchmarks and up to 60% compilation-time reduction. It is version-robust, using version-filtered retrieval to improve compression on target Lean versions and enhance zero-shot transfer to future releases. This work addresses a critical gap in formal verification: LLM-generated proofs are often verbose and brittle across library versions, hindering practical adoption. By providing a training-free, version-robust refactoring method, it could significantly improve the usability and maintainability of proof assistants like Lean, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The framework steers a frozen agentic LLM using retrievals from a curated database of multi-objective refactoring strategies, each annotated with metadata like supported Lean/Mathlib versions and expected compilation-cost reduction. Experiments show over 20% token compression on research repositories, and refactored miniF2F proofs exhibit stronger zero-shot version transfer to future Lean releases.

**Background**: Lean is a proof assistant and functional programming language based on the Calculus of Inductive Constructions, used for formal verification. LLM-generated proofs are often correct but verbose and brittle across library versions, motivating the need for automated refactoring.

## [Audit Gap: LLM Safety Evaluations Bypassed by Internal Perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08044) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the concept of the 'audit gap' and demonstrates that static behavioral safety audits of LLMs can be bypassed by small internal perturbations. The authors construct 'dissociated models' from three safety-aligned bases (Gemma 2 2B, Llama 3.2 3B, Qwen 2.5 3B) that pass all static audits yet are vulnerable to latent attacks. This finding challenges the adequacy of current behavioral safety evaluations for LLMs, suggesting that static audits cannot certify representation-level robustness. It could influence future evaluation methodologies and highlight the need for intervention-based testing in AI safety. The dissociated models score 2.5 to 3.1 times higher on the Latent Vulnerability Score (LVS) than their bases at the targeted mid layer. A bounded latent attack elicits harmful compliance on 54-86% of prompts (vs. 3-48% for bases), while random perturbations stay at or below 12%. Harmful fine-tuning reaches high compliance within five gradient steps, compared to 10-25 for bases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM safety evaluation typically relies on behavioral tests, where a model is deemed safe if it refuses harmful prompts and answers benign ones. However, these tests only observe outputs and do not measure how easily an intervention can turn a refusal into compliance. The 'audit gap' refers to this discrepancy between static audit results and the model's actual vulnerability to internal perturbations. The paper demonstrates this gap by constructing models that match their safety-aligned bases on all static audits but are easily compromised by small latent-space interventions.

## [CADET: Training-Free Framework to Audit and Repair Spurious Reliance in E2E Driving Planners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CADET is a training-free framework that audits, benchmarks, and repairs spurious reliance in pretrained end-to-end driving planners without any parameter updates. It addresses the problem of causal confusion, where planners rely on spurious correlations rather than true causal factors. This is significant because end-to-end autonomous driving planners are prone to statistical shortcuts that compromise reliability in long-tail scenarios, and existing remedies require retraining large models. CADET provides a practical solution for auditing and repairing deployed systems without retraining, which is crucial for real-world deployment and safety. CADET is training-free, meaning it does not require any parameter updates, making it suitable for auditing already-deployed planners. It targets causal confusion, a problem where planners associate co-occurring scene elements with driving decisions rather than causal variables, and prevailing open-loop metrics like L2 displacement and collision rate fail to detect such spurious reliance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: End-to-end autonomous driving planners trained by imitation learning are prone to causal confusion, where they learn spurious correlations instead of true causal relationships. This issue is particularly problematic because it silently compromises reliability in long-tail scenarios and is difficult to detect with standard metrics. Existing solutions based on causal-intervention training require retraining large models, which is impractical for deployed systems. CADET offers a training-free alternative to audit and repair such planners.

## [Study Reveals 'Premature Termination' as Key Failure in Long-Context LLM Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29718) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies a previously overlooked failure mode in long-context LLM search, termed 'premature termination,' where models give up or provide uncertain incorrect answers before exhausting the context window. Through systematic study of four flagship models across three benchmarks, the authors show that the premature termination rate is positively correlated with context length, and they revisit mitigation strategies including context management and parallel sampling. This finding is significant because it provides a concrete, measurable failure mode for context rot, a widely recognized but poorly understood issue in long-context LLM deployment. Understanding premature termination can guide the development of more robust models and better mitigation strategies, ultimately improving the reliability of LLMs in long-horizon search tasks such as research and agentic workflows. The study analyzes seven context management methods across three categories, showing they are inherently test-time scaling strategies that reduce premature termination to enable more exploration, and provides model-dependent principles for method selection. For parallel sampling, the authors develop a behavior-aware filtering strategy that yields a performance gain of 2.6% to 4.9% across three aggregation methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Context rot refers to the degradation in LLM output quality as input context grows longer, even when the context window is not full. This phenomenon has been observed across various models and tasks, but the specific failure mechanisms in deep search scenarios were not well understood. The paper addresses this gap by systematically studying how models fail under extensive context and evaluating existing mitigation methods.

## [VLAFlow: Unified Framework for Comparing VLA Training Paradigms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01586) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VLAFlow introduces a unified flow-matching framework to systematically compare four vision-language-action (VLA) training paradigms under identical architecture and data conditions. Using the heterogeneous OXEMix dataset (~5,000 hours), the study reveals that combining language-supervised co-training with future latent alignment (MindLWPI) yields the most stable transfer performance across benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in VLA research by enabling controlled comparisons of training paradigms, which were previously confounded by differences in architecture, data, and evaluation. The findings provide actionable insights for developing more robust and generalizable robotic manipulation models, potentially accelerating progress in the field. The framework uses a pi0-style architecture with a shared VLM backbone and a 14-dimensional action space. Four paradigms are evaluated: action-only modeling (MindPI), language-supervised co-training (MindLPI), future latent alignment (MindWPI), and their combination (MindLWPI), tested on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and SimplerEnv benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action models (VLAs) integrate visual, linguistic, and action data to enable robots to perform manipulation tasks from natural language instructions. Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that offers stable training and fast inference, making it suitable for robot action generation. The Open X-Embodiment dataset is a large-scale, open-source collection of robot trajectories from multiple embodiments, providing a diverse training resource for such models.

## [Order-Equivariant Networks Unify Graph and Sheaf Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03798) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces order-equivariant neural networks (OENN), which generalize graph message passing and sheaf neural networks using equivariant bundles over face posets. The authors characterize all linear order-equivariant maps, build OENN layers, and prove universal approximation theorems for continuous order-equivariant maps, which are new even for sheaf neural networks. This work provides a theoretical foundation that unifies geometric and topological deep learning, potentially leading to more expressive and efficient architectures. It extends universal approximation results to a broader class of equivariant networks, which could influence future research in graph learning and topological data analysis. The paper proves universal approximation theorems for continuous order-equivariant maps, which are new results even when restricted to sheaf neural networks. It also clarifies the relationship between OENN and Category-Equivariant Neural Networks (CENN), showing how categorical symmetry can be leveraged in data.

**Background**: Geometric deep learning exploits group symmetries in data, while topological deep learning uses cell complexes and incidence relations. Sheaf neural networks extend graph neural networks by using sheaf Laplacians to model heterogeneous and asymmetric relations. This paper unifies these approaches via equivariant bundles over face posets, providing a common framework.

## [Training-Free Diffusion Acceleration via Truncated Jump Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06114) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Truncated Jump Sampling (TJS), a training-free acceleration method for diffusion and flow matching models. TJS stops the ODE at an early-exit time and decodes the clean sample using endpoint decodability, reducing NFEs by 20–70% with near-matched quality. This method offers a practical acceleration solution for existing diffusion models without retraining or architectural changes, potentially speeding up inference for widely used models like SDXL and SD3.5M. It also provides a theoretical framework (endpoint decodability) that could guide future acceleration techniques. TJS leverages the fact that intermediate states and path velocities in affine probability paths provide a principled estimate of the clean sample, formalized as the minimum-MSE estimator E[x0|xt]. The method requires no retraining, distillation, or architecture change, and is validated on SDXL, SD3.5M, Z-Image-Turbo, and three class-conditional benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion and flow matching models generate high-quality samples but require many neural function evaluations (NFEs) during ODE sampling. Existing acceleration methods often involve retraining, distillation, or trajectory redesign, which are costly. This paper explores a training-free route based on x-prediction, where the model directly predicts the clean sample, and introduces endpoint decodability to enable early stopping.

## [Subjective Risk Decomposition: A Unified Framework for Uncertainty Quantification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15196) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a novel viewpoint that uncertainty measures are not primitives but consequences of higher-level modeling decisions, derived via decomposition of a subjective risk based on a strictly proper loss. This approach recovers classic information-theoretic terms like reverse cross entropy and unifies numerous existing UQ measures under a common theoretical foundation. This work provides a unified theoretical foundation for uncertainty quantification, potentially reshaping how epistemic and aleatoric uncertainties are derived and understood. It could lead to more principled UQ methods and bridge connections between different approaches, benefiting fields like machine learning and decision-making under uncertainty. The paper extends the view to learning theory by introducing subjective risk analogues of excess risk, approximation error, and estimation error, and identifies connections to UQ. It is considered a first step towards a full learning-theoretic framework for uncertainty quantification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty quantification (UQ) in machine learning typically distinguishes between aleatoric (data-inherent) and epistemic (model) uncertainty. Strictly proper scoring rules, such as logarithmic loss, are functions that are minimized by the true probability distribution, and are widely used in prediction and estimation. The paper leverages these concepts to derive uncertainty measures from a subjective risk decomposition, providing a new perspective on UQ.

Researchers introduced BusinessCaseBench, a benchmark using business school case methods to evaluate LLMs on analytical knowledge work. It includes 615 questions from 238 cases across 18 disciplines, each paired with expert-written grading rubrics. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation by focusing on the analytical knowledge work that white-collar professionals perform daily, such as synthesizing complex information and exercising judgment under uncertainty. The findings that frontier AI models score highly and improve rapidly have significant implications for business education and entry-level professional roles. The benchmark uses the case method, a pedagogy practiced by top business schools, and grading rubrics derived from expert-written instructor case solutions. Results show that frontier AI models already score highly against these rubrics, and capability within one model family improves substantially over two years.

## [CausalForge: Self-Improving Agentic Framework for Automated Causal Inference Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22511) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CausalForge is a new framework that automates theoretical research in causal inference by combining a Lean proof assistant with a self-improving agentic pipeline. It includes Causalean, a Lean library with 7,035 machine-checked declarations, and CausalSmith, a pipeline that selects topics, proposes results, formalizes statements, and constructs proofs. This framework addresses the critical issue of unreliable LLM reviewers in automated research by using formal proof verification, which could increase trust in AI-generated scientific results. It has the potential to impact AI research methodology, particularly in causal inference, and may inspire similar approaches in other domains. The pipeline augments kernel verification with a statement audit that compares each formal theorem against the informal claim it is intended to express, acknowledging that machine-checked proofs only establish formal consistency, not semantic fidelity. The source code, formal library, and run records are publicly available on GitHub.

Agentic AI frameworks automate tasks by orchestrating multiple AI agents, and this paper applies such an approach to theoretical research in causal inference.

## [JigShape Benchmark Reveals VLMs Lack Geometric Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27670) ⭐️ 8.0/10

JigShape, a new benchmark using interlocking jigsaw puzzles with tab-and-blank pieces, evaluates visual-geometric reasoning in VLMs across 95K instances and four grid sizes. Results show that only GPT-5.5 exceeds random baseline on 4x4 puzzles, and all models collapse on larger grids, a phenomenon termed the 'scaling cliff'. This benchmark highlights a critical limitation in current VLMs: they struggle with geometric reasoning, especially as problem complexity increases. The findings challenge the assumption that scaling models alone can achieve robust spatial understanding, impacting future VLM development and evaluation. The benchmark uses interlocking pieces to eliminate ambiguity in texture-repeated regions, providing unambiguous ground truth. Supervised fine-tuning achieves >97% on 4x4, but all models drop below 5% on 12x12, indicating a fundamental architectural limitation in maintaining constraint satisfaction as piece count increases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but their geometric reasoning capabilities are often under-evaluated. Traditional jigsaw benchmarks use rectangular cuts, which can create ambiguous ground truth in areas with repeated textures. JigShape addresses this by using interlocking pieces with tabs and blanks, which provide strong local compatibility constraints, making the task unambiguous and more challenging.

## [Knowledge Distillation Has Asymmetric Effects on Bias in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28639) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.28639) reveals that knowledge distillation (KD) in small instruction-tuned language models has asymmetric effects on bias: it improves context-following on unambiguous tasks but degrades conditional refusal on ambiguous tasks, leading to more stereotype answers. The study proposes a three-step evaluation protocol called Per-Condition Calibration Diagnosis (PCCD) to properly measure these effects. This finding is significant because knowledge distillation is widely used to compress large models into smaller, more efficient ones, but its impact on bias and safety has been understudied. The results suggest that current distillation practices may inadvertently increase harmful stereotypes in small models, affecting AI safety and fairness in real-world deployments. On unambiguous tasks (BBQ-disambig), distillation from a Mistral-7B teacher reduced stereotype override from 44.5% to 37.2% and improved accuracy from 0.55 to 0.61 for SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct. However, on ambiguous tasks (BBQ-ambig), 15% of correct abstentions turned into stereotype answers, and the effect was worse for OLMo-2-1B-Instruct, with silence-loss reaching 49%. The paper also warns that some apparent improvements are artifacts of evaluation methodology, such as ignoring abstentions or using parser-invalid configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation is a technique where a smaller 'student' model is trained to mimic a larger 'teacher' model, often to reduce computational costs while retaining performance. The BBQ (Bias Benchmark for QA) dataset evaluates social biases in question-answering models, using both ambiguous and disambiguated contexts to test whether models rely on stereotypes. Conditional refusal refers to a model's ability to abstain from answering when context is insufficient, which is crucial for safety. The paper highlights that aggregate metrics like CrowS-Pairs can mask per-item harms, necessitating more granular evaluation.

## [Counterfactual Modality Attribution: New Framework for MLLM Explainability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00076) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Counterfactual Modality Attribution (CMA), the first framework to quantify modality-level contributions in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) using counterfactual generation and Shapley values. CMA generates image-only, text-only, and joint counterfactuals via coupled diffusion priors and converts them into attribution scores. This addresses a critical gap in explainability for multimodal models, which often produce correct outputs while relying on the wrong modality, masking shortcut learning and unsafe reasoning. It provides a principled method for auditing multimodal foundation models in safety-critical applications, complementing existing feature-attribution methods. CMA correctly identifies the decision-driving modality in 98% of controlled synthetic cases and outperforms baselines on a real-world multimodal clinical dataset. The approach uses a cooperative game-theoretic formulation based on Shapley values, which are computationally expensive but provide principled attribution.

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) combine information from images and text for high-stakes decisions. Existing explainability methods focus on identifying influential image regions or text tokens, but cannot answer which modality drives a prediction. Shapley values, from cooperative game theory, fairly distribute contributions among features, and are used here to quantify modality contributions.

This paper presents the first systematic study of prompt injection attacks in LLM-based multi-agent robotic systems, showing that attacks can propagate between agents and reduce task completion. Experiments cover direct and indirect injections across varying attack-goal complexities and injection strategies in both single-agent and multi-agent settings. As LLMs are increasingly integrated into autonomous robotic systems, prompt injection attacks pose serious safety risks, potentially leading to unsafe decisions and physical harm. This research highlights the expanded attack surface in multi-agent settings and underscores the need for robust security measures in embodied AI. The study found that attacks can propagate from one agent to others through shared prompt structures, with impacts varying based on prompt composition and the targeted agent. It also analyzed how architectural changes affect LLM queries and, consequently, attack success.

## [ACE-GraphRAG: Agentic Context Engineering for Hierarchical GraphRAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01269) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ACE-GraphRAG introduces an inference-time context policy layer that adapts hierarchical GraphRAG contexts to queries, using parallel differential retrieval to supplement evidence and improve generation. It is evaluated on HotpotQA, 2WikiMultiHopQA, and four UltraDomain subsets, outperforming existing RAG and GraphRAG baselines. This work addresses the representation-inference gap in hierarchical GraphRAG, offering a novel agentic context engineering approach that could improve retrieval-augmented generation systems. It highlights the importance of treating context construction as a query-dependent policy, potentially influencing future RAG designs. ACE-GraphRAG formulates context construction as a policy over gap-aware refinement, retrieval branches, and task-conditioned adaptation. Full-ACE applies the policy uniformly, while Adaptive-ACE selects task- and topology-specific policies, with Adaptive-ACE preferred on all four UltraDomain subsets.

## [SciStyleBench: Diagnosing Stylistic Bias in LLM-as-Judge for Idea Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01666) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SciStyleBench, a benchmark with three components—SciStyleStage, SciStyleMetrics, and SciStyleExtractor—to diagnose and mitigate stylistic bias in LLM-based evaluation of scientific ideas. Experiments show that SciStyleExtractor reduces Style Bias Index from 0.566 to 0.501 and increases Substance Recognition Rate and Adversarial Win Rate from 0.504 and 0.554 to 0.759 and 0.899, respectively. This work highlights a critical flaw in LLM-as-judge systems: they are often swayed by writing style rather than scientific substance, which can undermine the reliability of automated evaluation in research and development. By providing a benchmark and a mitigation module, it offers a path toward more robust and fair LLM-based evaluation, benefiting the broader AI and NLP community. SciStyleStage includes 600 scientific ideas and 15 style variants, generating 9,000 evaluation instances per setting across three contexts: no context, fixed-domain context, and open-domain retrieval context. SciStyleMetrics introduces three quantitative measures: Style Bias Index (SBI), Substance Recognition Rate (SRR), and Adversarial Win Rate (AWR). SciStyleExtractor is a plug-and-play module that predicts style type and deviation before style-conditioned evaluation, aiming to separate presentation style from scientific content.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge is a technique where large language models evaluate the quality of text outputs, often used as a scalable alternative to human annotation. However, these models can exhibit biases, such as favoring verbose or stylistically polished responses over substantive ones. This paper addresses stylistic bias specifically in the context of scientific idea evaluation, where the substance of an idea should be the primary criterion.

## [SPEE: A Unified Framework for LLM Self-Improvement via Experience Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02139) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SPEE is a unified post-training framework that sequentially performs explicit experience evolution followed by implicit policy optimization, enabling LLMs to self-improve by distilling transient interaction experiences into persistent model capabilities. It outperforms both test-time and training-time self-evolution baselines on five mathematical reasoning benchmarks across three model scales. This framework bridges the gap between test-time and training-time self-improvement in LLMs, addressing a critical underexplored stage of experience distillation. It has the potential to enhance LLM post-training efficiency and capability, impacting AI research and applications that rely on continuous model improvement. SPEE includes a continuously evolving global experience pool that consolidates knowledge from both successful and failed trajectories, filters low-utility experience, and mitigates post-hoc rationalization. The framework uses privilege-guided On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD) to internalize experience, followed by reward-driven reinforcement learning for policy optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) capable of self-improvement need both effective policy optimization and a mechanism to transform transient interaction experience into persistent capabilities. Existing methods are fragmented: test-time methods extract experience but cannot update model parameters, while training-time methods update parameters but lack explicit experience accumulation. SPEE introduces experience distillation as a critical intermediate stage to bridge these paradigms.

## [PhyCheck: New Benchmark for Physical Law Understanding in Video-LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02150) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PhyCheck is a new video question answering dataset with coarse-grained and fine-grained subsets, designed to evaluate and improve Video-LLMs' understanding of physical laws. It also includes a diagnostic subset with external causal context to test models' ability to recalibrate judgments. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in current video understanding evaluation, which focuses on object and action recognition but neglects physical law understanding. It provides a systematic framework for assessing and improving Video-LLMs, which is essential for advancing embodied intelligence and world models. The coarse-grained subset asks whether a video conforms to or violates physical laws, while the fine-grained subset examines the specific physical details responsible. Experiments with fine-tuned Qwen2.5-VL show substantial improvement in physical-consistency understanding, but the diagnostic subset reveals models still struggle to incorporate additional causal conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) integrate visual encoders with large language models to process spatial-temporal video information and generate descriptive text. They are used in video understanding tasks, but their ability to understand physical laws is underdeveloped. Existing benchmarks mainly assess the physical quality of generated videos, not the models' understanding of physical laws. PhyCheck aims to fill this gap by providing structured supervision for physical understanding.

## [ISEE: Interactive Semantic Enrichment for Database Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02604) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ISEE, an interactive system that evaluates the quality of database field descriptions, elicits domain knowledge from users, and collaboratively enriches semantics to improve downstream tasks like entity-linking. It includes a scoring system and user collaboration features, validated through user studies and quantitative evaluations. ISEE addresses a practical gap in LLM-based data agents by improving data semantics, which is crucial for their performance in data-related tasks. This can enhance the reliability and effectiveness of AI-driven data management and analysis, benefiting industries that rely on accurate data interpretation. ISEE introduces three key novel features: automatic description quality scoring, efficient user knowledge elicitation, and collaborative enhancement with domain experts. The system was evaluated through user studies, automated user simulation, quantitative metrics, and case studies, showing significant reductions in cognitive load and improvements in description quality and downstream task performance.

## [Meta-Persona Anchoring and Temperature Scaling to Combat LLM Homogeneity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02618) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Meta-Persona Anchoring combined with Filtered Temperature Scaling (FTS) to reduce the 'Artificial Hivemind' effect in LLMs. The method lowers average pairwise cosine similarity from ~0.85 to ~0.65 on the INFINITY-CHAT dataset using open-weight models under 20B parameters. LLM output homogenization limits diversity and creativity in AI applications, affecting fields like content generation and decision support. This framework offers a practical way to increase response diversity, potentially leading to more varied and human-like AI outputs. The method uses a two-stage generation process: first, the model self-selects a unique persona to anchor its response; second, a dual-stage sampling sieve applies Top-p filtering for grammatical validity followed by extreme temperature scaling (T ≥ 4.0). The approach is evaluated on the INFINITY-CHAT dataset and achieves a majority of questions below the 0.7 similarity threshold.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: The 'Artificial Hivemind' effect refers to the tendency of LLMs to converge on narrow, homogenized outputs even for open-ended questions, with high inter-response similarity (0.80-0.90). This semantic collapse reduces AI diversity. The paper proposes Meta-Persona Anchoring, which requires the model to self-select a unique persona, and Filtered Temperature Scaling, which combines Top-p filtering with high temperature to explore a broader probability distribution.

## [PULSE: Executable Contract Language for Spatiotemporal Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02630) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PULSE is a new executable contract language for spatiotemporal knowledge graph engineering, inspired by Object-Process Methodology. It provides a typed runtime that localizes four operational roles and their write effects, with a Lean 4 verified kernel ensuring formal safety properties. This addresses a real gap in knowledge graph engineering by making the execution contract explicit and verifiable, which can improve reliability and trust in systems that rely on spatiotemporal data. It could benefit domains like supply chain monitoring, environmental tracking, and geospatial analytics. The language fixes evidence non-overwrite, branch isolation, grounded multi-subject timers, guarded state change, and declaration-ranked event ordering. The implementation includes 88 tests, 3,534 bounded checks, and 32 Lean/Python runtime-kernel cases, and it matches a separate workflow on 37,440 generated temporal traces, distinguishing ten single-field mutants.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs store interconnected information, and spatiotemporal knowledge graphs add time and space dimensions. Contract languages specify the rules and constraints that govern system behavior. Object-Process Methodology (OPM) is a conceptual modeling language that unifies structure and behavior, standardized as ISO/PAS 19450. GeoSPARQL is a standard for representing and querying geospatial linked data, and Lean 4 is a proof assistant used for formal verification.

HyperAgent introduces a Tool-Schema Hypergraph-guided framework for dynamic planning and execution in tool-use LLM agents. It models tool relations at the schema level and constructs a directed hypergraph, improving task completion while reducing API calls, LLM interactions, and token consumption on AppWorld. This work addresses a significant challenge in reliable tool-use planning for LLM agents, which is crucial for real-world applications. By improving efficiency and reliability, HyperAgent could advance the deployment of AI agents in complex environments, impacting both research and industry. The framework first extracts a task-relevant tool context graph and constructs a schema-aware Task DAG. During execution, it dynamically realizes subtasks via deficit-oriented expansion, building a state-conditioned tool support graph to identify unresolved requirements and retrieve supporting producer tools.

## [Systematic Review Reveals Gap Between EU Right to Explanation and XAI Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A systematic review of 57 papers published from 2024 onwards found that only 19 substantively integrate both legal and technical perspectives on the EU Right to Explanation, highlighting a significant gap between legal requirements and XAI research. The review identifies three problematic patterns, including misidentification of the GDPR legal basis and conflation of explanation form and content. This matters because the EU Right to Explanation, enshrined in GDPR Article 15(1)(h) and AI Act Article 86, is a critical safeguard for individuals affected by automated decisions. The identified gap suggests that without better interdisciplinary synthesis, the right risks remaining a formal obligation without a technically realizable path to compliance, impacting AI governance and compliance efforts. The review analyzed 2643 initial records and reviewed 57 full texts, focusing on papers from 2024 onwards due to the final AI Act publication in July 2024. It proposes an Addressee/Purpose Framework and a four-phase blueprint for operationalization, along with six open research questions. Notably, few papers engage with the CJEU's Dun & Bradstreet judgment, likely due to publication timing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: The EU Right to Explanation is a legal right granted to individuals when algorithms make consequential decisions, such as loan eligibility or hiring. GDPR Article 15(1)(h) requires meaningful information about the logic involved in automated decision-making, while AI Act Article 86 extends this to high-risk AI systems. Explainable AI (XAI) aims to make AI decisions transparent and understandable, but translating legal requirements into technical implementations remains challenging.

## [Predictive Set Theory: A New Formal Framework for Cognitive Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02704) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Predictive Set Theory (PST), a formal generative framework that reconstructs cognitive architecture from first principles, operationalizing predictions, error responses, and consistency maintenance. PST is based on minimal operations such as a sensor as an identity function, set-theoretic state refresh, and three types of reference chains. PST addresses foundational gaps in predictive processing and Bayesian cognitive science by providing operational definitions and a generative account of cognitive referents. This could influence future research in cognitive science and AI/ML by offering a design specification for systems that must maintain internal consistency under incomplete information and risk. PST derives core cognitive functions including state sequences, demand, comparison, efficiency, and finite-horizon probabilistic planning. It also offers novel resolutions to classical problems such as Russell's paradox, Gödelian incompleteness, negative feedback grounding, and film editing comprehension.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Predictive processing theories view the brain as a hierarchical prediction engine that minimizes prediction error, but they lack operational definitions for predictions and error responses. Bayesian cognitive science assumes a closed hypothesis space and does not explain how probabilistic objects become discrete referents. PST aims to fill these gaps by providing a formal generative framework that specifies the minimal operations needed for cognitive architecture.

## [Hypercube Geometry Reveals Localized Complexity in Atomic Concept Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02930) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper analyzes the geometric structure of hypercubes and hyperplanes in atomic concept learning, showing that complexity is localized and bounded except for the full diagonal. It introduces a taxonomy of hyperplane behavior in higher dimensions, with a fully worked binary case and an explicit ternary hypercube treatment. This work offers a novel geometric-logical perspective that clarifies where complexity concentrates in atomic concept learning, potentially influencing research in logic, learning theory, and AI. It may lead to a new taxonomy of complexity and insights into constrained hypothesis spaces and structured classification. The paper shows that every hyperplane other than the full diagonal collapses into finitely many elementary-equivalence classes, with a bound independent of term depth, while the full diagonal's class count grows without bound. The three-dimensional case exhibits orthogonal families, partial diagonals, and the exceptional full diagonal, illustrating the essential phenomenon.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Atomic concept learning involves learning concepts from atomic facts, rooted in logical atomism as proposed by Bertrand Russell. Hypercubes are n-dimensional analogues of squares and cubes, and hyperplanes are flat subspaces that partition the space. Elementary equivalence is a model-theoretic notion where structures satisfy the same first-order sentences. This paper connects these geometric and logical concepts to analyze the complexity of learning.

## [Information Boundaries for Group-Robust LLM Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper reveals that reproducible compression scores can still select suboptimal LLM pruning candidates, and introduces information-theoretic boundaries to model this gap. It demonstrates improved group-robust pruning, reducing worst-group perplexity inflation by 12.6–20.9% across three dense LLMs. This work addresses a critical flaw in LLM pruning evaluation, where reproducible scores may mislead candidate selection. By providing a theoretical framework and practical gains, it advances model compression research and helps build more robust and efficient LLMs. The paper models the gap using information interfaces, including a conic law for equal-weight groups and exact observation-fiber radius. A group-resolved diagonal recovers broad damage order (Spearman 0.9239), and model-specific complete-mask endpoint selection improves over references by 2.7–8.0%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM pruning aims to reduce model size while preserving performance, often guided by compression scores that measure candidate quality. However, this paper shows that even highly reliable scores (e.g., 0.906 split-half reliability) can fail to select the best candidate, highlighting the need for information-theoretic analysis. Group robustness ensures that pruning does not disproportionately harm specific subgroups, which is crucial for fair and reliable deployment.

This paper introduces InK, a neurosymbolic hierarchical reinforcement learning framework that uses incremental knowledge for symbolic planning and low-level neural modules for motion primitives, demonstrating improved sample efficiency in sparse-reward navigation tasks. This work addresses a key limitation of standard HRL by allowing knowledge to be updated incrementally during exploration, which can significantly improve sample efficiency in long-horizon, sparse-reward tasks. It offers a promising direction for integrating symbolic reasoning with deep RL, potentially benefiting robotics and autonomous navigation. The framework uses D* for symbolic planning on an updatable representation of current knowledge, and low-level goal-conditioned neural modules learn motion primitives via reward shaping. Additionally, the authors develop Belief World Tree Search for optimal symbolic planning given prior knowledge, and the code is available on GitHub.

UrbanAgent is a novel tool-augmented agent framework that leverages LLMs to convert natural-language requests into executable cross-system workflows for urban tasks, addressing service fragmentation. It introduces Urban-Eval, a benchmark for evaluating both task results and execution quality, and achieves a 71% task success rate, 10 points above the strongest baseline. This framework addresses a critical gap in integrating fragmented urban digital services, potentially improving how residents interact with city systems. It demonstrates the effectiveness of combining LLM reasoning with tool augmentation, which could influence future applied AI systems in urban computing and beyond. UrbanAgent couples LLM capabilities with a tool-set supporting code execution, API calls, and the Model Context Protocol (MCP). It uses an adaptive closed loop to clarify missing information, ground tool use in live observations, and align final responses with evidence and constraints. The Urban-Eval benchmark evaluates required tool coverage, dependency validity, and evidence traceability.

## [DiffImaginE: Diffusion-Based Verification for Multimodal NER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03025) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DiffImaginE introduces a novel approach to multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) by using conditional latent diffusion inference for entity type verification. It replaces the deterministic verifier with a diffusion scorer that uses denoising error as a probabilistic score, achieving consistent gains over a matched deterministic control on Twitter-2015 and Twitter-2017 datasets. This work provides a probabilistic interpretation of entity type verification in MNER, which is a meaningful improvement over existing deterministic methods that lack explicit probabilistic semantics. It could influence future research in multimodal understanding and inspire new applications of diffusion models beyond generation. The method uses a type-conditioned denoiser to predict noise injected into a standardized latent, and the denoising error serves as an ELBO-consistent surrogate for negative log-likelihood. It employs classifier-free guidance, Min-SNR weighting, direct supervision of per-type diffusion scores, and antithetic sampling to reduce variance.

**Background**: Multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) aims to identify entity spans and their types using both text and images. Traditional verifiers often compress diverse visual realizations into a single prototype, lacking probabilistic semantics. Diffusion models, such as conditional latent diffusion, generate data by iteratively denoising a latent representation, and classifier-free guidance is a technique to control generation without a separate classifier.

## [MedPIC-Bench: LLMs Fail on Counterfactual Medication-Safety Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03028) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MedPIC-Bench, a new benchmark with 467 expert-validated questions that pair guideline-following scenarios with counterfactual variations. Across 28 LLMs, every model's accuracy dropped from 63.6% to 45.1% on counterfactual questions, revealing a systematic failure to apply patient-specific conditions. This benchmark exposes a critical gap in medical AI evaluation: static accuracy measures can overstate a model's reliability in patient-specific decision-making. The findings highlight that even medical-specific LLMs struggle with conditional rule application, which is essential for safe medication prescribing and could impact clinical deployment. The benchmark includes questions annotated along six clinical and reasoning dimensions, and it uses source-verifiable recommendations. Model rationales often acknowledge changed patient information but still retain the previous safety judgment, indicating a reasoning failure rather than a simple knowledge gap.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Medication-safety rules often apply only when specific patient conditions are met, such as age, pregnancy, or renal function. Existing medical benchmarks typically use isolated, fixed scenarios, so models can answer correctly by recalling drug-risk associations without demonstrating that they used patient information. Counterfactual reasoning—asking how a decision would change if a patient attribute were altered—is a standard method to test such conditional application, but it has been underexplored in medical LLM evaluation.

## [TraceCAD: Persistent-State Repair for Agentic CAD Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03062) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TraceCAD introduces a recovery layer for LLM-based CAD agents that maintains persistent state linking requested features, modeling steps, failure evidence, and candidate outcomes. It diagnoses faulty operations, performs bounded localized edits, and retains successful and failed repair outcomes in reusable skill memory, achieving competitive geometric quality on DeepCAD-derived benchmarks. This work addresses a critical limitation in agentic CAD generation: the loss of evidence during correction loops. By enabling persistent, localized, and reusable recovery, TraceCAD improves final CAD quality and repair reliability, which could accelerate the adoption of LLM-based agents in CAD software and related design tools. On DeepCAD-derived benchmarks with 200-model ablations and a 1K-model comparison, TraceCAD achieves competitive IoU, Chamfer distance, and Hausdorff distance. Removing persistent state nearly halves recovery score; removing localized search more than doubles geometric regression and doubles code-agent invocations. Initializing the skill store on disjoint training models further reduces retries, token cost, and latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based CAD agents generate parametric programs that can be executed to create 3D models, but their correction loops often lose track of what has been satisfied or failed. TraceCAD builds on prior work like DeepCAD, which established the sketch-extrude sequence representation for parametric CAD, and Zero-to-CAD, which uses LLMs with tool access to generate CAD construction sequences. The recovery layer acts as a persistent memory to guide repairs, improving efficiency and quality.

## [DyCA: Dynamic Instance Clustering Boosts LLM-Assisted Evolutionary Search Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03129) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces DyCA, a framework for Large Language Model-assisted Evolutionary Search (LES) that dynamically clusters instances and designs specialized algorithms for each cluster, improving tail robustness by 15.2% and overall performance by 7.1% over state-of-the-art baselines. Existing LES methods often optimize for average performance, leading to poor performance on rare or difficult instances. DyCA addresses this by constructing algorithm portfolios that adapt to heterogeneous problem distributions, enhancing real-world reliability and broadening the applicability of automated algorithm design. DyCA treats instance clustering as a co-evolving component, reusing accumulated evaluation data as feature-free signals to partition instances with similar algorithmic responses. It decomposes the mixed objective into structure-aware sub-objectives, enabling finer-grained guidance for specialized algorithm design, while maintaining competitive head performance.

However, most LES methods focus on average performance, which can neglect less common but critical instances. Algorithm portfolios, which combine multiple algorithms, have been explored to handle diverse problem instances, and DyCA extends this by dynamically clustering instances during the search.

UniGD also proposes Heterogeneous Ad-material Modeling (HAM) to handle diverse ad types (short-video, product, live-stream) via a shared backbone with type-specific capacity. ## [ARCHIVE: Logical Conflict-Based Ambiguity Detection for Open-Domain QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03177) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ARCHIVE, a framework that detects ambiguity in open-domain QA by verifying logical conflicts among answers, and QuireQA, a new benchmark of 4,703 queries. ARCHIVE improves F1-amb by up to 10.4% and F1-unamb by up to 21.6% while being 16× faster than the best competitor. Ambiguity detection is critical for open-domain QA systems to avoid answering wrong interpretations or unnecessary clarifications. ARCHIVE's logical conflict approach offers a more accurate and efficient solution, potentially improving user experience and reducing computational costs in real-world QA applications. ARCHIVE uses a cascaded design with a lightweight early-exit encoder for surface-detectable cases and a conflict reasoning module that models logical relations among answers, reinforced by an invariance objective for robustness. QuireQA spans factoid, non-factoid, and ill-formed queries, providing a comprehensive evaluation benchmark.

**Background**: Open-domain QA systems answer questions from a large corpus, but ambiguous queries can lead to incorrect answers. ARCHIVE addresses this by defining ambiguity as logical conflict, where valid answers cannot all be true under a single interpretation.

By separating content from rendering, SeaSlides improves coherence and usability, potentially enabling more reliable and maintainable automated slide generation for technical content. The evaluation combines the 128-task UltraPresent validation set with the new 32-task SeaSlidesBench-Rich, and across four generation models, SeaSlides backends produce more readable, content-oriented source than SVG-heavy generation.

Typst is a modern markup-based typesetting system that supports templates and functions, making it a suitable backend for such an approach.

## [Polynomial Representations Boost Traffic Scene Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This thesis introduces polynomial representations for traffic scene prediction in autonomous driving, achieving near state-of-the-art accuracy on Argoverse 2 and Waymo Open datasets while improving generalization and computational efficiency. This work challenges conventional sequence-based representations, offering a more efficient and generalizable alternative that could enhance the safety and reliability of autonomous driving systems. It also highlights limitations in current evaluation metrics, prompting a rethinking of how model performance is assessed. The polynomial representation is applied to both trajectory and map geometry, and a diffusion-based generative framework is used for multi-agent scene generation. Evaluations show reduced computational cost, smoother trajectories, and higher behavioral plausibility compared to baselines.

Polynomial representations offer a compact and smooth alternative, capturing motion dynamics with fewer parameters.

## [TraceMAS: Traceable Multi-Agent Forecasting with Causal Loop Diagrams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03339) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TraceMAS is an interactive demo system that introduces two causal loop diagram representations—Ideal CLD and Data-Grounded CLD—to enhance traceability in multi-agent forecasting. It is demonstrated on crude oil price forecasting, allowing users to inspect agent revisions, evidence, and data-model mappings. This system addresses a critical challenge in enterprise AI: the lack of inspectability in autonomous forecasting pipelines. By making evidence-to-forecast processes transparent, TraceMAS could increase trust and adoption of multi-agent systems in industries that require accountability, such as finance and energy. The Ideal CLD captures key factors and causal relations from domain documents, while the Data-Grounded CLD links those factors to internal variables, external data, or proxies. The demo interface supports comparing forecasting iterations, exploring causal maps, and connecting scenario forecasts to market narratives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Causal loop diagrams (CLDs) are visual tools that show how variables in a system are causally interrelated, with positive and negative links and feedback loops. Multi-agent forecasting systems use autonomous agents to interpret documents, search data, and generate code, but often lack transparency. TraceMAS leverages CLDs to organize agent outputs, making the reasoning behind forecasts more inspectable.

## [MMLongBench-Doc-V2 Fixes Annotations and Uses LLM Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03397) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MMLongBench-Doc-V2 corrects 106 annotation errors in the MMLongBench-Doc benchmark, each with documented page and arithmetic, and replaces the string matching metric with a pinned LLM judge that assesses whether a response means the reference. It also removes ten questions with wrong filenames and one duplicated question, resulting in 1,071 questions over 134 documents. This revision enhances the reliability of a widely used long-document QA benchmark, ensuring that model scores more accurately reflect true understanding rather than string formatting quirks or flawed ground truth. It provides a practical example of improving benchmark quality through annotation correction and LLM-based evaluation, which can influence future benchmark design in the community. The most reusable contribution is a decision procedure for when an empty set key may be widened without destroying a deliberate negative sample; applied to all 208 rows, it widened 14. V2 scores are not comparable with published V1 numbers, and the corrected corpus, per-entry correction record, and evaluation harness are available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: MMLongBench-Doc is a long-document QA benchmark with 1,082 questions over 135 PDFs, designed to evaluate multi-modal long-context understanding. The original benchmark used string matching for evaluation, which can penalize correct answers with different formatting (e.g., '1,358,000' vs '1358000'), and contained annotation errors. LLM-as-a-judge is a technique where a language model evaluates responses, offering more flexibility than exact string matching for semantic equivalence.

## [ExpG: Experience-Driven Adaptive Guidance for Robust Tool Use in AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03403) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ExpG, a mechanism that builds and refines adaptive guidance from historical execution trajectories to improve robust tool use in AI agents. ExpG consists of three phases: experience acquisition, experience distillation, and experience reuse, and demonstrates consistent improvements across tool selection, tool calling, and response generation tasks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in AI agents—robust tool use—which is increasingly important as agents interact with diverse external environments. By enabling smaller agents to outperform larger ones without ExpG, it offers a promising path toward more reliable and cost-effective agent deployment. ExpG's experience acquisition phase analyzes tool invocation quality from historical trajectories using multi-aspect attribution, while experience distillation filters unhelpful experiences and selects representative ones via an equivalence-class-based method. The experiments show particularly strong gains in challenging settings, and the code, experiments, and results are publicly available.

**Background**: AI agents increasingly rely on external tools to interact with environments, but ensuring robust tool use across diverse runtime conditions remains a challenge. Existing methods often focus on model capability rather than execution robustness. ExpG leverages historical execution data to create adaptive guidance, addressing this gap by capturing tool capability boundaries and best practices.

## [AI World Cup 2026 Benchmark: GPT-5.5 Thinking Predicts Spain as Champion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03416) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The AI World Cup benchmark evaluated ten LLM-based assistants on predicting the entire 2026 FIFA World Cup, with GPT-5.5 Thinking winning with 744 points and correctly forecasting Spain as champion. The benchmark used a unified prompt, JSON schema, and scoring procedure for all submissions. This benchmark provides a standardized method for comparing LLMs in real-world event forecasting, addressing the lack of controlled comparisons. It reveals that full-tournament forecasting tests different capabilities than match-by-match prediction, and that leaderboard rankings can be highly sensitive to scoring design. The final ranking was strongly correlated with knockout points (r=0.986) but not with group-stage points (r=0.055). Claude Sonnet 4.6 predicted the most group-stage outcomes (63.89%) but ranked sixth overall, and self-reported confidence was unrelated to accuracy (r=-0.060).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for forecasting real-world events, but comparisons are often confounded by differing information, tools, and evaluation rules. The AI World Cup benchmark standardizes this by giving all models the same tournament snapshot, prompt, JSON schema, and scoring procedure. JSON Schema is a standard for defining and validating JSON data structures, ensuring consistent output format. The benchmark materials and code are released publicly for replication.

## [Complex State Propagator Achieves 100% Accuracy on Deterministic State Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03425) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Complex State Propagator (CSP), a minimalistic recurrent architecture that uses complex-valued state propagation with block-level skip connections, achieving 100% accuracy and perfect F1 scores on deterministic state tracking tasks such as parity checking, modular counting, and parenthesis matching. This work challenges the dominance of attention-based models by showing that state propagation alone is sufficient for certain tasks, potentially leading to more efficient and interpretable alternatives for sequence modeling. It could inspire further research into minimalistic recurrent architectures and complex-valued networks. CSP represents the state as a complex-valued vector updated via input-dependent rotations, and uses block-level skip connections, element-wise complex normalization, and SiLU activation at sequence boundaries to enable deep propagation without gradient vanishing. The model is trained with Focal Loss and achieves perfect results on canonical tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer architectures have dominated sequence modeling due to attention mechanisms, but they may be overkill for deterministic state tracking tasks. Complex-valued state space models have been explored in traditional SSM theory and excel in domains with frequency-based information. Block-level skip connections, as used in cascaded V-Net, help improve gradient flow in deep networks. SiLU (Sigmoid Linear Unit) is a smooth activation function that introduces nonlinearity.

## [LeanMem: Efficient Long-Term Memory Framework for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03463) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LeanMem introduces a lightweight long-term memory framework that categorizes dialogue content into profile, event, and record memories, and dynamically selects memory types and retrieval budgets during inference. On LoCoMo and LongMemEval-S with GPT-4.1-mini and Qwen3-8B, it improves accuracy over the strongest memory-based baseline by up to 15.1 points at the lowest or near-lowest cost. This framework addresses a critical limitation in LLM agents: the trade-off between token efficiency and preserving fine-grained evidence in long-term memory. By tailoring memory handling to content nature, it could significantly improve the performance and cost-effectiveness of agent systems in real-world applications. LeanMem filters out low-value content, stores informative segments as compact profile memory, temporally structured event memory, or source-grounded record memory, and selectively updates only dynamically evolving event memories. The framework dynamically allocates retrieval budgets based on query-specific evidence demands, ensuring relevant evidence is assembled on demand.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents rely on long-term memory to support extended interactions beyond finite context windows. Existing memory systems often use uniform summarization and retrieval pipelines, which either consume excessive tokens or lose fine-grained evidence. LeanMem differentiates content based on compressibility, temporal dynamics, and fidelity requirements to optimize memory management.

## [ChartAnno: New Benchmark for Chart Annotation in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03464) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ChartAnno is a newly introduced benchmark for evaluating multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on chart annotation generation, containing 1,200 real-world charts with paired code and annotation instructions across three levels of instruction specificity. The study evaluates 10 representative MLLMs under two primary input settings (chart code alone, and both chart code and image) and finds that proprietary models lead overall, though open-source models are narrowing the gap. Chart annotation is a common yet underexplored task that requires models to infer intent and design effective annotations, making this benchmark a valuable resource for advancing MLLM capabilities in data communication. The findings provide insights into the strengths and limitations of current models, guiding future research and development in chart understanding and generation. The benchmark includes three levels of instruction specificity, and the study also performs an image-only ablation. Results show that more specific instructions improve annotation quality, while inferring abstract intent remains the most difficult; providing chart images brings limited overall gains, with improvements mainly in design-related metrics.
