## [ToolLIFT: Function-Level Graphs for Generalizable Tool Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03468) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ToolLIFT, a new framework, lifts tool-specific trajectories into function-level workflow graphs (FWG) to enable generalizable tool planning for LLM agents. It introduces trajectory-lifting, decoupled workflow planning and tool selection, and RL-based source-gated rewards, outperforming baselines on ID and OOD benchmarks. This addresses a key limitation in LLM agent tool planning: poor generalization to unseen tool sets. By abstracting workflows at the function level, ToolLIFT could make AI agents more adaptable and reduce the need for retraining when tools change, impacting real-world agent deployments. The framework uses a trajectory-lifting mechanism to encode workflow structures in the FWG, and decouples workflow planning from tool selection. It employs RL with source-gated and skill-specific rewards to maintain traceable dataflow, and was validated on two in-distribution and three out-of-distribution benchmarks.

**Background**: LLM agents often rely on historical tool-use trajectories to plan and coordinate tool usage. Existing methods build tool-level graphs from these trajectories, which are tied to specific tools and fail to generalize. ToolLIFT leverages the insight that analogous tasks share common function-level workflow structures, enabling transferable planning across different tool sets.

## [FOUND-AF: Standardized Benchmark for ECG Foundation Models in AF Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03597) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FOUND-AF introduces a unified, leakage-controlled benchmarking framework to evaluate nine publicly available ECG foundation models from five families (HuBERT-ECG, CLEF, ST-MEM, ECG-JEPA, ECGFounder) on four heterogeneous datasets (AFDB, CinC2017, CPSC2021, LTAFDB) for atrial fibrillation detection. Under identical experimental conditions, ECGFounder consistently achieved the strongest overall performance with a favorable trade-off between accuracy, model size, inference time, and memory usage. This benchmark addresses the critical need for standardized evaluation in the rapidly growing field of ECG foundation models, enabling fair comparisons and informed model selection for clinical deployment. It demonstrates that compact, clinically pretrained encoders can deliver robust and computationally efficient AF detection, potentially accelerating the adoption of these models in real-world healthcare settings. The framework uses frozen feature extractors with standardized preprocessing, model-native resampling, a fixed XGBoost classifier, and recording-level grouped cross-validation to ensure leakage control. Evaluation includes classification metrics, ROC analysis, paired recording-level bootstrap comparisons with Holm correction, embedding-space visualization, and computational efficiency profiling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Atrial fibrillation (AF) is the most common sustained cardiac arrhythmia, increasing risks of stroke, heart failure, and mortality. ECG foundation models are pretrained on large, heterogeneous ECG datasets to learn transferable representations that can be fine-tuned or used as feature extractors for downstream tasks like AF detection. However, prior studies varied in datasets, preprocessing, classifiers, and validation protocols, making direct comparisons difficult. FOUND-AF provides a standardized, deployment-oriented benchmark to evaluate these models under identical conditions, facilitating reproducible research and practical model selection.

## [MRCF: A Modality Reliability-Calibrated Framework for Incomplete Multimodal Sentiment Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MRCF, a novel framework that explicitly models modality reliability to handle incomplete observations in multimodal sentiment analysis, addressing reliability mismatch and propagation bias. Experiments on CMU-MOSI, CMU-MOSEI, and CH-SIMS show strong performance under standard incomplete-observation protocols. This work addresses a critical gap in multimodal sentiment analysis by explicitly modeling modality reliability, which is often overlooked in existing methods. It could improve the robustness of sentiment analysis systems in real-world scenarios where modalities are frequently missing or degraded. MRCF consists of three components: a Reliability-Aware Branch that estimates sample-specific modality reliability from intramodal quality cues and cross-modal semantic consistency, a Reliability-Guided Interaction Branch that modulates cross-modal information flow, and a Reliability-Calibrated Fusion Module that integrates reliability and semantic cues for final prediction. The framework is evaluated on three benchmark datasets, demonstrating its effectiveness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal sentiment analysis (MSA) integrates text, audio, and vision to infer human affect, but real-world observations are often incomplete. Existing methods for incomplete-observation MSA follow two paradigms: reconstruction-based methods that recover missing information, and joint-representation methods that learn directly from incomplete inputs. However, these methods typically treat modality reliability only implicitly, leading to issues such as reliability mismatch and propagation bias.

## [SA-OPD: Filtering Spurious Signals in On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03632) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SA-OPD, a spurious-signal-aware on-policy distillation framework that identifies and filters misleading token-level supervision based on input-groundedness and optimization impact. It consistently outperforms vanilla OPD and competitive selective methods in both LLM and VLM settings. This work addresses a fundamental failure mode in language models where token-level judgments may rely on spurious signals rather than task-specific evidence, which can mislead distillation. By filtering these signals, SA-OPD improves the efficiency and effectiveness of knowledge transfer, potentially benefiting model compression and training pipelines across the industry. SA-OPD introduces a lightweight input-groundedness proxy to estimate whether a token-level signal truly depends on the input, and filters tokens that exhibit both low input-groundedness and extreme distillation divergence. The method is validated on both large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs), demonstrating consistent improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) is a knowledge distillation technique where the student model generates its own trajectories and a teacher provides dense token-level supervision, addressing distribution mismatch between training and inference. However, language models can produce token-level judgments driven by input-agnostic language priors or formatting conventions, which are termed spurious signals. These signals can generate large gradients but contribute little to task improvement, motivating the need for filtering mechanisms like SA-OPD.

## [Evaluating ZSC Algorithm Robustness to Implementation Details](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03644) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a new evaluation scheme called cross-implementation cross-play (XIXP) and applies it to the Other-Play algorithm, providing the first systematic evaluation of how implementation details affect zero-shot coordination (ZSC) algorithm performance. The findings suggest that for Other-Play, the standard single-implementation evaluation is a reasonable proxy for the more thorough cross-implementation evaluation. This work addresses a critical gap in ZSC evaluation, as current practices often rely on single implementations, which may not reflect real-world variations. By demonstrating that standard evaluation can be a reasonable proxy, it provides confidence in existing results and encourages more robust evaluation practices in multi-agent coordination research. The XIXP scheme varies implementation details that prior work has shown to affect MARL performance, such as neural network architecture and training hyperparameters. The study focuses on Other-Play, a popular ZSC algorithm, and evaluates its robustness across multiple independent implementations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Zero-shot coordination (ZSC) aims to enable agents to coordinate with unseen partners without prior joint training. In practice, ZSC algorithms are often evaluated using a single implementation trained across different random seeds, which may not capture the variation from independent implementations. This paper introduces cross-implementation cross-play to systematically test robustness to implementation details.

## [AutoSND: LLM-Driven Framework Automates Network Dismantling Heuristic Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03653) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoSND is a novel three-stage tree search framework that uses large language models to automatically discover network dismantling heuristics. It converts execution evidence into structural policies, yielding two final candidates, AutoSND-Q and AutoSND-S, which prioritize quality and speed respectively. This work addresses a key limitation in existing LLM-based heuristic design methods by enabling structural-level guidance from execution feedback, potentially improving the efficiency and interpretability of network dismantling algorithms. It could impact fields like network robustness analysis and automated algorithm design, offering a more scalable approach to discovering effective heuristics. The framework consists of three stages: broad exploration with simple heuristics, compilation of candidate records into structural policies, and continued tree search conditioned on these policies. Experiments on 12 real-world networks and 3 large networks show improved search performance and stability, with final candidates using residual degree as backbone and bounded local signals for node ordering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Network dismantling aims to identify a minimal set of nodes whose removal disconnects the network, which is crucial for assessing system robustness and containing misinformation. Traditional heuristics are manually designed, but recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled automated heuristic generation. However, existing LLM-based methods often fail to leverage execution feedback for structural improvements, which AutoSND addresses.

<a id="item-189"></a>
## [RAPO: Dual-Channel Framework to Curb Catastrophic Forgetting in Multimodal RFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03660) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Risk-Aware Policy Optimization (RAPO), the first dual-channel framework for explicit risk governance in continual reinforcement fine-tuning (RFT) of multimodal large language models. RAPO reduces final forgetting by 79.8% relative to its RLOO backbone on the MLLM-CL benchmark while retaining new-task competitiveness. This work challenges the common belief that RFT inherently resists catastrophic forgetting, showing that forgetting escalates under pronounced task distributional shifts. By providing a plug-and-play solution that generalizes to any RFT algorithm without cross-task memory, RAPO could significantly improve the reliability of continual post-training for multimodal LLMs, benefiting AI/ML researchers and practitioners. RAPO operates on two channels: the policy channel uses Risk-Aware Policy Scaling to adaptively calibrate per-sample update magnitude via rollout reliability and Fisher-inspired local predictive sensitivity; the data channel uses Risk-Aware Dynamic Bucket Sampling to reorganize training batches through dynamic risk stratification. It requires no cross-task memory and can be applied to any RFT algorithm without modification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement fine-tuning (RFT) is a method used to adapt large language models to new tasks via reinforcement learning, and it was previously believed to naturally resist catastrophic forgetting—the tendency of a model to lose previously learned knowledge when trained on new tasks. However, this paper shows that under significant task distributional shifts, forgetting escalates sharply across representative RFT algorithms. Fisher information is a statistical measure used in machine learning to quantify the sensitivity of a model's output to changes in its parameters, and it has been used in techniques like elastic weight consolidation to mitigate catastrophic forgetting.

## [Shielding for Higher-Order Safety: A Game-Based Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03662) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a finite-state safety-game construction for synthesizing shields that enforce high-order smoothness constraints, such as jerk limits, in cyber-physical systems. The authors define differential safety properties using finite differences over a discretized state space and reduce shield synthesis to an ordinary safety game over a history state space. This work addresses a real gap in runtime enforcement for cyber-physical systems, where classical shields only consider state predicates and ignore derivative constraints like jerk. It provides a formal framework and efficient synthesis algorithm, which could improve safety in applications such as autonomous vehicles and robotics. The synthesis algorithm stores exactly k past states for properties of order k, and the authors prove this memory is necessary. They also describe an iterative synthesis procedure for a maximally permissive shield that operates over hierarchies of derivative constraints, pruning the state space at each step for efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Safety shields are runtime enforcement mechanisms that restrict a controller's actions to guarantee safety. Classical shields are synthesized for state predicates, but many cyber-physical applications require constraints on derivatives of the state, such as speed, acceleration, and jerk. This paper extends shield synthesis to handle such high-order smoothness constraints using finite-state safety games.

## [TARL: Transaction-Aware Reliable Ledgers for Executable Memory Management in Long-Term Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TARL, a framework that maps each memory statement to one of five executable actions (add, ignore, revise, reject, defer) and updates accepted, pending, and rejected ledgers. It also presents TARL-Mem, a benchmark with fine-grained action labels and next-state targets, and demonstrates improvements in action prediction and state recovery across multiple evaluations. This work addresses a critical limitation in long-term agent memory management, where existing systems only make binary Write/Hold decisions, failing to distinguish between different types of information updates. By providing a more nuanced framework and benchmark, it could improve the reliability and accuracy of AI agents in real-world applications, reducing memory pollution and cumulative errors. TARL identifies the affected memory, resolves its temporal scope, compares source reliability, and updates three ledgers (accepted, pending, rejected). The framework is trained by comparing memory states produced by alternative update operations, and the TARL-Mem benchmark includes evaluations for in-domain, cross-source, temporal, counterfactual, and sequential scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term agents rely on persistent memory to retain knowledge, but a single update error can distort future retrieval and reasoning. Most existing memory systems simplify updates to a binary Write/Hold decision, which cannot capture the nuance of whether new information should be added, ignored, used to revise outdated beliefs, rejected as unreliable, or deferred for verification. TARL aims to address this by providing a more granular and executable memory update framework.

## [Black-Box Diagnostic for Epistemic Revision in LLM Collectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03722) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a black-box diagnostic to measure whether increased output dispersion in LLM collectives reflects genuine epistemic revision or merely premise-preserving reformulation. It proposes the Coherence Index (CI) with the Meta-Predictive Clarity System (MPCS) and evaluates five-agent collectives from gpt-4o-mini and gemini-2.5-flash across 310 paired episodes per condition. This work addresses a critical gap in collective intelligence research by distinguishing genuine epistemic revision from superficial output diversity in LLM collectives. It provides a reusable method that could improve the reliability of LLM-based collective decision-making and highlight model-specific limitations. On gpt-4o-mini, conditional dissent improved false-premise recovery by +17.7 points (p<1e-6), while static persona diversity harmed recovery (-8.1, p=.007). On gemini-2.5-flash, the same intervention yielded no gain (26.1% vs 27.1%, p=.84) despite a verified dispersion drop, and mechanism tagging showed 94% of post-RDP responses reformulate rather than concede (vs 24% on GPT).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Collective intelligence research often treats disagreement as evidence of epistemic diversity, but in LLM collectives, diverse outputs may not indicate genuine belief revision. The paper operationalizes dispersion-revision coupling, measuring whether interventions that increase output dispersion also lead to changes in epistemic stance. The diagnostic is black-box, operating on generated text alone, and uses the Coherence Index (CI) to verify dispersion changes and per-turn stance annotation to measure revision.

## [FISA: Failure-Informed Image Self-Augmentation Boosts MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FISA, a framework that generates augmented images from a model's own failure cases to improve multimodal large language models (MLLMs) without external supervision. It applies dual fidelity filtering to preserve semantic consistency and demonstrates consistent performance gains on visual question answering benchmarks. This work addresses the underexplored area of image augmentation for MLLM self-improvement, offering a principled way to reduce annotation costs. By aligning augmentation with the model's actual weaknesses, it could lead to more efficient and targeted training, benefiting the broader vision-language community. FISA constructs visually challenging yet answer-preserving image complications, verifies their utility through self-examination, and applies dual fidelity filtering to avoid semantic distortion. Experiments show compatibility with existing textual self-augmentation methods and superior data efficiency over generic image augmentation baselines.

Image augmentation typically uses generic transformations that may not address the model's specific weaknesses. FISA leverages failure cases to generate targeted augmentations, aiming to improve model performance more effectively.

## [KnowHal: Unified Benchmark for Multimodal Hallucination Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03782) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KnowHal is a new benchmark that integrates knowledge hallucination into a unified multimodal hallucination evaluation framework, covering four dimensions: entity, attribute, relation, and knowledge. It includes 1,800 samples across 10 domains and 50 categories, constructed via a semi-automated pipeline with LLM assistance, CLIP-based filtering, and human verification. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing evaluation frameworks by unifying knowledge-related failures with other hallucination types, enabling a more comprehensive assessment of MLLMs. It reveals that the knowledge dimension is the most challenging for nearly all evaluated models, highlighting a critical area for improvement in AI safety and reliability. The benchmark uses paired positive and negative questions over shared images and entities, enabling controlled comparisons among perceptual errors, knowledge-related errors, and false-premise acceptance. The evaluation of 14 representative MLLMs shows that most models exhibit substantial performance degradation on negative questions, indicating limited robustness to false premises.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) often generate hallucinated content that is inconsistent with the visual input. Existing benchmarks typically focus on entity, attribute, and relation hallucinations, while knowledge-related failures are often studied separately. KnowHal aims to provide a unified evaluation framework by incorporating knowledge hallucination as a fourth dimension, using a semi-automated pipeline that leverages LLMs and CLIP for efficient construction.

## [UNLINK-VL: Benchmarking Cross-Modal Knowledge Unlearning in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03791) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces UNLINK-VL, a real-world benchmark for evaluating cross-modal knowledge unlearning in vision-language models (VLMs). It uses visually identifiable entities and Wikidata facts to assess forgetting across textual, visual, and cross-modal scenarios. This benchmark addresses an underexplored area in AI safety: whether knowledge unlearning transfers across modalities. The findings reveal that text-only unlearning often fails to transfer to visual and cross-modal settings, highlighting the need for cross-modal evaluation to avoid overestimating unlearning effectiveness. UNLINK-VL comprises four subsets that test direct forgetting, relational propagation, preservation of non-target knowledge, and robustness to paraphrased queries. Experiments show that multimodal unlearning remains effective under textual evaluation, while text-only unlearning transfers poorly to visual and cross-modal scenarios.

UNLINK-VL leverages Wikidata, a structured knowledge base, to create realistic unlearning targets with associated images and relational facts.

## [LatentGuard: Efficient and Inspectable Latent Reasoning for LLM Safeguards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03838) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LatentGuard introduces a novel framework that brings continuous latent reasoning to LLM guard models, using a staged curriculum to compress textual rationales into compact latent states. Experiments show LatentGuard-8B improves mean weighted F1 from 83.95 to 84.91 over GuardReasoner-8B while reducing critical-path reasoning cost from 268.56 generated rationale tokens to 1.60 latent reasoning tokens. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying reasoning-based guard models: the high cost of generating explicit rationales for every interaction. By enabling efficient latent reasoning with an on-demand audit decoder, LatentGuard offers a practical path toward scalable and inspectable LLM safeguards, which is essential for real-world AI safety deployment. LatentGuard uses a staged curriculum to progressively compress task-aligned textual rationales into compact latent states, enabling safety verdicts to be predicted directly from continuous representations. An isolated auxiliary decoder generates compact audit artifacts on demand, keeping rationale generation off the standard inference path; the audit decoder achieves an audit utility score of 85.75.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Guard models are safety-critical components that filter harmful content in LLM inputs and outputs. Traditional reasoning-based guard models generate explicit rationales, which are costly, while latent reasoning methods reduce token generation but lack inspectability. LatentGuard combines the efficiency of latent reasoning with an on-demand audit mechanism, addressing both deployment cost and transparency concerns.

ADMITBench introduces a reference framework for evaluating the admissibility of industrial LLM advisories at the action level, using a versioned, safety-governed evaluation contract. Release 0.1.0 is a public reference implementation for research, not for physical execution. This framework addresses a critical gap in AI safety for industrial applications by providing a structured method to assess whether LLM recommendations are evidence-based, authorized, and safe. It helps prevent unsafe actions in industrial settings, benefiting organizations deploying LLMs in critical infrastructure. The framework implements non-compensatory checks covering record integrity, evidence and state validity, hazard understanding, action admissibility, and physical consequence verification. It explicitly distinguishes between safety-governed evaluation and safety certification, clarifying that the evaluator, model, or plant is not certified.

## [TACT: Taxonomy-Aligned Post-Training for Adaptive ESL Tutoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03952) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TACT introduces a taxonomy-aligned post-training framework for LLM-based ESL tutors, developing two taxonomies (Tutor-Strategy with 13 strategies and Student-Move) and a corpus (TACTCorpus) with 32,379 annotations. The resulting model, TACTutor, improves over its backbone by 20.30% on TACTBench and outperforms proprietary baselines in a blinded study with 50 learners. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM-based ESL tutoring by integrating human-tutoring principles into model training and evaluation, moving beyond mere fluency to pedagogical adaptivity. It provides an open foundation (data, benchmark, model weights) that could accelerate research and development of more effective AI tutors for language learners. TACTutor is built by post-training Qwen3.5-4B using supervised fine-tuning followed by taxonomy-aligned Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), optimizing for scaffolding quality rather than reference imitation. The TACTBench benchmark includes 78 authentic tutoring contexts, and the model maintains performance on external educational benchmarks while achieving the highest mean rating in a blinded learner study.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for conversational practice in English-as-a-second-language (ESL) education, but effective tutoring requires selecting appropriate pedagogical actions based on learner behavior and dialogue context. Human-tutoring research provides principles for adaptive support, yet these are often task-specific and not well integrated into LLM training. TACT addresses this by creating taxonomies grounded in established literature to guide post-training and evaluation, aiming to make AI tutors more pedagogically adaptive.

<a id="item-201"></a>
## [Voice vs. Keyboard Input: How Perturbations Affect LLM Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03970) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study introduces HIVE, a suite of voice transcription and QWERTY keyboard perturbations, and systematically evaluates their impact on LLM performance. It finds that voice transcription noise significantly reduces accuracy across instruction-tuned models, while keyboard errors are more tolerable, with token survival being the key factor. This research addresses a practical and under-explored question about how different input modalities affect LLM robustness, which is crucial for real-world applications like voice assistants and dictation tools. The findings can guide developers in designing more robust systems and understanding the limitations of current models. The study presents seven findings, including that voice transcription perturbations lower accuracy across all tested instruction-tuned models, and the structure of transcription, not fillers, carries the cost. It also shows that the gap between voice and keyboard channels appears only in tasks requiring construction or deduction, not in multiple choice, and that a thinking budget recovers keyboard performance but not voice.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used with various input methods, including typing and voice. Voice input often involves automatic speech recognition (ASR) that introduces transcription errors, while keyboard input may have typos. Understanding how these perturbations affect model performance is important for building robust systems. The study introduces HIVE to systematically test these effects.

## [Multi-Camera Trajectory Forecasting with Trajectory Tensors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.04694) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the problem of multi-camera trajectory forecasting (MCTF) and proposes a Which-When-Where framework that predicts object trajectories across camera networks. It also introduces trajectory tensors, a new representation for encoding multi-camera trajectories and uncertainties, and provides a new dataset (WNMF) with 600 hours of video from 15 camera views. This work addresses a significant limitation of existing trajectory forecasting methods, which typically focus on single-camera scenarios, by enabling long-term forecasting across camera networks. This is crucial for applications like surveillance and traffic monitoring, where multi-camera setups are common and a wider field-of-view is needed. The proposed trajectory tensor models outperform coordinate-based MCTF models and adapted SCTF methods in experiments. The framework follows a Which-When-Where approach, predicting in which cameras the object appears, and when and where within the views. The code and dataset are publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Trajectory forecasting is a key task in computer vision, used in autonomous driving, surveillance, and robotics. Traditional methods typically predict trajectories in a single camera view, which limits the field-of-view and makes long-term prediction difficult. Multi-camera setups can overcome this by covering larger areas, but they introduce challenges such as data association and cross-view coordination. The proposed trajectory tensors offer a unified representation to handle these challenges.

## [CLIP-EBC: Enhanced Blockwise Classification Enables Accurate CLIP-Based Crowd Counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09281) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CLIP-EBC, the first fully CLIP-based model for crowd density estimation, along with the Enhanced Blockwise Classification (EBC) framework. EBC uses integer-valued bins and a regression loss based on density maps to improve counting accuracy, and CLIP-EBC achieves state-of-the-art performance on the NWPU-Crowd test set with an MAE of 58.2 and RMSE of 268.5. This work demonstrates that vision-language models like CLIP can be effectively adapted to regression tasks such as counting, which were previously dominated by specialized CNN architectures. It provides a new direction for leveraging pre-trained multimodal models in dense prediction tasks, potentially impacting fields like surveillance, urban planning, and public safety. The EBC framework is backbone-agnostic and can improve existing classification-based counting methods by up to 44.5% on the UCF-QNRF dataset. CLIP-EBC outperforms the previous best method STEERER by 8.6% in MAE and 13.3% in RMSE on NWPU-Crowd. Code and weights are publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Crowd counting is typically formulated as a regression problem where a model predicts a density map from an image, and the total count is obtained by integrating the density. CLIP (Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training) is a vision-language model trained on image-text pairs, excelling in recognition tasks but not directly suited for regression. Traditional classification-based counting methods quantize count values into bins, but this introduces label ambiguity near bin boundaries and ignores regression accuracy.

## [Evaluating 45 Speech Quality Metrics for Neural Audio Codecs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24457) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper evaluates 45 objective speech quality metrics by correlating their scores with subjective listening scores for clean speech across 17 codec conditions, finding that neural-based metrics like scoreq and utmos achieve the highest Pearson correlations. It also reveals that non-intrusive metrics tend to saturate at high subjective quality levels. This study provides practical guidance for researchers and engineers in selecting reliable objective metrics for evaluating neural audio codecs, which is crucial for advancing audio compression technology. Identifying saturation issues helps avoid misleading quality assessments, especially for high-quality codecs. The evaluation covers 45 metrics, including intrusive and non-intrusive types, and uses Pearson correlation as the primary measure. The findings indicate that neural-based metrics outperform traditional ones, but non-intrusive metrics saturate at high quality levels, suggesting a need for caution when using them for high-quality codec evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Objective speech-quality metrics are algorithms that predict perceived speech quality without human listeners, commonly used to evaluate codecs. Traditional metrics like PESQ and POLQA are intrusive, requiring a clean reference signal, while neural-based metrics like scoreq and utmos are trained on large datasets to align with human Mean Opinion Scores (MOS). Scoreq uses a contrastive regression loss to improve generalization, and utmos combines ensemble learning with self-supervised learning features.

<a id="item-205"></a>
## [PASE: Low-Hallucination Generative Speech Enhancement via WavLM Phonological Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13300) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PASE is a novel generative speech enhancement framework that leverages the phonological prior embedded in the pre-trained WavLM model to reduce linguistic and acoustic hallucinations. It adapts WavLM into a denoising expert via representation distillation and trains a vocoder with a dual-stream representation to preserve speaker identity and prosody. This work addresses a critical limitation of generative speech enhancement models—hallucinations under severe noise—which can lead to incorrect spoken content or inconsistent speaker characteristics. By reducing hallucinations, PASE could improve the reliability of generative SE in real-world applications such as hearing aids, teleconferencing, and voice assistants. PASE categorizes hallucinations into linguistic and acoustic types, and introduces targeted strategies: representation distillation to clean WavLM's final-layer features, and a dual-stream vocoder training that separates high-level phonetic content from low-level acoustic features. Experimental results show PASE surpasses state-of-the-art discriminative models in perceptual quality and significantly outperforms prior generative models with lower hallucinations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Generative speech enhancement (SE) models, such as diffusion-based approaches, have achieved superior perceptual quality compared to traditional discriminative methods. However, they can hallucinate under severe noise, producing incorrect spoken content or inconsistent speaker characteristics. WavLM is a self-supervised speech model that captures rich phonological information, which can serve as a robust prior to constrain the generation process.

## [StuPASE: Low-Hallucination Studio-Quality Speech Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09234) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StuPASE, an extension of PASE, introduces dry-target finetuning and a flow-matching module to achieve studio-quality speech enhancement while maintaining low hallucination, outperforming state-of-the-art methods. The paper is accepted by Interspeech 2026. This work addresses the critical trade-off between perceptual quality and hallucination in generative speech enhancement, which is essential for real-world applications like hearing aids and voice assistants. By improving PASE, it pushes the boundary of what generative models can achieve in adverse acoustic conditions. The dry-target finetuning replaces targets with simulated early reflections, substantially improving dereverberation. The flow-matching module replaces the GAN-based generative module in PASE, enabling studio-quality generation even under strong additive noise. Audio demos are available online.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Generative speech enhancement (SE) aims to restore clean speech from noisy recordings, but often suffers from hallucination—generating incorrect content or speaker characteristics under severe noise. PASE is a prior framework that categorizes hallucinations into linguistic and acoustic types and uses phonological priors from WavLM to reduce them. Flow matching is a recent generative modeling paradigm that combines aspects of continuous normalizing flows and diffusion models, offering stable training and high-quality samples.

## [UniPASE: Universal Speech Enhancement with High Fidelity and Low Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14606) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UniPASE is a new generative model for universal speech enhancement that extends the PASE framework. It introduces DeWavLM-Omni, a unified representation-level module fine-tuned from WavLM via knowledge distillation, enabling high-fidelity and low-hallucination enhancement across multiple sampling rates. This work addresses critical issues of fidelity and hallucination in speech enhancement, which are important for real-world applications like hearing aids and voice assistants. By achieving state-of-the-art performance and winning the URGENT 2026 Challenge, UniPASE demonstrates a significant advancement that could influence future research and deployment in the speech processing community. UniPASE uses a neural vocoder to reconstruct 16-kHz waveforms from enhanced acoustic representations, and a PostNet converts these to 48 kHz before resampling to original rates. The model is trained on a large-scale supervised multi-distortion dataset and achieves superior or competitive results on several evaluation datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Universal speech enhancement (USE) aims to restore speech from various distortions across different sampling rates. WavLM is a self-supervised speech representation model that captures rich information for full-stack speech tasks. Knowledge distillation is a technique to transfer knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, often used for model compression. Neural vocoders synthesize waveforms from acoustic features, enabling high-fidelity speech reconstruction.

## [LLM Legal Answers Often Cite Wrong Authority](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02621) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.02621) shows that LLMs can give correct legal answers while citing the wrong legal authority, and vice versa, across 238 Taiwan bar-exam items. The study proposes a joint evaluation of answer correctness and authority grounding for legal benchmarks. This finding challenges the common practice of scoring only final answers in legal AI benchmarks, which can mask critical errors in legal reasoning. It underscores the need for trustworthy AI in high-stakes legal domains, where citing the correct statute is as important as the answer itself. In criminal law items, 24.0–42.4% of valid responses were answer-correct but missed the gold authority, while 15.2–21.7% were answer-incorrect but cited it. The study also used a statutory-retrieval probe and a citation-abstention intervention to show that answer and citation behaviors can move separately at the output level.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Legal benchmarks typically evaluate LLMs by checking the final answer, even when models also provide legal citations. This paper tests whether answer correctness can serve as a proxy for authority grounding, using Taiwan bar-exam items that have verified governing provisions. The study also includes a preliminary extension to PRC civil law, observing similar citation-unrequested authority marking.

## [Speculative Correction: Draft-then-Refine Decoding for Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02625) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces speculative correction, a draft-then-refine decoding strategy for diffusion language models (DLMs), where a smaller model drafts a full response and a larger model refines it. Experiments with LLaDA2.1-Flash and LLaDA2.1-Mini show accuracy improvements (e.g., GSM8K-384 from 0.848 to 0.899) and speedups (up to 2.17x) over block-autoregressive baselines. This work provides a training-free, plug-and-play method to improve both accuracy and inference speed of diffusion language models, which are emerging as alternatives to autoregressive LLMs. It demonstrates that bidirectional refinement is a useful decoding primitive, potentially influencing future DLM deployment and inference optimization. The study evaluates two configurations: Flash-Flash (same model drafts and refines) and Mini-Flash (smaller Mini drafts, larger Flash refines). Causal ablations show that completed drafts provide useful initializations, and full global refinement yields additional gains on GSM8K, while local refinement captures much of the gain on MBPP and MATH. The results support a Pareto-frontier interpretation rather than uniform quality matching.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models (DLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising masked tokens, allowing bidirectional context, unlike autoregressive models that generate left-to-right. Standard decoding for DLMs often adapts them to block-autoregressive generation. Speculative decoding is a known technique for autoregressive LLMs where a small draft model proposes tokens and a larger model verifies them in parallel, reducing latency while preserving output distribution. This paper adapts the draft-verify idea to DLMs, using a draft-then-refine approach where the draft serves as an editable initialization for bidirectional refinement.

## [Multimodal Transformer Surrogate for CO2 Storage Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02629) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed a multimodal auto-regressive transformer surrogate that models variable well operations and quantifies uncertainty in geological carbon storage. Trained on 4000 GEOS flow simulations, it predicts saturation and pressure at monitoring locations with median saturation MAE of 0.028. This work advances surrogate modeling for carbon storage by handling variable operations and providing uncertainty quantification, which is crucial for safe and efficient CO2 sequestration. It demonstrates the potential of transformer-based models in subsurface applications, potentially reducing computational costs and improving decision-making. The surrogate processes three input modalities: 3D geomodel, scalar parameters for relative permeability, and control variables, fused via self-attention. It captures the switch from rate to bottom-hole-pressure control and achieves median relative errors of 0.2-5% for other quantities of interest. The model is used in a hierarchical Markov chain Monte Carlo data assimilation procedure, reducing uncertainty in key metaparameters like fault permeabilities.

**Background**: Geological carbon storage involves injecting CO2 into subsurface formations, requiring accurate modeling of complex processes. Traditional numerical simulations are computationally expensive, prompting the development of surrogate models. Transformers, originally for natural language processing, have been adapted for scientific applications due to their ability to handle sequential and multimodal data. The SEAM CO2 geomodel provides a realistic benchmark for testing such methods.

## [Thesis Proposal Targets Hidden Technical Debt in AI-CPS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02638) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new thesis proposal on arXiv outlines a research plan to characterize, identify, and repair hidden technical debt in AI-intensive cyber-physical systems (AI-CPS), including the development of an automated tool using agentic AI. This research addresses a growing but underexplored challenge in AI-CPS, which are critical in domains like autonomous vehicles and healthcare. Understanding and managing technical debt in these systems could improve their reliability and long-term maintainability, with significant implications for software engineering and AI deployment. The thesis proposes a three-phase approach: first, characterizing AI-CPS technical debt by analyzing AI ecosystems and repositories and interviewing developers; second, defining identification and mitigation approaches; third, developing and validating an automated tool that supports agentic AI solutions to monitor, govern, and repay the debt.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Cyber-physical systems (CPS) integrate computation with physical processes, and when they incorporate AI components, they become AI-intensive CPS (AI-CPS). Technical debt refers to the implicit cost of shortcuts taken during development, which can slow future progress. In AI systems, this debt can be hidden and not trigger errors, making it harder to detect. Agentic AI refers to autonomous AI agents that can take actions to achieve goals, which the proposed tool would leverage.

## [IR2Solve: Structured IR for Cost-Efficient Optimization Autoformulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02641) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IR2Solve introduces a pipeline that uses a single semantic LLM call to generate a schema-constrained intermediate representation (ModelIR), followed by deterministic verification and compilation to solver-ready formulations. It achieves strong objective correctness across six benchmarks while using significantly fewer LLM calls and tokens than existing methods. This work addresses the accuracy-cost trade-off in LLM-based optimization autoformulation, making it more practical for large-scale deployment. By reducing inference cost while maintaining correctness, it could enable broader adoption of LLMs in operations research and decision-support systems. ModelIR explicitly represents sets, parameters, variables, objectives, and constraints using restricted Python-like expression strings, and uses a scalar-constraint convention to avoid free-index errors. On a matched ten-instance cost panel, IR2Solve uses one semantic call per instance, while Chain-of-Experts and SAC-Opt use 8 and 39 calls, consuming 3.3x and 22.9x more tokens, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) can translate natural-language optimization problems into solver-ready formulations, but direct code generation is brittle and error-prone. Intermediate representations (IRs) are structured targets that separate semantic understanding from code generation, as seen in compilers like LLVM. IR2Solve leverages this concept to improve reliability and reduce cost in optimization autoformulation.

## [Verified Tool Calls Boost LLM Agent Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02645) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a verification-aware tool wrapper that augments tool calls with postcondition verification, verify-before-retry logic, and idempotency keys. It reduces duplicate actions while maintaining task success rates in simulated non-atomic failure scenarios. This addresses a critical reliability gap in LLM agents, which often assume atomic tool calls. The approach improves agent robustness without modifying the underlying model, making it practical for real-world deployments where timeouts and partial updates are common. The wrapper includes postcondition verification to confirm tool effects, verify-before-retry to avoid redundant retries, and idempotency keys to prevent duplicate actions. Experiments were conducted in a controlled simulated environment with injected non-atomic failures across multiple task templates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents rely on external tools for multistage tasks, but real-world tools often exhibit non-atomic behaviors like timeouts, delayed visibility, and partial state updates. Existing frameworks assume atomic calls with binary success/failure, leading to reliability issues. The proposed method strengthens tool interaction semantics without requiring model changes.

## [Cross-Anesthetic ECoG Decoding Fails at Threshold, Not Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02646) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.02646) separates representation from decision threshold in cross-anesthetic ECoG state decoding, showing that band-power features transfer across five anesthetics with high AUROC, while the failure is confined to the threshold. A causal, label-free baseline-anchored threshold fixes ketamine decoding, improving balanced accuracy from 0.50 to 0.85. This finding challenges the assumption that cross-drug decoding failures stem from representation differences, redirecting focus to calibration. It has implications for brain-computer interfaces and anesthesia monitoring, where robust decoding across drug classes is critical. The study used mouse electrocorticography (250-Hz-bandlimited LFP, sampled at 1875 Hz) and leave-one-anesthetic-out evaluation across isoflurane, dexmedetomidine, ketamine, propofol, and midazolam. Ketamine ranking achieved AUROC 0.980 (cluster CI 0.821–1.000), while fixed-threshold accuracy was not significant (p=0.3795), and Riemannian domain adaptation was net-negative.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Electrocorticography (ECoG) records electrical activity directly from the brain surface, providing high spatial and temporal resolution. Band-power features are commonly used in neural decoding, and Riemannian geometry is a popular approach for brain-computer interfaces, especially for domain adaptation. This study addresses the challenge of decoding anesthetic state across different drug classes, which is important for clinical monitoring and brain-computer interface robustness.

## [Fiduciary Theory Proposed as New Framework for AI Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02660) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.02660) argues that fiduciary theory, drawn from business ethics and law, should govern AI assistant deployment, deriving alignment criteria from the four canonical fiduciary duties of loyalty, care, good faith, and candour for the developer-user relationship. This interdisciplinary approach offers a novel grounding for AI alignment, shifting focus from values in user-AI interactions to obligations developers owe users. It could influence AI governance and design by providing concrete, legally-inspired criteria, potentially complementing existing ethical frameworks. The paper maps four user-side risks of extended AI assistant deployment to the four fiduciary duties and specifies institutional measures for discharging each duty. It argues these obligations hold independently of any de facto harm to users, grounding alignment in the developer-user relationship rather than in promoted values.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment typically focuses on ensuring AI systems act in accordance with human values, often using moral traditions like bioethics or virtue ethics. Fiduciary theory, from law and business ethics, imposes duties of loyalty, care, good faith, and candour on parties in positions of trust, such as corporate directors or trustees. This paper applies that framework to the developer-user-AI triad, where developers exercise discretionary control over AI behavior, memory, and engagement parameters.

## [Verifier-Guided Workflow Enhances Symbolic Model Discovery for Physical Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02662) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a verifier-guided (VG) workflow around the ODEFormer symbolic transformer, using dynamical and physical-admissibility criteria to select equations from a multi-trajectory candidate pool. It demonstrates improved performance on Van der Pol oscillators and successfully discovers reduced-order equations for vortex shedding at fixed and varying Reynolds numbers. This work addresses the open challenge of transferring pretrained symbolic transformers to high-dimensional physical systems, offering an interpretable and physically auditable alternative to opaque ML surrogates. It could enable reliable forecasting and model discovery in atmospheric and plasma systems, which are of societal relevance. The VG workflow outperforms the original ODEFormer workflow on held-out initial conditions for Van der Pol oscillators. For vortex shedding, it discovers fixed-parameter reduced-order equations that recover the fundamental shedding oscillator and higher harmonics without a wake-specific candidate library or prescribed Navier-Stokes structure, and the cross-parameter model generalizes to withheld regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: ODEFormer is a transformer model that performs symbolic regression of dynamical systems, inferring ordinary differential equations (ODEs) from observed trajectories. The Van der Pol oscillator is a classic nonlinear system with non-conservative damping, and vortex shedding is a fluid dynamics phenomenon where vortices form behind bluff bodies, relevant in engineering and atmospheric science. The challenge is that pretrained symbolic transformers often fail to transfer to high-dimensional, real-world data, which this work addresses.

## [CT-HEG: Bidirectional Timestamp-Attributed Event Graph for ICU Mortality Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02663) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CT-HEG, a continuous-time heterogeneous event graph schema for ICU in-hospital mortality prediction, and presents CHIRP-Net, a four-layer heterogeneous GATv2Conv network. On MIMIC-IV v3.1, CHIRP-Net achieves a 5-seed mean AUROC of 0.8449±0.0071, with an ensemble AUROC of 0.8618. This work addresses a critical challenge in clinical AI: modeling irregular, heterogeneous EHR data for mortality prediction. By demonstrating that graph-based approaches with bidirectional connectivity and time-attentive edge features can outperform traditional sequence models, it offers a promising direction for improving ICU care and patient outcomes. The CT-HEG schema encodes each ICU stay as a typed, timestamped graph with three node types (visit, vital, lab_event) and 2D edge attributes (t_hours/48, value_norm) without imputation. The ablation study reveals that removing reverse edges drastically reduces AUROC by 0.1968±0.0073, and collapsing heterogeneous edge types into one relation (7x fewer parameters) outperforms the full model on all seeds.

**Background**: ICU mortality prediction relies on modeling irregular clinical observations across heterogeneous entity types. Traditional sequence models handle irregular sampling but ignore relational structure, while graph models often assume fixed-interval inputs. CT-HEG addresses this by representing ICU stays as continuous-time heterogeneous graphs, leveraging GATv2Conv, an improved graph attention layer that fixes the static attention problem of standard GAT.

## [Unifying Residual Connections and GeoNorm via Sphere Retractions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02668) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper shows that on a hypersphere, the entire family of retraction maps collapses to a single scalar design choice, unifying Euclidean residual connections and GeoNorm. It introduces Proj-SpheretNorm and Cay-SpheretNorm, which are norm-preserving and require only algebraic operations, and generalizes them into a one-parameter family, p-SpheretNorm. This unification provides a new theoretical framework for understanding and designing normalization and residual connection schemes in deep learning. It suggests that the exponential map, used by GeoNorm, is not optimal, and that finite p values can yield better performance, potentially improving training stability and convergence in large models like Transformers. The paper recovers the identity map and GeoNorm as limits at p=0 and p=∞, respectively, while Proj-SpheretNorm and Cay-SpheretNorm correspond to p=1 and p=2. Experiments on nanoGPT show that all three methods outperform existing lightweight connection schemes, with the best validation loss at finite p.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Residual connections are a standard technique in deep learning to stabilize training of deep networks. GeoNorm is a recent method that performs normalization on a Riemannian manifold, keeping hidden states on a hypersphere. Retraction maps are a general way to move from the tangent space back to the manifold, with the exponential map being one specific choice. The Cayley transform is a well-known algebraic mapping used in various mathematical contexts.

## [DenialRAG: New Single-Document RAG Poisoning Attack via Embedded Denial](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02678) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DenialRAG is a novel single-document poisoning attack on retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems that explicitly names the correct answer, denies it, and provides an attacker-controlled explanation to mislead the LLM. It was evaluated across three open-domain QA datasets, eight target LLMs from four vendors, and five inference-time defenses, showing model-dependent effectiveness. This attack highlights a significant security vulnerability in RAG systems, which are widely used to ground LLMs with external knowledge. By demonstrating that a single crafted document can effectively mislead multiple models, it underscores the need for robust defenses and careful deployment of RAG in security-sensitive applications. DenialRAG embeds the conflict between correct and poisoned answers directly into the retrieved passage, making it distinct from prior attacks that avoid explicit denial. The attack's success rate varies significantly across models; it achieved the highest ASR on all Mistral-7B datasets but was less effective on others, and defenses reduced ASR but left residual risk in some settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that enhances LLMs by retrieving relevant documents from an external corpus and incorporating them into the context. Corpus poisoning attacks exploit this by inserting malicious documents that, when retrieved, can steer the LLM's output. Prior single-document attacks typically avoided explicitly naming the correct answer, but DenialRAG takes a complementary approach by embedding a direct denial.

## [TraceCompiler: Mining LLM Agent Traces into Deterministic Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02680) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TraceCompiler is a novel system that mines clusters of noisy LLM agent traces and compiles them into executable, mostly deterministic workflows. It identifies producer-consumer dependencies with high precision (0.928) and recall (0.943) on the T1 dataset, and achieves 0.993 precision on AppWorld. This addresses a real problem in LLM agent efficiency and reliability: agents often repeat procedures and produce noisy traces. By compiling traces into deterministic workflows, TraceCompiler can reduce API calls (e.g., from 34 to 11 for a Venmo intent) and improve consistency, potentially impacting LLM agent tooling and deployment. The system classifies bindings as constants, user inputs, copied outputs, transforms, or residual LLM decisions. It only admits inter-tool dependencies when a consumer argument contains a value uniquely attributable to an earlier producer, and marks ambiguous relations as 'suspected' without imposing ordering constraints. The compiler also refuses to compile intents with under-determined irreversible side effects, as demonstrated with a Spotify/Todoist example.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often use tools and repeat procedures, generating traces that mix reusable structure with retries and exploration. Producer-consumer dependencies are a key concept in program analysis, where a 'def' (definition) reaches a 'use' if the value flows from one to another. TraceCompiler leverages this concept to extract deterministic workflows from noisy traces, similar to how def-use edges are used in compiler optimization.

## [Training Logs Can Sharpen Model Comparisons, But Selection Noise Looms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02705) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes using arm-specific covariate adjustment on training logs to reduce uncertainty in model comparisons, and empirically shows that simple adjustments often help, while broad covariate searches can be counterproductive. This work addresses a practical problem in machine learning evaluation, offering a way to make model comparisons more precise without extra computational cost. It could influence how researchers and practitioners report performance differences, especially in stochastic training settings. The study spans three architectures and three datasets in vision tasks. The main limitation is covariate selection: broadly searching the log pool for the most correlated statistic often adds more noise than it removes, even when useful statistics exist in hindsight.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Comparing stochastically trained models requires estimating both performance difference and its uncertainty from repeated runs. Traditional covariate adjustment uses pre-measured covariates, but training logs are produced during training, so the paper uses arm-specific adjustment where each model is adjusted only with its own run statistics. This approach aims to reduce uncertainty without biasing the reported effect, which remains the raw mean difference.

## [Unified 2D Framework Integrates LLMs for Lesion Detection, Segmentation, and Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02805) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a unified 2D lesion analysis framework that integrates LLM-based reasoning, bounding box detection, segmentation, and radiology report generation on the original DeepLesion dataset. It achieves a Dice score of 62.6% for segmentation, a 28.5% improvement over nnUNet, and BLEU_1 of 64.3% for report generation. This work addresses the challenging problem of lesion segmentation in the original DeepLesion dataset and demonstrates significant performance gains, potentially improving automated medical image analysis and report generation. It also highlights the integration of LLMs with traditional vision tasks, which could influence future research in medical imaging AI. The framework achieves mAP50 of 70.1% and mAP50-95 of 46.4% for detection, and BLEU_4 of 49.6%, METEOR of 34.7%, and ROUGE_L of 60.1% for report generation. The authors have released the implementation, dataset, and models on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: DeepLesion is a large-scale dataset of over 32,000 annotated lesions from CT images of 4,400 patients, released by the NIH Clinical Center. Segmentation performance is often measured using the Dice score, which quantifies the overlap between predicted and ground-truth segmentations. Object detection accuracy is commonly evaluated using mean Average Precision (mAP) at different Intersection-over-Union (IoU) thresholds, such as mAP50 and mAP50-95.

## [Vector-Symbolic Memory System for ACT-R Models Sociocultural Decision-Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02807) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel declarative memory system for the ACT-R cognitive architecture that uses a vector-symbolic autoencoder to represent semantic associations at multiple levels, encoding episodic memories differently from semantic memory vectors via HRR operations. The system is tested on ACT-R models of a racially contextualized implicit association test (IAT). This work addresses a significant gap in cognitive modeling by providing a mechanism to represent sociocultural influences on decision-making, which is often overlooked in traditional ACT-R models. It could enable more realistic simulations of human behavior in culturally diverse contexts, benefiting fields like cognitive science, AI, and social psychology. The system uses a simple HRR (Holographic Reduced Representation) operation to encode episodic memories separately from semantic memory vectors extracted from text, producing a final chunk activation for a memory request. The approach is validated using ACT-R models of a racially contextualized implicit association test (IAT), demonstrating its applicability to sociocultural tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: ACT-R is a cognitive architecture that simulates human cognition through a set of programmable mechanisms, often implemented in Common Lisp. Vector-symbolic architectures (VSAs) like HRR use high-dimensional vectors and operations like binding and superposition to represent structured knowledge. This paper integrates these ideas to model how sociocultural structures influence decision-making, addressing the challenge of representing multiple levels of semantic association.

## [CURV: Curriculum Visual Grounded Reasoning for Chart QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02833) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CURV, a curriculum learning framework that enhances chart understanding in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by reformulating chart question answering (CQA) as multi-step visual grounded reasoning. It also presents CCQA, a three-level curriculum dataset with scalable synthetic generation across diverse chart types and reasoning patterns. This work addresses a significant limitation in current MLLMs, which often struggle with accurate visual grounding and coherent reasoning chains in chart understanding. By internalizing visual reasoning with dynamic grounding, CURV achieves up to 20.50% improvements over baselines and generalizes to real-world benchmarks, potentially advancing applications in data analysis and document understanding. CURV coordinates logical reasoning with dynamic visual grounding through spatial attention concentration at each reasoning step. The curriculum progresses from basic single-operation reasoning to complex multi-chart compositional tasks, and experiments show generalizability to out-of-domain multimodal reasoning tasks with up to 10.20% improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Chart question answering (CQA) requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to integrate visual comprehension with logical reasoning. While extrinsic chain-of-thought prompting and visual cues can improve performance, current MLLMs lack intrinsic visual grounded reasoning capabilities, leading to inaccurate perception and reasoning disconnected from visual evidence. Curriculum learning, inspired by educational frameworks, structures training from simple to complex tasks, which is applied here to develop these capabilities.

## [LTGA: Learnable Tsallis Graph Attention with Per-Edge Index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02938) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LTGA (Learnable Tsallis Graph Attention), a graph attention layer with a learnable Tsallis entropic index q that interpolates between heavy-tailed, softmax, and compact-support attention at four granularities, from a global scalar to a per-edge index. Experiments show that learning q does not outperform a validation-tuned frozen grid, but it offers interpretability by pruning 42% of attention coefficients to zero. This work addresses the limitation of fixed softmax normalization in graph attention, which cannot adapt to both homophilic and heterophilic graphs. The finding that learning the Tsallis index does not beat a tuned grid is an important nuance for the graph neural network community, suggesting that search may be more effective than learning for this hyperparameter. LTGA-Edge achieves the best average rank (2.75) across eight benchmarks at ten seeds, but the omnibus test does not reject (p=0.199). A validation-tuned frozen grid reaches 61.4%, tuned α-entmax 62.2%, and a capacity-matched q≡1 control 62.0%, against 61.7% for LTGA-Edge; restoring the pruned edges costs 7.1 points, while random pruning costs 13.0 more.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Graph attention networks (GATs) typically use softmax to normalize attention scores, which is the maximum-entropy choice under Shannon statistics. However, homophilic graphs (where connected nodes tend to have similar labels) and heterophilic graphs (where connected nodes tend to have different labels) may require different attention shapes, such as sparse or heavy-tailed distributions. Tsallis entropy generalizes Shannon entropy with a parameter q, and α-entmax is a related sparse attention mechanism that can produce exactly zero attention weights.

## [Rubrics as Privileged Information Boost Open-Ended Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02948) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper extends on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) to open-ended generation by using rubrics as soft privileged information (PI), showing that soft rubric PI outperforms hard reference completions and rubric-as-reward RL on benchmarks like HealthBench and ResearchQA. This work challenges the conventional use of rubrics as scalar rewards, demonstrating that dense token-level supervision via soft PI provides a richer training signal. It could improve the efficiency and quality of fine-tuning LLMs for open-ended tasks, impacting areas like healthcare and scientific research. The method, RuPI, beats rubric-as-reward RL by up to +0.10 absolute score on HealthBench and outperforms reference-PI by +0.034 to +0.079 across three models. It also generalizes to the RubricHub Science corpus, achieving 66.6% on ResearchQA versus 64.2% for reference-PI and 57.6% for RaR RL.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) is a training method where a single model acts as both student and teacher, with the teacher conditioned on privileged information like ground-truth solutions. Privileged information (PI) is extra information available during training but not at inference, and rubrics are structured criteria used to evaluate responses. This paper applies soft PI in the form of rubrics to open-ended generation, where multiple valid responses exist.

## [ValueFormer: Causal Transformer for VLA Failure Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02958) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ValueFormer introduces a policy-agnostic causal transformer that emits dense per-frame value and binary mistake-detection signals in a single forward pass, improving failure detection in vision-language-action (VLA) policies. On a real-robot bimanual sandwich-assembly task, it lifts task completion from 70% to 85% and reduces live serving cost by 3-5 times. This work addresses the critical problem of silent failures in VLA policies, which is essential for reliable semi-autonomous systems. By providing dense, continuous signals for failure detection, it enables safer and more efficient robot operation in real-world scenarios. The model uses a frozen DINOv3 backbone and a compact causal transformer, with a stage-aware success-then-decay return for labeling failed episodes. It also supervises detection from mistake intervals, allowing recovered mistakes to carry signal, and uses a batched bf16 encoder to reduce serving cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies trained by behavior cloning often fail silently, as imitation lacks a notion of progress. Reinforcement learning could provide this, but it is impractical when real-robot experience is costly and environments resist simulation. A terminal success/failure bit is too sparse, so dense per-frame labels are needed. ValueFormer leverages a causal transformer, which respects temporal order, and a Monte Carlo value estimate to provide dense signals.

## [HyperFL: Query-Adaptive Fault Localization via Hypernetwork LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02967) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HyperFL introduces a query-adaptive representation learning framework for software fault localization, using a hypernetwork to generate query-specific LoRA parameters for the query encoder. It achieves up to 13.3% relative improvement in function-level MRR@10 and 16.7% in Hit@1 over the state-of-the-art SweRank method. This work addresses a key limitation of retrieval-based fault localization methods that use fixed query representations, which fail to handle the diversity of real-world issue reports. By enabling dynamic query adaptation, HyperFL improves localization accuracy, potentially accelerating automated debugging and program repair. HyperFL keeps the code encoder fixed and reusable while only adapting the query encoder via LoRA parameters generated by a lightweight hypernetwork. Experiments on a real-world issue localization benchmark show consistent improvements across multiple embedding backbones, and analysis reveals distinct adaptation patterns for different issue characteristics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Software fault localization is a crucial step in automated debugging and program repair, aiming to identify the code locations responsible for reported issues. Recent retrieval-based approaches treat this as a dense retrieval task, learning a shared embedding space between issue reports and source code. However, these methods often use a fixed query representation, which may not capture the diversity of real-world issue reports. HyperFL leverages hypernetworks, which generate weights for another network, and LoRA, a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique, to dynamically adapt query encodings.

The paper introduces TQLite, a novel distillation framework that uses a multi-LRM jury to generate synthetic training data, enabling small language models (SLMs) to approach the MQM evaluation performance of large reasoning models (LRMs). The study also provides an extensive empirical benchmark of SLMs, LLMs, and LRMs across various translation quality evaluation setups. This work addresses the scalability challenge of deploying large models for translation quality evaluation, offering a cost-effective alternative that maintains high performance. It is significant for NLP practitioners and organizations needing real-time, efficient evaluation without sacrificing accuracy. TQLite leverages a multi-LRM jury to aggregate evaluation responses from diverse models, combined with practical data curation techniques to generate high-quality synthetic data. The results show that SLMs trained via TQLite far exceed off-the-shelf SLM evaluation capabilities, though the paper is an arXiv preprint and impact may be limited to the translation evaluation niche.

Large reasoning models (LRMs) are a type of LLM trained for multi-step logical reasoning, showing superior performance on complex tasks. Knowledge distillation is a technique to compress large models into smaller ones while preserving performance, which is central to TQLite's approach.

## [V-FIND: Uncovering Sparse Forensic Neurons in Video Forgery Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces V-FIND, a framework that discovers sparse, functionally specialized neurons in video forgery detectors, and shows that activating these neurons with a lightweight linear classifier achieves strong detection performance across multiple benchmarks. This work offers a new perspective on video forgery detection by shifting from resource-intensive full-model retraining to exploiting intrinsic, sparse forensic knowledge, potentially enabling more efficient and interpretable detection systems. It also contributes to the broader understanding of how discriminative information is encoded in deep neural networks. V-FIND first localizes critical layers with pronounced real-forged discrepancies, then identifies latent anchor neurons that consistently carry forgery-discriminative signals, organizing them into a compact forensic subspace. The backbone remains frozen, and only a lightweight linear classifier is trained, yet the method delivers strong performance on external benchmarks for generated videos.

This work provides a novel interpretability lens for understanding LLM numerical reasoning, potentially guiding improvements in model architecture and prompting strategies for tasks involving sequential data. ## [DinoSplat-OV: Training-Free Open-Vocabulary Segmentation for Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03023) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DinoSplat-OV, a training-free framework that adapts DINOv3's image-text capabilities for open-vocabulary semantic segmentation in remote sensing, without fine-tuning or additional pretraining. It incorporates Text-aware Laplacian Propagation and Gaussian Splatting Upsampling to handle dense, multi-scale, and large imagery. This work fills a gap by extending DINO-series models to training-free open-vocabulary segmentation in remote sensing, a domain where pixel-level annotations are costly. It demonstrates competitive performance on benchmarks like UDD5, DOTA, and LoveDA, offering a viable path for scalable and annotation-efficient segmentation. The Text-aware Laplacian Propagation module combines textual semantic affinities with local visual similarity to denoise patch-level predictions, while the Gaussian Splatting Upsampling module reconstructs pixel-level features via RGB-guided anisotropic aggregation and test-time optimization. A global-anchor sliding-window strategy supports large-scale imagery.

## [PLAN: Parallel Liquid-Inspired Network for Efficient FJSP Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03041) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PLAN reformulates continuous liquid-state dynamics into a discretized and parallelizable formulation, enabling efficient representation learning for flexible job shop scheduling (FJSP). It reduces average makespan by up to 2.3% and inference latency by up to 31.7% across deterministic, stochastic, and dynamic FJSP benchmarks, using only 22-47% of baseline parameters. This work addresses a critical trade-off in DRL-based FJSP: attention-centric models are accurate but parameter-heavy and slow, while liquid neural networks are efficient but sequential. By making liquid dynamics parallelizable, PLAN offers a scalable and efficient backbone that could improve real-world scheduling optimization and extend to other combinatorial problems. PLAN decouples state evolution from context aggregation, using liquid-inspired updates for the primary state and a lightweight module for global context. It serves as a plug-and-play backbone, pairing with a stochastic module for stochastic FJSP and replacing heavy heterogeneous graph transformers in dynamic FJSP. The maximum inference latency reduction reaches 69.2% on the largest instances.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Flexible job shop scheduling (FJSP) is an NP-hard combinatorial optimization problem where each operation can be processed on any machine from a given set. Deep reinforcement learning (DRL) approaches often use attention-based architectures, which are accurate but suffer from high parameter counts and inference latency. Liquid neural networks (LNNs) offer parameter efficiency through dynamic, continuous-time state evolution, but their sequential nature limits computational efficiency. PLAN bridges this gap by discretizing and parallelizing liquid dynamics.
