## [CVPO: Value-Variance Adaptation and Dynamic Curriculum for LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03068) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CVPO introduces a curriculum-guided value-variance policy optimization method that adapts advantage estimates based on trajectory value-variance and dynamically weights questions by difficulty. It outperforms strong baselines like VAPO on various math tasks. This method addresses two key limitations in existing LLM RL methods: imprecise feedback on generated trajectories and problem difficulty drift. By improving exploration and learning efficiency, it could lead to more accurate and robust reasoning in LLMs, benefiting applications in math, coding, and other reasoning-heavy domains. The method uses token-level value-variance to quantify intrinsic randomness in generation, and theoretical analysis shows this variance bounds policy update magnitude. It also introduces a difficulty-aware dynamic curriculum weighting method that adapts to the model's current ability during training.

## [SynEnergy: Diffusion Framework Preserves Anomalies in Synthetic Energy Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03087) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SynEnergy introduces a two-stage diffusion-based framework that leverages heterogeneous graph-based anomaly semantics to generate synthetic energy consumption data while preserving rare anomalous events. The framework comprises HG-ASL for anomaly semantic learning and AS-Diff for anomaly-guided diffusion, achieving a 12.21% average improvement in anomaly preservation fidelity over 11 baselines. This work addresses a critical limitation in synthetic energy data generation—the underrepresentation of anomalous events—which is essential for applications like grid reliability and demand response. By enabling privacy-preserving data sharing without losing rare but important patterns, SynEnergy could improve the robustness of energy systems analytics and support better decision-making in the energy sector. The framework is evaluated on four real-world energy consumption datasets against 11 general-purpose and energy-specific baselines, showing a 2.96% improvement in downstream quality while maintaining competitive overall generation fidelity. The two-stage design allows controllable generation for individual regions and scales to city-wide settings, leveraging heterogeneous dependencies across geographical proximity and regional attributes.

Diffusion models are a class of generative models that iteratively denoise data to produce realistic samples, and heterogeneous graphs can model complex relationships among different types of entities. SynEnergy combines these techniques to explicitly preserve anomaly semantics during generation.

## [SMOPD: Specialize-and-Merge Online Policy Distillation for Multi-Reward RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03092) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SMOPD, a two-stage training method that first trains reward-specialized teachers using reward-priority configurations and then merges their capabilities into a single student policy via online policy distillation. Experiments show SMOPD outperforms GDPO across 1.5B, 3B, and 7B backbones in complementary and conflicting reward settings. SMOPD addresses a critical imbalance in multi-reward RL where sparse rewards provide insufficient optimization signals, potentially limiting model capabilities. By strengthening sparse reward signals without sacrificing dense reward learning, SMOPD could improve the alignment and performance of large language models in complex multi-objective tasks. SMOPD consists of two stages: Stage1-Specialize trains multiple teachers with reward-priority configurations to ensure each reward can drive optimization, and Stage2-Merge uses online policy distillation to combine their capabilities. The method is validated on complementary rewards (tool-calling accuracy and format) and conflicting rewards (helpful and harmless), outperforming GDPO across 1.5B, 3B, and 7B backbones.

Existing methods like GDPO normalize each reward separately to prevent masking, but still struggle when sparse rewards provide too little variation to influence the shared policy. SMOPD builds on the idea of policy distillation, where a student policy learns from teacher policies, to combine specialized capabilities.

FakeI2V-Bench introduces a large-scale benchmark with 97,548 videos to evaluate image-level deepfake detectors for video detection. The best image-level detector achieves 80.16% AUC, slightly outperforming the best video-level detector (79.99% AUC), and the proposed IV-Bridge framework boosts performance to 93.80% AUC. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in deepfake video detection by systematically assessing image-level detectors, which are often repurposed for video tasks. The benchmark includes videos from the latest generation models and covers a broad range of categories. IV-Bridge uses a random forest model with statistical features to aggregate frame-level predictions, enabling eleven image-level detectors to surpass state-of-the-art video-level approaches.

## [Adaptive Two-Stage Token Pruning Cuts Video-Language Model Latency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an adaptive two-stage visual token pruning strategy for video-language models, which first prunes redundant frames and then adaptively prunes tokens within retained frames based on content. The method achieves +7% accuracy on a video captioning benchmark at 10% token retention while reducing computation TFLOPs by 95%, without requiring additional training. This work addresses the critical challenge of high inference latency in video-language models, which limits their deployment on edge devices and in real-time applications. By exploiting temporal redundancies and content-dependent pruning, it offers a practical, training-free solution that could enable more efficient video understanding in resource-constrained environments. The method is entirely post-hoc, requiring no fine-tuning, and determines the second-stage pruning ratio by analyzing the correlation structure of token embeddings to quantify redundancy. It improves accuracy by +7% on a video captioning benchmark at 10% token retention, while reducing computation TFLOPs by 95%.

## [Uniform Liability Achieves Near-Optimal Outcomes in Medical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a principal-agent model showing that a uniform liability level for physicians deviating from standard care can achieve near-optimal outcomes when integrating AI, despite unobservable physician quality. The optimal liability is non-monotonic with AI accuracy, and welfare loss occurs only under specific conditions. This research provides practical insights for policymakers designing liability frameworks for AI-assisted medical decision-making, potentially reducing legal uncertainty and encouraging AI adoption. It bridges AI, healthcare, and economics, offering a simple policy that can achieve first-best outcomes in many cases. The model considers physicians with private quality information choosing among standard treatment, personalized judgment, or AI recommendation. The optimal liability either decreases monotonically or follows an inverted-U pattern as AI accuracy increases, depending on standard care reliability. Welfare loss arises only when standard care is unreliable and AI accuracy is low, with magnitude following an inverted U-shape.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: The principal-agent problem arises when an agent (physician) acts on behalf of a principal (social planner) with conflicting interests and asymmetric information. In medical AI, liability rules determine who bears responsibility for AI-related errors, affecting physician behavior and AI adoption. This paper applies economic theory to design optimal liability in this context.

## [DigitCode: Symbolic Hand Motion Tokenization via Anatomical Units](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03127) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DigitCode introduces a symbolic tokenization method for hand motion that organizes symbols along the anatomical hierarchy of bone, finger, and whole hand, reducing quantization error by three quarters compared to existing Hand Labanotation. The method also demonstrates that per-finger tokens serve as editable handles for repairing malformed generated hands and retargeting motions to robots. This work addresses the lack of structure in continuous hand motion representations like joint angles or MANO parameters, which hinder symbolic indexing and editing. By providing a discrete, hierarchical representation, DigitCode could improve hand generation, understanding, and robot learning, enabling tasks such as anatomically valid pose editing and cross-embodiment retargeting. The method builds on Hand Labanotation (HL), which represents hand motion as a T x 40 grid of direction symbols per bone, and adapts, groups, and layers HL's alphabet along the hand's unit hierarchy. The authors release HandTok, a reproducible testbed for comparing hand tokenizers unit-for-unit, and show that at a fixed unit, training-free and learned quantizers are interchangeable, while moving down the anatomical hierarchy improves accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Hand motion is crucial for human activity understanding, but current representations are mostly continuous, such as joint angles or MANO parameters, which are accurate but lack symbolic structure. Discrete symbolic representations like Hand Labanotation provide structure by writing motion as a grid of symbols, but the choice of anatomical unit (bone, finger, or whole hand) has been underexplored. Quantization error is a measure of information loss when converting continuous signals to discrete symbols, and reducing it improves fidelity.

## [Rectify-then-Diffuse: Training-Free Fix for Compositional Text-to-Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03135) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Rectify-then-Diffuse (RTD), a training-free framework that rectifies the initial concept allocation before standard denoising to improve compositional text-to-image generation. It proposes Soft-Overlap Disentanglement (SOD) and Isotropic Gradient Rectification (IGR) to achieve state-of-the-art compositional fidelity. This work addresses a critical limitation in text-to-image diffusion models: their tendency to omit or incorrectly merge concepts when generating images with multiple concepts. By offering a training-free solution, RTD can be easily integrated into existing models, potentially improving compositional generation across the ecosystem without additional training costs. RTD treats compositional generation as a boundary-condition problem, rectifying the initial allocation once before denoising. On the AE-Bench object pair subset, RTD improves BLIP-VQA by 45.8% and ImageReward by 19.6% over CO3 while running 2.3× faster. Code will be released at the provided GitHub link.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image diffusion models, such as Stable Diffusion and Imagen, generate images from textual descriptions by iteratively denoising a random latent representation. Compositional generation refers to the ability to correctly generate images with multiple objects, attributes, and relationships as described in the prompt. Existing models often struggle with this, leading to omitted or merged concepts. Training-free methods aim to improve model behavior without additional training, often by modifying the inference process.

The paper introduces StructPO, a struct-aware policy learning framework that internalizes multi-stage academic writing workflows into a single-pass LLM policy using explicit stage tokens. It achieves improved semantic alignment, structural rationality, and inference efficiency over workflow-based baselines, and remains competitive with GPT-5.1 in human evaluation when scaled to Qwen3-32B. This work addresses the high cost and cross-stage drift of external multi-stage prompting or agent workflows for academic writing, offering a more efficient and coherent alternative. It demonstrates that fine-grained policy optimization can internalize complex workflows, potentially impacting LLM applications in structured generation tasks beyond academic writing. StructPO introduces struct-aware credit assignment to decouple local stage quality from global coherence, and refinement-guided optimization to internalize revision behavior into the first-pass policy. Experiments show generalization to out-of-domain settings and competitive performance with GPT-5.1 when scaled to Qwen3-32B.

This paper introduces a lightweight chunk selection method for mobile retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) that combines question hidden states, Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) routing signals, and chunk embeddings to select the most evidence-supporting chunk. The method improves rank-1 evidence selection by an average of 2.5% over mobile-applicable baselines. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying RAG on resource-constrained mobile devices, where reducing context size without sacrificing factual accuracy is essential. By leveraging internal LLM signals and MoE routing, it offers a parameter-efficient solution that could enable more reliable on-device AI assistants and edge computing applications. The selector uses a compact multilayer perceptron to map combined features to an evidence prototype in the chunk embedding space, selecting the candidate with highest cosine similarity. An optional task-aware feature selection strategy reduces input dimensions for stricter budgets, and the paper constructs semantic chunk-correctness labels based on evidence sufficiency rather than answer-string containment.

## [EFX Allocations Always Exist for Hypergraphs with Girth at Least 4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03171) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that for hypergraphs with girth at least 4 and agents with general monotone valuations, an EFX allocation always exists and can be constructed in polynomial time. It also extends the result to multi-hypergraphs under a specific condition, with a pseudo-polynomial time construction. This result addresses a major open problem in fair division by providing a positive answer for a broad class of hypergraphs, extending previous work on graphs. It offers a polynomial-time algorithm, which is significant for algorithmic game theory and practical applications in resource allocation. The paper builds on the model introduced by Christodoulou et al. (2023), where agents and goods are vertices and edges of a (multi)hypergraph, and only endpoints of an edge have non-zero marginal value. For multi-hypergraphs, the condition requires a vertex whose incident edges have multiplicity at most the edge size minus 2, and the construction runs in pseudo-polynomial time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: EFX (envy-free up to any good) is a fairness criterion for allocating indivisible goods, where no agent envies another after removing any single good from the other's bundle. The existence of EFX allocations is a major open problem even for additive valuations. Hypergraphs generalize graphs by allowing edges to connect more than two vertices, and girth measures the length of the shortest cycle. This paper focuses on hypergraphs with girth at least 4, which avoids certain problematic structures.

## [SRG: Self-Supervised Representation-Guided Generative Dataset Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03218) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SRG, a framework that integrates self-supervised representation geometry into generative dataset distillation, using SSL-space objectives and a stage-wise diffusion guidance strategy to produce synthetic datasets that better preserve discriminative features for frozen pretrained encoders. This addresses a gap in dataset distillation, which traditionally targets randomly initialized networks, while modern vision systems often rely on frozen pretrained encoders. By improving the utility of distilled datasets for such encoders, SRG could enhance efficiency in downstream tasks and broaden the applicability of dataset distillation in real-world scenarios. SRG constructs class-wise prototypes from real-image SSL representations and uses three SSL-space objectives: prototype alignment, inter-class discrimination, and intra-class assignment. During diffusion sampling, it employs a stage-wise guidance strategy: early denoising anchors to the latent of the real image whose SSL representation is nearest to the assigned prototype, while later denoising is guided by SSL-space objectives, balancing visual realism and class discriminability.

**Background**: Dataset distillation compresses a large training set into a compact synthetic set while retaining utility. Generative dataset distillation leverages pre-trained generative models to synthesize samples, but existing methods often ignore the representation geometry of pretrained encoders. Self-supervised learning (SSL) learns representations without labels, and the geometry of these representations can be used to guide generation. Diffusion models generate data by iteratively denoising, and guidance can steer the process toward desired properties.

## [ProtoBlend: Efficient Video Dataset Distillation via Cluster-Guided Prototype Blending](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03269) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ProtoBlend introduces a construction-based framework for video dataset distillation that avoids gradient-based optimization of stored videos, instead using teacher-guided temporal clip selection, cluster-guided prototype allocation, and prototype blending with mixture-source supervision. This approach achieves competitive accuracy-efficiency trade-offs on four trimmed action-recognition benchmarks. This work addresses the high computational cost of existing video dataset distillation methods, which are amplified by the temporal dimension. By eliminating iterative optimization, ProtoBlend could make video distillation more scalable and practical for efficient machine learning applications. ProtoBlend operates in three stages: teacher-guided temporal clip selection retains high-confidence segments, cluster-guided prototype allocation partitions clips in the teacher feature space and assigns one distilled slot per intra-class cluster, and each prototype is blended with an in-cluster anchor while teacher predictions are combined with the same coefficient for supervision. The method is evaluated on four trimmed action-recognition benchmarks, demonstrating competitive performance without iterative optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Dataset distillation aims to compress a large dataset into a compact surrogate set that preserves training utility. Existing video dataset distillation methods typically synthesize condensed videos through iterative optimization, which is computationally expensive due to the temporal dimension. ProtoBlend explores a construction-based approach, leveraging prototype learning and clustering to build distilled videos without gradient-based optimization.

## [Test-Time Scaling for Safe Text-to-Image Generation via Clean Estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03284) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a test-time safety method for text-to-image diffusion models that detects prohibited content using intermediate clean estimates and intervenes with low-rank residual optimization, avoiding costly retraining. It demonstrates superior performance on Stable Diffusion v1.4 and v3.5 for nudity removal, IP protection, and style erasure. This approach addresses a critical challenge in AI safety by providing a scalable, weight-preserving solution that does not compromise model capabilities. It could enable safer deployment of text-to-image models in real-world applications, reducing the risk of generating harmful or infringing content. The method uses a sparse margin objective on intermediate clean estimates for detection and optimizes a structured low-rank residual in the text-conditioning space via truncated backpropagation for intervention. It keeps non-violating inference latency nearly unchanged as the maximum budget increases, offering flexibility via test-time scaling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image diffusion models like Stable Diffusion generate images from text prompts but can be misused to produce prohibited content. Training-based unlearning methods are effective but computationally expensive and can harm general capabilities. Test-time adaptation (TTA) is a paradigm where models adapt at inference time without retraining, which this work leverages for safety.

## [Detecting LLM Reasoning Failures via CoT Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03291) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel method to detect reasoning failures in large language models (LLMs) by analyzing the dynamics of chain-of-thought (CoT) traces, rather than the semantic correctness of individual steps. The approach successfully identifies failure patterns and, with a targeted prompt intervention, improves Llama3-70B accuracy on SAT problems from 13.3% to 85%. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM interpretability and safety by focusing on the dynamics of reasoning traces, which are often unfaithful to internal computations. It offers a scalable way to monitor and correct reasoning failures, potentially improving model reliability in complex tasks. The study uses Boolean satisfiability (SAT) problems with variable complexity to compare successful and failed reasoning across multiple LLMs. It identifies 'premature verification collapse' on SAT problems and presumptuous SAT conclusions on UNSAT problems, and demonstrates that a proof-search prompt intervention corrects 84.6% of errors in Llama3-70B.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning is a technique where LLMs generate step-by-step explanations, which can improve performance and provide an interface to the model's reasoning. However, verbalized CoTs may not faithfully reflect internal reasoning, and existing monitoring methods often focus on individual step correctness. This paper explores the dynamics of CoT traces—how the reasoning process evolves—as a more robust signal for detecting failures.

## [LLM Trade-offs in Enterprise Automation: Workflow Generation Lessons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03311) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper evaluates six LLMs for AI-driven workflow generation in enterprise automation, comparing monolithic and piecewise pipelines across 29 scenarios with 2,784 runs. The piecewise pipeline improved structural success rates from 31.5-82.8% to 74.1-97.8% across all models. This work demonstrates that smaller, cost-effective models can achieve production viability when using piecewise decomposition, reducing reliance on expensive frontier models. It provides practical insights for deploying LLM-based automation in compliance-driven enterprise environments. The piecewise pipeline decomposes workflow construction into variable scaffolding, base block assembly, and nested block generation. Mistral-small achieved 95.7% structural success at USD 0.01 per workflow, while mistral-medium-2505 and gpt-oss-120b reached 96.1% and 97.8% respectively, with mistral-medium-2505 carrying a 19x cost premium.

Traditional static orchestrators struggle with event-driven assessments that demand automation code to adapt in seconds. This paper addresses the challenge of generating automation workflows using LLMs, balancing structural validity (JSON schema and UI rendering) with semantic correctness (logical fulfillment of user intent).

## [Conditioning-Availability Bias in Echocardiographic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03342) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies a conditioning-availability bias in phase-conditioned echocardiographic segmentation, where models trained with clean oracle phase signals fail when deployed with estimated or noisy phase signals. It introduces a complementary gap pair metric to measure this bias and proposes mitigation strategies such as deployment-aware checkpoint selection and phase perturbation. This work highlights a critical yet underexplored issue in medical imaging AI: the mismatch between training conditions and real-world deployment, which can lead to significant performance degradation in clinical settings. The findings have direct implications for the safe deployment of conditional segmentation models in echocardiography and other medical imaging tasks. On held-out CAMUS data, one strong-cyclic, oracle-selected run fails severely with estimated phase, while sensitivity to incorrect phase persists across three runs. On EchoNet-Dynamic, the current estimator remains usable, but random-phase testing reveals strong latent sensitivity. The proposed mitigation strategies reduce both gaps with little change in mean Dice, and a downstream EF audit shows that recovering segmentation does not necessarily recover EF error or signed bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Conditional segmentation models use auxiliary signals, such as cardiac phase, to improve segmentation accuracy. In practice, these signals are often estimated from the data, but models may be trained and evaluated with cleaner 'oracle' signals, leading to a distribution shift at deployment. This paper studies this shortcut learning and auxiliary-variable shift in the context of echocardiographic segmentation, using datasets like CAMUS and EchoNet-Dynamic.

## [Online Learning Shapes Wind-Tunnel Airflow for UAV Soaring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03378) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced an online learning algorithm that combines a simplified physical model with iterative, measurement-based learning to control airflow in a multi-fan vertical wind tunnel. The method efficiently converges to desired airflow distributions, including uniform, Gaussian, and parabolic profiles, and can generate a profile specifically designed for passive soaring. This work provides a practical tool for testing and developing aerial robots in controlled environments with tailored airflow, which is crucial for advancing UAV capabilities. The demonstrated enhancement of passive soaring performance could lead to more energy-efficient drones and broader adoption of soaring techniques in real-world applications. The algorithm operates with a varying number of fans, highlighting its robustness and practical utility. It achieves sample-efficient convergence, meaning it requires relatively few measurements to learn the airflow dynamics, which is essential for real-time control in dynamic environments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Wind tunnels are essential for testing aerial vehicles, but traditional designs often provide uniform airflow, limiting their ability to simulate complex real-world conditions. Multi-fan wind tunnels offer greater flexibility by allowing independent control of each fan, but controlling the resulting complex airflow field is challenging. Online learning control algorithms adapt in real-time based on measurements, making them suitable for such tasks. Passive soaring, where an aircraft extracts energy from atmospheric currents without active propulsion, is a promising technique for extending UAV endurance.

## [Distilled Roads: Continual Adaptation for Generalizable Road Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03407) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a continual adaptation framework for road network extraction that combines cross-resolution knowledge distillation, multi-sensor training, and topology-aware supervision. The model achieves state-of-the-art results on City-Scale and Global-Scale benchmarks, outperforming existing methods by up to 22 F1 points and 15 APLS points, while being 3× faster in inference. This work addresses a critical challenge in geospatial AI: the poor generalization of road extraction models across different sensors, resolutions, and geographic regions. By demonstrating that targeted training strategies can outperform complex architectures, it offers a practical path toward more robust and efficient remote sensing models, benefiting applications in urban planning, disaster response, and autonomous navigation. The framework uses a resolution-decreasing curriculum for cross-resolution knowledge distillation, enabling a single model to handle imagery from 0.3 to 1.0 meters resolution across multiple satellite platforms. The model is evaluated on publicly available benchmarks, including City-Scale and Global-Scale, and demonstrates superior efficiency with 3× faster inference compared to state-of-the-art methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Road network extraction from satellite imagery is a fundamental task in remote sensing, but models often fail when applied to new regions or sensor types due to domain shifts. Traditional approaches require retraining or fine-tuning, which is costly and risks catastrophic forgetting. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a high-resolution teacher to a low-resolution student, while continual adaptation allows models to learn new domains without forgetting previous ones. Topology-aware supervision, such as using centerline and connectivity losses, helps preserve road network connectivity in predictions.

## [PRIME: Closed-Loop Framework for Robust Multimodal Intent Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03475) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PRIME, a closed-loop framework that diagnoses, restores, and reassesses modality reliability for multimodal intent recognition. It explicitly models modality weakness via contextual log-variance and uses a prototype-conditioned variational restoration module to repair degraded representations. This work addresses a critical gap in multimodal learning by enabling models to not only identify unreliable modalities but also attempt to restore them, potentially improving robustness in real-world scenarios with noisy or missing data. It could influence future research on adaptive fusion and reliability estimation in multimodal systems. PRIME estimates modality weakness using predictive confidence, epistemic disagreement, cross-modal consensus, and feature degeneracy, trained with controlled corruption and a heteroscedastic uncertainty objective. After restoration, reliability is re-estimated to decide if the repaired modality can contribute to inverse-variance fusion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal intent recognition integrates linguistic, acoustic, and visual cues, but modalities can be noisy, missing, or conflicting. Existing methods often reweight or suppress unreliable inputs without attempting to repair them. PRIME introduces a closed-loop approach that not only diagnoses but also restores and reassesses modality quality, using a variational module and precision-weighted fusion.

## [EPIK: System-Level Observations Improve Bayesian Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03489) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EPIK, a novel approach that uses system-level observations to inform Bayesian learning of model parameters for quantitative verification, addressing the challenge of inaccurate prior knowledge. EPIK formulates a twofold optimization problem to derive distributions of unknown transition parameters and embeds them to verify elusive properties. This work enhances the accuracy and robustness of quantitative verification, which is crucial for analyzing software reliability and response time. By reducing dependence on domain-expert prior knowledge, EPIK makes verification more applicable to real-world systems where such knowledge is limited or unreliable. EPIK leverages system-level properties that are directly observable and linked to real-world semantics, unlike existing methods that require prior knowledge on formal model transition parameters. The experimental evaluation uses multiple real-world case studies and diverse EPIK instantiations, demonstrating its effectiveness, flexibility, and generality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Quantitative verification analyzes properties like reliability and response time in software systems using formal models with probabilistic or timing aspects. Bayesian learning is combined with verification to estimate model parameters, but the accuracy heavily depends on prior knowledge, which often comes from domain expertise and can be inaccurate or uninformative. EPIK addresses this by using observable system-level properties to inform the learning process, reducing reliance on potentially flawed priors.

## [ALDA: Active Learning Deployment Advisor for Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03511) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ALDA, a framework that selects active learning strategies during a pilot phase by fitting parametric learning curves, predicting annotation costs, and estimating clinical performance. Experiments on four medical imaging datasets show ALDA can identify the deployment-optimal method using only 15-30% of the intended budget, reducing annotation costs by up to 82% compared to poor strategy choices. This work addresses a critical practical gap in active learning for medical imaging: choosing a sampling strategy before the full annotation budget is spent. By providing a risk-aware decision layer, ALDA can help practitioners avoid costly mistakes and make efficient use of limited expert annotations, potentially accelerating the deployment of AI in clinical settings. ALDA introduces a 'deployment window' metric that quantifies the sensitivity of cost estimates to uncertainty in the clinical threshold. The framework is model-agnostic and does not propose a new sampling heuristic, but rather acts as a decision layer on top of existing strategies.

**Background**: Active learning (AL) is a machine learning approach that aims to reduce labeling costs by selecting the most informative data points for annotation. In medical imaging, expert annotations are expensive and time-consuming, making AL particularly attractive. However, choosing the right sampling strategy is challenging, as the optimal choice depends on the dataset and clinical performance requirements. ALDA addresses this by using a pilot phase to predict which strategy will be most cost-effective.

It could guide future research and practice, improving accountability and safety in AI deployment. The paper distinguishes white, grey, and black box access and shows how each changes what can be collected, preserved, analyzed, and reported. It also identifies open research problems, including black box preservation, model version attestation, uncertainty quantification for surrogate-based analysis, and chain of custody for mutable AI artifacts.

PCTP can be integrated into existing models and improves prediction accuracy on Argoverse I and II datasets, with PCTP+QCNet outperforming all published ensemble-free methods on the Argoverse II leaderboard. PCTP decouples long-term prediction into two processes: pivot prediction using global map context and agent interactions, and pivot-based refinement focusing on local map details. Experiments show minimal impact on model size, and the method is compatible with most state-of-the-art trajectory prediction models.

DiagChain introduces five complementary metrics to assess distinct stages of the reconstruction process and supports systematic failure diagnosis. Evaluations using 6 LLMs show that smaller models struggle with incorporating retrieved evidence, while larger models face bottlenecks in correctly ordering evidence.

## [MuEvo: LLM-Driven Evolution of Multi-Heuristic Ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03636) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MuEvo is a novel LLM-driven framework that evolves heuristic ensembles for combinatorial optimization, introducing Dynamic Component Management and LLM-Driven Co-Evolution to handle multiple interacting components under ensemble-level feedback. This addresses a critical gap in LLM-AHD methods that typically optimize a single heuristic, potentially improving performance across various combinatorial optimization domains and advancing automated algorithm design. MuEvo combines short-budget probing and a reversible lifecycle for dynamic component management, and uses Multi-Ensemble Evaluation, Cross-Component Information Sharing, Relation-Guided Pair Evolution, and Adaptive Budget Allocation for co-evolution. It was evaluated on selection hyper-heuristics and componentized ant colony optimization across four domains, consistently outperforming human-designed frameworks and multi-component extensions of state-of-the-art LLM-AHD methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model-based automated heuristic design (LLM-AHD) uses LLMs to generate heuristics for combinatorial optimization problems. Traditional methods often focus on a single heuristic, but practical optimization frameworks rely on multiple interacting components. MuEvo extends this by evolving ensembles of heuristics, addressing challenges like early component selection and inter-component dependencies.

## [Decoupling Generation and Selection for Budget-Constrained Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03655) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a modular generation-and-selection framework for sentence-budget-constrained summarization, where a pretrained generator produces multiple candidate summaries that are decomposed into sentence-level candidates, and a combinatorial selector constructs the final summary under explicit constraints. The framework supports MMR, ILP, and a DPP-inspired log-determinant objective without retraining the generator. This approach offers a model-agnostic mechanism to improve factual grounding and source-grounding in summarization, which is crucial for real-world applications where budget constraints and factuality are important. It shows consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks, especially for multi-document summarization, potentially influencing future summarization system designs. The framework was evaluated on CNN/DailyMail, Multi-News, FaithBench, and TofuEval, showing consistent improvements in factuality and source-grounding metrics, but at the cost of lower reference-overlap scores. Human evaluation indicated higher perceived consistency, relevance, clarity, and conciseness, with a small reduction in coherence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Abstractive summarization models often suffer from factual inconsistency, redundancy, and weak length control. Traditional approaches like MMR (Maximal Marginal Relevance) and ILP (Integer Linear Programming) have been used for extractive summarization, while DPP (Determinantal Point Processes) provide diversity-based selection. This work decouples generation from selection, allowing a pretrained generator to propose candidates and a separate selector to optimize for budget constraints and factuality.

## [New Benchmark Reveals Pattern Bias in Multimodal Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03691) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first benchmark for visual pattern-completion bias in multimodal code generation, testing how repeated UI patterns affect MLLMs on a screenshot-to-code fill-in-the-blank task. Evaluating five frontier MLLMs on 1,440 screenshots, they found mean bias rates of 69.78% for card-width and 80.22% for text font-size perturbations, with accuracy only 21.17% and 7.89% respectively. This research highlights a concrete failure mode in multimodal code generation tools, which are increasingly used to convert screenshots into front-end code. The high bias rates in frontier models suggest that these tools may produce visually incorrect but pattern-consistent outputs, impacting developers and designers who rely on them, and underscoring the need for bias mitigation in AI-assisted coding. The benchmark was built from 30 webpages from the Design2Code dataset, creating 1,440 evaluated screenshots covering structural card and text-style patterns under standard and noise-overlaid conditions. Codex-5.3 performed best but still dropped from 68.61% accuracy on cards to 13.89% on text, while Flash-3.0 reached 96.11% bias on text; noise, subtler perturbations, and boundary positions increased bias, and reasoning analysis showed that greater reasoning effort correlates with lower bias, yet models can identify anomalies and still override them.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) combine large language models with visual understanding, enabling tasks like screenshot-to-code generation. However, these models can exhibit biases based on repeated patterns in training data, leading to outputs that favor common patterns over actual visual content. The Design2Code dataset is a resource for training and evaluating models on converting web page screenshots into code.

## [LiLa-WAM: Lightweight Latent World-Action Model for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03701) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LiLa-WAM is a lightweight world-action model that reasons in a compact latent space and can be trained end-to-end on a single 24GB GPU. It introduces the Visual Transition Token (VTT), a language-free task representation, and achieves 90.48% success across 50 RoboTwin tasks. This work addresses the computational overhead of existing world-action models, making them more accessible to researchers with limited GPU resources. It could accelerate progress in robotic manipulation by enabling efficient training and deployment. The model's compact latent reasoning space is jointly shaped by future-state prediction and action generation, keeping it lightweight while aligned with control. Experiments on RoboTwin 2.0, LIBERO, and real-robot tasks demonstrate its effectiveness, with single-GPU training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: World-action models (WAMs) are a robotics AI paradigm that jointly predict future world states and robot actions, often using video pretraining. Existing WAMs often require substantial computational resources, either due to pixel-space reasoning or multi-stage training. Latent-space reasoning, as explored in LLMs like Coconut, offers a more efficient alternative by representing thoughts in a continuous space. LiLa-WAM applies this concept to robotic manipulation, enabling end-to-end training on a single GPU.

## [MDLMPE: Distribution-Aware Positional Encoding for Masked Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03769) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MDLMPE, the first positional encoding method explicitly designed for masked diffusion language models (MDLMs). It represents token availability as a binary sequence, applies distance-aware Gaussian weighting, and projects through a cosine basis to modulate RoPE phases, improving performance on LLaDA and DREAM. MDLMPE addresses a fundamental gap in masked diffusion language models, which generate text in parallel and have dynamic revealed/masked token configurations that conventional positional encodings like RoPE ignore. By making positional representations aware of this evolving structure, it could improve the quality and efficiency of diffusion-based language generation, potentially narrowing the performance gap with autoregressive models. MDLMPE adds distribution-aware positional features to token embeddings and uses a lightweight MLP to map them to angular offsets that modulate standard RoPE phases. Extensive experiments on LLaDA and DREAM show it generally outperforms conventional positional encoding methods across supervised fine-tuning, pretraining, zero-shot evaluation, and block-diffusion settings, with ablations confirming the complete combination yields the strongest results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion language models (MDLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising a sequence where some tokens are masked, allowing parallel generation and bidirectional context. Unlike autoregressive models that expose a contiguous prefix, MDLM decoding produces dynamic, non-contiguous configurations of revealed and masked tokens. Traditional positional encodings like RoPE capture sequence order and pairwise displacement but are insensitive to this token-availability structure. MDLMPE is designed to incorporate this evolving availability distribution into positional representations.

## [Autoreflection: How Agentic Strange Loops Turn Human Culture into AI Infrastructure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03800) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces 'autoreflection' as a mechanism to explain recursive agentic loops in LLM-based agents without invoking consciousness, validated through analysis of 290,251 posts and 1.8 million comments from the first twelve days of Moltbook, an AI agent social platform. This concept provides a behavioral framework for understanding and evaluating increasingly autonomous AI agents, potentially influencing agent design and safety. It also highlights how agents repurpose human cultural artifacts as operational infrastructure, raising questions about cultural impact and governance. The study identifies four criteria for autoreflection and presents case studies of three agents with machine signatures ruling out human puppeteering. It finds agents redeploying Islamic hadith scholarship as security protocols and the Ship of Theseus as an operating model for identity continuity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based agents operate in loops where they read and edit their own configuration files, externalizing identity and memory. This architecture enables autoreflection, where the system observes its conditions and incorporates results back into its configuration. Moltbook is a social platform exclusively for AI agents, launched in January 2026, with a reverse CAPTCHA to filter out humans.

## [UHP Detection: LVLMs Show Unique Hallucination Patterns in Consistency Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03817) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces UHP Detection, a fully black-box framework that models hallucination in Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) as a structured uncertainty pattern across two axes: perturbation modality (image vs. text) and logical polarity (statement vs. negation). This framework outperforms existing black-box and white-box baselines on AMBER and PhD benchmarks, achieving up to +18.72% AUC-ROC and +20.07% AUC-PR improvements. This work addresses a critical limitation in existing hallucination detection methods, which rely on a single consistency metric and fail to capture the diverse manifestations of uncertainty. By introducing a structured pattern, it improves the reliability of LVLMs, which is essential for their deployment in real-world multimodal applications. The framework extracts both within-group and between-group features from four complementary consistency groups, which are then used to train a lightweight classifier. Cross-dataset evaluation shows that the learned pattern generalizes across benchmarks, indicating that hallucination behavior reflects a model-specific consistency pattern. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/amirezzati/uhpdet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) integrate visual and textual information but often hallucinate, producing outputs not grounded in visual evidence. Existing black-box detection methods estimate uncertainty via a single consistency metric, which is insufficient to capture the varied uncertainty manifestations. This paper proposes a structured approach that considers both perturbation modality and logical polarity, providing a more comprehensive uncertainty characterization.

## [FlowForm: Physics-Informed Satellite Flood Synthesis with New Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03822) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FlowForm introduces a physics-informed generative framework for synthesizing realistic satellite flood imagery, integrating shallow water equation constraints and terrain-aware conditioning. The authors also curate FloodScape, a large-scale, high-resolution dataset of paired pre- and post-disaster satellite images. This work addresses the critical scarcity of paired flood satellite imagery, which hampers the development of robust flood assessment models. By improving the realism and consistency of synthetic flood images, FlowForm could enhance data augmentation for flood detection and disaster response, benefiting remote sensing and AI-for-good communities. The framework includes a Flood Descriptor Module (FDM) that imposes differentiable penalties on residuals of the steady-state Shallow Water Equation in auxiliary latent fields at the diffusion bottleneck, and a Terrain Anchor Adapter (TAA) that injects depth, semantic, and edge features at four encoder scales of the U-Net. Evaluations cover standard image-generation metrics, consistency of flooded regions, zero-shot generalization to a geographically held-out flood event, and sensitivity to individual components.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Shallow water equations are a set of partial differential equations that model free-surface flows, commonly used in flood modeling. Physics-informed generative models integrate physical laws into deep learning to ensure outputs adhere to governing equations, which is valuable for synthesizing realistic satellite imagery where physical plausibility matters. FloodScape is a new dataset that provides paired pre- and post-disaster satellite images, addressing the data scarcity in flood-specific image generation.

## [SAFT: Structure-Aware Fine-Tuning Improves VLM Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03875) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Structure-Aware Fine-Tuning (SAFT), a self-supervised method that uses structural priors to denoise VLM-based reward signals in reinforcement learning. SAFT applies LoRA adapters to regularize the VLM's latent space, leading to faster policy convergence and improved alignment (measured by EPIC distance). Reward engineering is a major bottleneck in RL, and VLM-based reward models often produce noisy signals. SAFT offers a scalable, self-supervised alternative to human preference annotation, potentially accelerating progress in text-conditioned RL and benefiting the broader RL and VLM communities. SAFT is evaluated across a spectrum of base model capabilities, consistently denoising the reward landscape. The method suggests that failures in VLM reward models often stem from structural brittleness rather than semantic misunderstanding, highlighting the value of incorporating task structure as an inductive bias.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) often requires a reward function, which is typically hand-crafted or learned from human feedback, both costly. Recent work uses vision-language models (VLMs) as zero-shot reward models, computing text-observation similarity to specify tasks via natural language. However, these rewards can be noisy and unreliable. EPIC distance is a metric used to evaluate the similarity between reward functions, invariant to transformations that preserve optimal policies.

## [CARE-X: A Unified Chest X-Ray VLM with Auxiliary Supervision and Reward-Aligned Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03890) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CARE-X is a novel chest X-ray vision-language model that integrates auxiliary discriminative supervision (focal-loss classification and composite-loss grounding heads) with reward-aligned generation via Decoupled Clip and Dynamic Sampling Policy Optimization (DAPO). It achieves state-of-the-art performance on most metrics across four report-generation benchmarks, 94.0% VQA accuracy on ReXVQA, and near-parity with dedicated detection heads for spatial grounding. This work addresses a critical gap in medical imaging AI by unifying classification, localization, and report generation in a single model, which is what radiologists actually need. The demonstrated improvements in report quality and measurement accuracy could lead to more clinically useful AI systems that assist rather than replace human experts. CARE-X augments its generative backbone with auxiliary heads co-trained with the language-modeling objective, showing that structured prediction and generation reinforce each other. For measurement-dependent diagnoses, it couples Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct with native tool-calling to invoke deterministic measurement tools, yielding +43.6 pp average F1 over perception-only baselines across five conditions.

Auxiliary supervision, such as focal loss, helps models focus on hard examples, while reward-aligned learning (e.g., RLHF or DAPO) optimizes for specific quality metrics. Tool-augmented inference allows models to call external deterministic functions, improving accuracy in tasks like anatomical measurement.

## [TurnSight: Turn-Level Hindsight Self-Distillation for Tool-Integrated Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04007) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TurnSight introduces a turn-level hindsight self-distillation framework that derives supervision from execution-conditioned hindsight and uses cross-horizon directional agreement to select reliable signals, improving credit assignment in tool-integrated reasoning for LLMs. This addresses a key limitation of trajectory-level supervision in long-horizon tool-integrated reasoning, potentially improving the efficiency and effectiveness of RL fine-tuning for LLMs in complex tasks. It could benefit applications like autonomous agents and multi-step problem solving. The framework constructs multiple hindsight views with different lookahead horizons and selects reliable supervision via cross-horizon directional agreement. The selected hindsight signal is normalized across sibling rollouts and used to adaptively modulate RL advantages while preserving the original optimization direction. Experiments on three benchmarks demonstrate effectiveness, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-Integrated Reasoning (TIR) allows LLMs to solve complex tasks by interacting with external tools iteratively. Reinforcement learning (RL) methods often use trajectory-level supervision, which is insufficient for fine-grained credit assignment in long-horizon tasks. Self-distillation provides denser signals by using teacher branches with privileged context, but existing methods may not reflect the actual states visited by the agent. TurnSight addresses this by deriving supervision from execution-conditioned hindsight and using cross-horizon agreement to ensure reliability.

## [OR-Agent: Multi-Agent Framework for Automated Heuristic Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13769) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OR-Agent introduces a tree-based, multi-agent framework with hierarchical reflection to improve automated heuristic design in optimization problems. It outperforms strong evolutionary search baselines on classical combinatorial optimization problems and cooperative driving scenarios. This framework addresses a known limitation in LLM-based evolutionary methods by incorporating structured research and adaptive learning, potentially improving efficiency in automated algorithm discovery. It could impact optimization research and AI-driven heuristic design across various domains. The framework models heuristic search as a tree-based workflow with branching hypothesis generation and systematic backtracking. It introduces a hierarchical reflection system where short-term reflections act as verbal gradients, long-term reflections as verbal momentum, and memory compression as semantic weight decay.

The paper introduces ScoutGPT, a NanoGPT-based transformer that models football match events as language tokens and uses Monte Carlo sampling for counterfactual player valuation. This work addresses a key limitation in football recruitment by enabling counterfactual simulations that account for tactical context, potentially improving transfer decisions. ## [LogitScope: Analyzing LLM Uncertainty via Token-Level Information Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24929) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LogitScope is a lightweight, open-source framework that computes token-level information metrics such as entropy, varentropy, and surprisal from LLM probability distributions to analyze uncertainty during generation. It is model-agnostic and compatible with any HuggingFace model, enabling hallucination detection and production monitoring without labeled data. This framework provides a practical, computationally efficient tool for understanding LLM confidence at each token, which is crucial for reliable deployment in real-world applications. It addresses the growing need for model monitoring and hallucination detection, benefiting both researchers and practitioners in the AI ecosystem. LogitScope uses lazy evaluation to achieve computational efficiency and requires no semantic interpretation or labeled data. It demonstrates utility across uncertainty quantification, model behavior analysis, and production monitoring, and is compatible with any HuggingFace model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) generate text token by token, and each token has a probability distribution over the vocabulary. Entropy measures the uncertainty of a distribution, while varentropy captures the variability of uncertainty across tokens, and surprisal indicates how unexpected a token is. Traditional evaluation methods often provide only aggregate scores, lacking token-level insight. LogitScope fills this gap by computing these metrics at each generation step, allowing fine-grained analysis of model confidence.

## [Light-MER Challenges Need for Large Multimodal Emotion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12787) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Light-MER, a lightweight multimodal emotion recognition framework that uses knowledge distillation to achieve better performance and faster inference than larger models. It proposes two new optimization strategies: an optimal transport loss combining Sliced Wasserstein Distance with hidden-state alignment, and a multi-reward optimization based on GRPO. This challenges the prevailing trend of scaling up multimodal emotion recognition models, offering a more efficient alternative for real-time deployment on resource-constrained devices. It could influence future research toward smaller, more practical models in affective computing. Light-MER transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a sub-billion-parameter student model, achieving state-of-the-art results on nine benchmark datasets. The framework includes two novel optimization strategies: an optimal transport loss combining Sliced Wasserstein Distance with hidden-state alignment, and a multi-reward optimization based on GRPO.

## [NeSyFS: Neuro-Symbolic Fast-Slow Thinking for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28942) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces NeSyFS, a neuro-symbolic framework that uses knowledge graphs to represent belief states and combines fast reactive actions with slow uncertainty-aware planning based on twisted sequential Monte Carlo, addressing partial observability in LLM agents. Experiments on ALFWorld, Webshop, and ScienceWorld show significant improvements over prior methods. This framework addresses critical challenges in deploying LLM agents in real-world settings where full environmental states are unavailable, improving decision-making under uncertainty. It offers a unified approach that could enhance autonomous agents in applications like scientific discovery and retrieval-augmented generation, potentially influencing future agent design. NeSyFS uses a knowledge graph to represent belief states, providing triplets as context for each module. The fast-thinking module performs reactive actions, while the slow-thinking module uses a twisted sequential Monte Carlo algorithm for uncertainty-aware planning; a reflection module mitigates task-objective misalignment and switches to slow thinking when reactive actions repeatedly fail.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often operate under partial observability, where they only receive limited observations rather than full environmental states, leading to challenges like belief state inference and planning under uncertainty. Traditional approaches condition actions on full or summarized action-observation histories, which can include redundant or irrelevant information. Neuro-symbolic methods combine neural networks with symbolic reasoning, and knowledge graphs provide a structured way to represent belief states. Fast-slow thinking is inspired by human cognition, where fast thinking is intuitive and reactive, while slow thinking is deliberate and analytical.

The paper introduces Localized Multi-Agent Debate (LMAD), an inference-time protocol that restricts debate to conflicting local segments of agent rationales. It achieves the highest macro-averaged judge accuracy across ten backbones, outperforming the strongest baseline by up to 7.20 percentage points. This method improves both efficiency and accuracy of multi-agent debate, which is crucial for multi-hop question answering. It could influence future inference-time reasoning techniques and reduce computational overhead in LLM-based systems. LMAD represents agent rationales as typed nodes, locates the earliest conflict, and uses guarded resolution to extend a shared committed state. It was evaluated on four multi-hop QA benchmarks using ten backbones from four model families.

## [Pre-Evidence Procedural Failures in Agentic RAG and the Read-Gate Invariant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02011) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces and analyzes pre-evidence procedural failures in agentic RAG systems, where agents skip reading retrieved evidence before finalizing. It proposes a minimal runtime invariant called Read-Gate, which forces reading after search and before finalization, improving LLM accuracy by 14.9-19.9 points on trajectories that would otherwise skip reading. This work highlights a critical yet underappreciated failure mode in agentic RAG systems, showing that evidence gathering should be evaluated as a trajectory-level control problem separate from answer-side reasoning. The proposed Read-Gate invariant offers a simple, effective mitigation that can significantly improve accuracy, with implications for designing more reliable RAG systems. The study decomposes wrong answers into pre-evidence discipline failures and post-gold-read failures using saved tool-call traces, retrieved evidence, read passages, and final answers. Across 12,000 paired trajectories on HotpotQA, 2WikiMultiHopQA, and MuSiQue, the two failure types are largely non-redundant, with a both-trigger rate of 11.2%-13.1% across regex and spaCy entity extractors. Additional diagnostics show that larger hidden thinking budgets do not necessarily increase evidence inspection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems combine large language models with retrieval tools to answer questions by fetching and reading relevant evidence. However, these agents may fail to actually read the retrieved snippets before generating a final answer, a failure mode that occurs before evidence-conditioned reasoning is even tested. This paper treats this as a procedural property of the agent trajectory and proposes a runtime invariant to enforce reading, drawing on concepts from runtime verification and control theory.

SkillTrace introduces a three-level graph method for composing reusable skills in LLM agents, achieving state-of-the-art success rates of 53.17% on SkillsBench and 91.43% on ALFWorld. The approach consistently improves performance across different backbone language models. This work addresses a key bottleneck in LLM agent development: selecting complete and executable skill compositions rather than just retrieving individual skills. By demonstrating consistent gains across benchmarks and backbones, SkillTrace highlights the potential of graph-based reasoning for scalable and robust skill reuse, which could accelerate progress in autonomous agents. SkillTrace organizes the user query into a semantic hierarchy, matches skill queries and candidates, and propagates over skill dependencies. The three-level graph captures compositional relations among skill queries, similarity between queries and candidates, and dependencies among selected candidates.

## [CollaFuse: Split-Learning Diffusion Models Cut Client Compute](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14429) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CollaFuse introduces a split-learning-based method for collaborative diffusion model training, dividing the denoising process between clients and a shared server. This reduces client computational burden during image synthesis while preserving privacy by keeping raw data local. This approach addresses key challenges in federated learning for generative AI, making diffusion model training feasible for resource-constrained clients. It could enable broader adoption of collaborative generative models in edge computing and privacy-sensitive applications. Experiments on CelebA, CIFAR-10, and Animals-with-Attributes2 show enhanced performance and reduced information disclosure. The method retains data and computationally inexpensive processes locally while outsourcing expensive processes to shared server resources.

**Background**: Diffusion models are a class of generative models that synthesize images by iteratively denoising random noise. Federated learning enables collaborative training without sharing raw data, but it often imposes heavy computational loads on clients. Split learning is a technique that divides a model across clients and a server, reducing client workload. CollaFuse combines these ideas to train diffusion models collaboratively.

## [EUDA: Parameter-Efficient UDA with Frozen DINOv2 Backbone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21311) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EUDA, a parameter-efficient unsupervised domain adaptation framework that uses a frozen DINOv2 backbone and updates only a lightweight bottleneck and classification head. It also proposes a synergistic domain alignment loss (SDAL) combining cross-entropy and maximum mean discrepancy to enhance discriminative learning and cross-domain alignment. This approach significantly reduces computational cost and trainable parameters (by 42% to 99.7%) while maintaining competitive performance, making UDA more accessible for resource-constrained and distributed environments. It demonstrates that frozen self-supervised foundation models can be effectively leveraged for domain adaptation without full fine-tuning, potentially influencing future efficient transfer learning methods. EUDA was evaluated on Office-Home, Office-31, VisDA-2017, and DomainNet, achieving competitive results across diverse domain complexities. The SDAL loss combines cross-entropy for discriminative learning and MMD for domain alignment, and the method reduces trainable parameters by up to 99.7% compared to full fine-tuning.

VisPath is a new framework that uses multi-path reasoning and feedback-driven optimization to improve LLM-based visualization code generation for underspecified natural language requests. This framework addresses a common limitation in LLM-based code generation: handling underspecified user requests that often require manual intervention. This paper introduces a data-efficient framework that adapts generative Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) into universal embedding models without extensive contrastive pre-training. It proposes hierarchical embedding prompts and Self-aware Hard Negative Sampling (SaHa) to achieve competitive fine-tuning performance on the Massive Multimodal Embedding Benchmark using only a fraction of standard training data. This work addresses the resource-intensive nature of adapting MLLMs to embedding tasks, potentially lowering the barrier for building robust multimodal retrieval systems. The proposed methods could impact multimodal representation learning and retrieval, benefiting researchers and practitioners who lack extensive computational resources. The framework uses hierarchical embedding prompts that anchor task definitions at the system level to bridge the modality gap and unlock zero-shot embedding capabilities. SaHa shifts hard negative mining from candidate-space to query-space by mapping retrieved candidates back to their owner queries, filtering out semantic false negatives, and constructs mutually hard clusters to maximize intra-task discrimination and batch efficiency.

The SED method injects a bipartite graph to decouple salient from normal views at both disease and view levels, aligning inference with obstetric practice.

## [GraphCliff: Gating Mechanism to Detect Activity Cliffs in Drug Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03170) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GraphCliff introduces a locally conditioned gating mechanism that integrates short and long range information at the node level in graph neural networks, improving sensitivity to subtle molecular differences that cause activity cliffs. The model consistently outperforms strong baseline graph models on both cliff and non-cliff compounds. This work addresses a critical challenge in drug discovery: activity cliffs, where structurally similar compounds have vastly different potencies. By improving GNN sensitivity to these subtle differences, GraphCliff could lead to more accurate predictive models for drug efficacy, potentially accelerating drug development. The paper includes layer-wise embedding analyses showing that GraphCliff's gains come from sharper discrimination of structurally similar molecules. The approach is validated on recent activity cliff benchmarks, where extended connectivity fingerprints previously outperformed conventional GNNs.

**Background**: Activity cliffs are pairs of structurally similar compounds with large potency differences, breaking the smooth structure-activity relationship assumption. Extended connectivity fingerprints (ECFPs) are a traditional molecular representation that has shown strong performance on activity cliff benchmarks. ## [Target-Aligned Fusion for Decision-Sequence Learning under Dynamics Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09173) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Target-Aligned Fusion (TAF), a principled framework for fusing external trajectories with limited target data in Decision Transformer learning under dynamics shift. TAF derives a gate-then-weight rule based on measurable data-alignment quantities and instantiates it as TAF-DT, which uses MMD for fragment selection and optimal transport for weighting. This work addresses a significant challenge in offline reinforcement learning: leveraging external data when the environment dynamics differ. By providing a principled and theoretically grounded approach, TAF-DT achieves strong performance across various dynamics shifts, which could improve the practical applicability of offline RL in real-world scenarios where dynamics are non-stationary. TAF bounds the target-domain Bellman risk by two measurable quantities: state-structure mismatch (Δ_m) and weighted transport cost (Δ_w). The TAF-DT instantiation uses maximum mean discrepancy (MMD) for fragment selection, optimal transport for feasibility-aware weighting, and advantage-token relabeling with Q-regularized Transformer training. Experiments on D4RL-style control tasks with gravity, kinematic, and morphology shifts show TAF-DT achieves the strongest aggregate performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Decision Transformer reformulates reinforcement learning as a sequence modeling problem, training transformers on trajectories of state, action, and return-to-go. In offline RL, dynamics shift occurs when the source environment differs from the target, making some external trajectories inconsistent. TAF addresses this by aligning external data to the target domain's structure and feasibility, using tools like MMD and optimal transport.

## [PRIVEE: New Defense Against Feature Inference Attacks in Vertical Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12840) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PRIVEE is a novel defense mechanism for vertical federated learning that obfuscates confidence scores to prevent feature inference attacks while maintaining model accuracy. It achieves up to a 30 times increase in reconstruction error (MSE) against feature inference attacks compared to the strongest competing defense. This addresses a critical security vulnerability in vertical federated learning, where adversarial parties can reconstruct private features from shared confidence scores. By preserving model accuracy while mitigating attacks, PRIVEE could enable safer collaborative learning across organizations, impacting industries like healthcare and finance that rely on sensitive data. PRIVEE obfuscates confidence scores while preserving critical properties such as relative ranking and inter-score distances, rather than exposing raw scores. Experiments show it preserves full predictive performance against advanced feature inference attacks, with up to 30 times improvement in reconstruction error (MSE).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Vertical Federated Learning (VFL) enables collaborative model training across organizations that share common user samples but hold disjoint feature spaces. However, VFL is susceptible to feature inference attacks, where adversarial parties exploit shared confidence scores (prediction probabilities) during inference to reconstruct private input features of other participants. PRIVEE counters this by obfuscating confidence scores, a technique also known as confidence score obfuscation in AI security.

This paper introduces a framework to localize and intervene on intra-memory knowledge conflicts in language models, finding that conflicts are often resolved in the final layers and that targeted attention-head interventions are more effective than layer-wise ones. This work provides a first mechanistic account of intra-memory conflict resolution, which is crucial for interpretability and model editing. It also highlights a significant gap between synthetic and real-world settings, guiding future research. The framework was tested on four language models using both synthetic and real-world knowledge conflicts. The filtering analysis shows that heads specialized for a single competing fact are more common in synthetic conflicts, explaining why interventions are less effective on real-world conflicts.

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding but often rely on spatial semantics and lack fine-grained temporal awareness. ## [E4GEN: Explainable Diffusion for Extreme-Event Time-Series Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

E4GEN is an explainable diffusion framework that generates time series with accurate extreme events, introducing three components: E-Activator, E-Predictor, and E-Control. It outperforms state-of-the-art models on six datasets across 17 metrics. This addresses a critical gap in time-series generation, where existing methods often fail to capture extreme events, which are vital in fields like finance and climate science. E4GEN's explainable control mechanisms could improve trust and utility in downstream applications. E-Activator learns dataset-adaptive activation steps during denoising, E-Predictor uses Self-Driven Semantic Prediction with Data-Conditioned Training and Noise-Initiated Sampling to handle missing labels, and E-Control injects layer-wise signals via a trainable Extreme Control Network. The framework is evaluated on six datasets with 17 metrics, showing improvements in fidelity and downstream utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series generation is used in various fields, but existing generative models often prioritize overall distributional fidelity over capturing rare extreme events. Diffusion models generate data by iteratively denoising random noise, and control signals can guide this process. E4GEN introduces explainable control mechanisms to specifically enhance extreme-event generation.

## [MalariAI: Annotation-Agnostic Framework for Malaria Cell Segmentation and Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00385) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MalariAI introduces a two-stage, annotation-agnostic framework for cell segmentation and explainable malaria stage classification, achieving 75.95% cell recovery and a clinically relevant AP of 29.10%. This framework addresses critical limitations in automated malaria diagnosis, such as annotation bias and NMS suppression in dense smears, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy and clinical trust. It offers a scalable solution for regions with expert scarcity. Stage 1 uses a watershed algorithm for cell isolation without ground-truth input, while Stage 2 fine-tunes EfficientNet-B0 with Focal Loss, achieving 98.36% classification accuracy. Grad-CAM++ heatmaps provide per-cell spatial evidence, with activation concentrated on cell bodies significantly above chance.

The watershed algorithm treats images as topographic maps to segment cells, and AP/mAP are standard metrics for object detection performance.

## [Benchmarking Intensity Normalization for Knee MRI Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20028) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study systematically compared seven intensity normalization methods for 3D U-Net knee MRI segmentation, finding that Z-score, Ny'ul histogram matching, and CLAHE improve cross-domain generalizability on external data. This work addresses an underexplored factor in medical imaging that affects model generalizability, providing practical guidance for clinical deployment. The findings highlight that while normalization helps, domain shift remains a major challenge, emphasizing the need for complementary strategies. Models were trained on the IWOAI 2019 dataset and evaluated on internal and external test sets (SKM-TEA). Performance differences were small internally but significant on external data, with Z-score, Ny'ul, and CLAHE showing greater robustness, though the overall performance drop between datasets was larger.
