## [TriGlue: Biology-Inspired Generative Model for Molecular Glue Ternary Complexes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22143) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TriGlue is a novel generative framework that formulates molecular glue design as a ternary complex generation problem, decomposing it into an SE(3)-equivariant interface estimation stage and an interface-conditioned flow matching stage for joint ligand generation and complex assembly. The model generates chemically valid molecules and plausible ternary complexes, as demonstrated in extensive experiments. This work addresses a relatively unexplored area in computational drug design—molecular glue design—which is crucial for targeted protein degradation (TPD). By providing a generative model that can design molecular glues and predict ternary complex structures, TriGlue could accelerate the discovery of novel TPD therapeutics, impacting the pharmaceutical industry and patients with previously undruggable diseases. TriGlue uses an SE(3)-equivariant interface estimation module to predict protein-protein interfaces from unbound monomers, and a flow matching network to jointly generate the molecular glue and predict rigid-body transformations for ternary complex assembly. The code is publicly available on GitHub, and the model is evaluated using metrics like DockQ and Vina.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Molecular glue degraders are small molecules that induce ternary complex formation between an E3 ubiquitin ligase and a target protein, leading to targeted protein degradation via the ubiquitin-proteasome pathway. Unlike traditional structure-based drug design, molecular glue design requires modeling unknown protein-protein interfaces and simultaneous ligand generation, protein-protein docking, and ternary complex assembly. SE(3)-equivariant neural networks are architectures that respect 3D rotational and translational symmetries, making them suitable for predicting molecular structures.

## [Cortex: Compact Quake Policy via Behavioral Cloning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22739) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Cortex, a compact transformer-based policy with 10.98 million trainable parameters and a frozen DINOv3 encoder, achieves notable performance in Quake E1M1 using only behavioral cloning, with 19 of 20 episodes recording at least one kill. It is trained on 6,849 recordings (474.7 hours) from the Pixels2Play corpus, and each sampled training epoch takes 3.3 minutes on a single RTX 5080. This work demonstrates that a simple, compact architecture can achieve strong performance in a visually rich game without reinforcement learning or explicit memory, potentially influencing future imitation learning and game AI research. It highlights the effectiveness of frozen visual features and efficient training, which could reduce computational costs for game-playing agents. Cortex uses a six-layer transformer over a frozen DINOv3 encoder, processing 17.09 million cached decision frames. Ablations show that denser visual tokens improve combat and survival, while longer optimization and naive action history improve offline metrics but not consistently in actual play. The policy does not complete the level but consistently reaches the opening door, button room, and gate descent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Behavioral cloning (BC) is a supervised learning approach that maps observed states to expert actions, commonly used in imitation learning for game AI. Transformer-based policies use self-attention to capture long-range dependencies in sequential decision-making tasks. DINOv3 is a self-supervised Vision Transformer (ViT) foundation model pretrained on natural images, often used as a frozen encoder for downstream tasks. The Pixels2Play corpus provides a large dataset of human gameplay recordings for training such policies.

AgentGUI is a locally hosted GUI that enables users to observe and steer AI agents across multiple concurrent, long-running sessions. It features rich trajectory visualizations, manual and automated steering, and integration with open-source and frontier agent frameworks. This tool addresses the growing need for human oversight of increasingly autonomous AI agents, potentially improving efficiency and reliability in real-world deployments. It offers a practical solution for developers and operators to manage complex agent tasks, reducing trace inspection time by 38% and improving task completion via drift prevention. In a controlled user study, AgentGUI reduced the time to identify key elements from agent traces by 38% (p = 0.023). Its automated drift prevention feature raised task completion rates of small local agents by up to 34 percentage points across a 0.8B–9B model ladder (N=50 runs per model).

A new benchmark evaluates LLM competence in logical inference over probability operators, using 14,320 procedurally-generated English prompts across fifteen inference templates. Testing 29 models, it finds systematic answer biases independent of logical form, with only 9 of 29 models exceeding random chance. This benchmark provides a rigorous method to quantify LLM capabilities in formal reasoning over uncertainty expressions, which is crucial for high-stakes domains like medicine and law. The findings highlight significant limitations in current models' logical reasoning, potentially guiding future improvements in AI reasoning. The benchmark systematically varies question form, negation strategy, and surface content, and also tests variations in verb phrases/activity, gender, and origin of names. The 'competence floor' metric summarizes the worse of a model's accuracy on Yes-correct and No-correct items, revealing biases across every axis.

## [FFCResNet Enables First Large-Scale ECG Interval Accuracy Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00058) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an end-to-end ECG delineation pipeline using Fast Fourier Convolution ResNet (FFCResNet) and reports the first large-scale interval measurement accuracy study on 10,646 clinical 12-lead ECGs, with full statistical characterization including bias, Bland-Altman limits of agreement, and rhythm-stratified errors. This work addresses a critical gap in ECG delineation literature by focusing on clinical interval accuracy rather than just fiducial-point errors, which is directly relevant to cardiac diagnosis. The large-scale validation and honest characterization of performance across different rhythms could improve trust and adoption of automated ECG analysis in clinical settings. The system achieves a QT MAE of 17.5 ms (95% CI: 16.9-18.2), QRS duration MAE of 14.8 ms, and ventricular rate MAE of 0.8 beats/min, with all biases statistically significant but within inter-observer variability for sinus rhythms. However, rhythm-stratified analysis reveals substantially higher QT errors for supraventricular tachycardias (SVT MAE: 75.0 ms; AVRT MAE: 85.3 ms) compared to sinus bradycardia (9.3 ms) and sinus rhythm (8.9 ms).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: ECG delineation involves identifying the boundaries of P, QRS, and T waves, which are essential for measuring intervals like PR, QRS duration, and QT/QTc. Traditional methods often rely on fiducial point detection, but clinical decisions depend on interval accuracy. Fast Fourier Convolution (FFC) is a neural network operator that combines local temporal convolutions with global spectral processing via FFT, enabling the model to capture both local and global context. Bland-Altman analysis is a standard statistical method for assessing agreement between two measurement techniques, providing bias and limits of agreement.

## [LLM-OSDA: Dynamic Auction for Native Ads in Multi-Turn LLM Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00123) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LLM-OSDA, a dynamic cost-per-click auction mechanism that integrates Bellman optimal stopping, winner allocation, and envelope pricing for native advertising in multi-turn LLM conversations. It uses a bid-independent LLM layer to estimate click quality and render ads, while bids only enter the committed auction mechanism. This work addresses a novel and timely problem in LLM-native advertising, shifting the unit of sale from a fixed slot to a moment within an evolving conversation. It provides a rigorous mechanism design approach that ensures truthful bidding and improves revenue, which could influence how ads are integrated into conversational AI systems. The mechanism guarantees monotone expected discounted-click allocation and truthful bidding in expectation under an exact Bellman oracle. For practical deployment, a learned StopNet approximates Bellman action values, with decisions differing from optimal only near the stopping boundary, and experiments show an 11% net revenue improvement over fixed-timing baselines while maintaining user retention.

**Background**: Optimal stopping problems, such as the secretary problem, are often solved using Bellman equations and dynamic programming. Envelope pricing, based on the envelope theorem, is a standard technique in mechanism design to derive truthful payment rules. LLM-native advertising embeds sponsored content directly into model-generated responses, requiring new auction mechanisms that handle timing and allocation jointly.

## [ProPRL: Property-Aware Framework for Prerequisite Relation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03006) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ProPRL is a novel framework for prerequisite relation learning in educational knowledge graphs, which adaptively fuses complementary evidence from a concept-resource hypergraph and a directed learning-behavior graph, and enforces an irreversibility constraint to penalize contradictory reverse predictions. This work addresses key limitations of existing link prediction methods in prerequisite relation learning, which often fail to adaptively integrate educational evidence and may produce contradictory predictions. By improving the accuracy of prerequisite relations, ProPRL can enhance adaptive instruction and personalized learning experiences. ProPRL uses direction-preserving personalized propagation to aggregate multi-hop behavioral evidence, and a Pair-conditioned Gate to adaptively weight and fuse two views for each candidate ordered concept pair. The irreversibility constraint is an anti-symmetry regularizer that penalizes high confidence in both directions of the same concept pair, and experiments on multiple real-world datasets show state-of-the-art performance.

**Background**: Prerequisite relation learning is a fundamental task in educational technology that identifies dependencies between learning resources or concepts to facilitate personalized learning and optimize content organization. Educational knowledge graphs (EduKGs) structure this information as entities and relationships, and link prediction methods are often applied to infer prerequisite relations. However, conventional link prediction may not fully exploit the complementary evidence from different data sources or enforce the inherent asymmetry of prerequisite relations.

## [Evidence-Grounded Multimodal KG Pipeline for Lecture Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03161) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a novel evidence-grounded multimodal pipeline that constructs knowledge graphs from lecture videos by combining transcription, OCR, and vision-language models. In initial tests on three neural-network lectures, it achieved 90.38% endpoint coverage and 100% accuracy on a three-question retrieval test. This work addresses the limitation of transcript-only retrieval in preserving multimodal information from lecture videos, offering an auditable method for educational knowledge extraction. It could enhance educational technology by enabling more accurate and explainable retrieval of concepts and relationships from video content. The pipeline processed 3,118 frames, 756 transcript segments, and 559 anchors, retaining 1,022 concept and 312 relationship mentions, which were canonicalized into 172 concepts and 282 relationships. The authors emphasize that the contribution is an auditable construction method rather than a state-of-the-art performance claim.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Lecture videos contain knowledge distributed across speech, slides, diagrams, and equations, which traditional transcript-only retrieval fails to fully capture. Knowledge graphs organize information as concepts and relationships, and provenance-rich graphs track the evidence supporting each statement. Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, enabling extraction from multimodal content. This pipeline leverages these technologies to build an auditable educational knowledge graph.

## [Enactive AI: A Decision-Centric Framework for Complex Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03413) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces Enactive AI, a conceptual framework for enterprise and industrial reasoning, organized around four complementary roles: Organizational World, Site World, Schema Intelligence, and Enactive Decision Cycle. It aims to expand AI from model capability to system-aware action. This framework addresses the gap between AI capabilities and real-world complex systems, emphasizing reliability, feasibility, resilience, and responsibility. It could guide future AI deployment in enterprise and industrial settings, making AI more governable and socially valuable. The framework includes an Organizational World for strategic-institutional logic, a Site World for operational-realization, Schema Intelligence as a coupling mechanism, and an Enactive Decision Cycle for self-evolving updates. However, the paper is largely theoretical and lacks empirical validation, with an incomplete abstract.

**Background**: World models in AI are systems that learn internal simulations of environments, enabling agents to predict outcomes before acting. The Organizational World Model represents how an organization works, while the Site World Model is a physically bounded operational model. Enactive AI draws on enactive cognition, emphasizing learning from consequences.

## [AI Policy Divergence in US Higher Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03584) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new preprint analyzes AI policies across US higher education institutions from 34 states using NLP, revealing that university-level policies focus on data security and risk mitigation, while school-level policies emphasize pedagogy and tool usage. The study also finds that few business schools have AI policies distinct from university frameworks. This research highlights a critical gap in AI governance within higher education, which could lead to misalignment between institutional policies and discipline-specific learning objectives. It underscores the need for aligned yet flexible policies to prepare students for AI-driven workforce demands. The study uses natural language processing (NLP) to analyze institutional AI policies, finding a clear divergence between university-level and school-level policies. Business schools, in particular, often lack distinct AI policies, creating challenges for faculty, students, and accreditation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Natural language processing (NLP) is a subfield of computer science that uses computational methods to analyze text and extract meaningful information, increasingly applied in policymaking. Higher education institutions are adopting AI but struggle with how to integrate it into curricula and governance, especially when policies are set at different levels.

## [SAT-Edge-Agent: Hardware-in-the-Loop Edge-Agent Orchestration for Onboard Satellite Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03728) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SAT-Edge-Agent presents a hardware-in-the-loop (HIL) edge-agent system deployed on a commercial off-the-shelf ARM-based heterogeneous edge system-on-chip, coordinating a browser workspace, FastAPI agent, local OpenAI-compatible language service, and a YOLO-style oriented-object-detection endpoint. In experiments with two FAIR1M workloads repeated 20 times each, it achieved 20/20 task completions, with mean full-agent latencies of 29.353 s and 60.937 s, while detector time was only 2.93% and 2.48% of full-agent means. This work establishes a reproducible HIL boundary for observable satellite edge-agent orchestration, addressing the need for onboard intelligence that translates mission intent into local tool calls under communication and power constraints. It demonstrates that edge-agent systems can achieve reliable task completion with low detector latency, potentially enabling more autonomous satellite operations and reducing reliance on ground-based processing. The system uses a local OpenAI-compatible language service and a project-internal YOLO-style oriented-object-detection endpoint that returns FAIR1M metadata-backed structured results. Profiling shows most visible latency occurs outside detector execution, with mean CPU utilization around 20.5%, and a 200-ms NPU-load field averaged 100% but represents a shared-accelerator software field rather than calibrated utilization. The public evidence package includes sanitized request-level records, redacted JSON, normalized SSE examples, and scripts to reproduce the statistics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Onboard satellite intelligence requires a task layer that translates mission intent into local tool calls and returns machine-consumable artifacts under communication and power constraints. Hardware-in-the-loop (HIL) testing involves running the actual hardware in a simulated environment to validate system behavior. The FAIR1M dataset is a benchmark for fine-grained oriented object detection in remote sensing imagery, containing over 1 million instances and 15,000+ images. Oriented object detection aims to locate and classify objects with arbitrary orientations, which is crucial for aerial and satellite imagery analysis.

## [Low-Cost Hybrid Reservoir Computing for Sign Language Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03444) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a lightweight hybrid reservoir computing (HRC) model for isolated sign language video recognition, combining deep reservoir computing (DRC) and bidirectional reservoir computing (BRC). It achieves Top-1, Top-5, and Top-10 accuracies of 61.12%, 86.05%, and 92.56% on the WLASL100 dataset, with training time reduced to a few seconds. This work addresses the high computational cost of deep learning in sign language recognition, enabling deployment on edge devices. It demonstrates that reservoir computing can achieve competitive accuracy with significantly lower resource requirements, potentially making SLR more accessible in real-world applications. The model uses MediaPipe to extract body and hand keypoints, which are processed by the HRC architecture, and a ridge regression model maps the final state to class labels. The training time is drastically reduced compared to deep learning methods like Bi-GRU, highlighting its lightweight nature.

MediaPipe is a library that provides pre-trained models for extracting human body keypoints. WLASL100 is a benchmark dataset for word-level American Sign Language recognition, containing 100 common words.

## [VIBE: New Benchmark for Affective Profiling of LLM Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03810) ⭐️ 6.0/10

VIBE is a newly introduced benchmark for entity-centered affective profiling of large language model outputs, using Valence-Arousal-Dominance (VAD) dimensions and a structured 'Affective Passport' reporting format. It separates generation from external scoring and distinguishes scalar favorability, response-level VAD, and target-directed VAD. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing sentiment and emotion benchmarks by combining target-directed VAD attribution, an explicit scorer contract, and a passport reporting format. It provides a standardized way to document affective profiles, which is important for transparency and reproducibility in NLP research and for understanding how LLMs frame socially salient targets. The benchmark includes three empirical layers: H1 shows scalar favorability does not subsume arousal and dominance, with valence cross-validated (rV = 0.944 judge-human, rV = 0.954 inter-scorer) but arousal and dominance being single-scorer directional estimates (rA = 0.495, rD = 0.702 among human annotators). H2 demonstrates that whole-response and target-directed VAD are different contracts, and H3 is a protocol-drift diagnostic showing that elicitation conditions shift profiles.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Valence-Arousal-Dominance (VAD) is a dimensional framework that quantifies emotions along three continuous, orthogonal axes: valence (positivity/negativity), arousal (calmness/excitement), and dominance (control/submission). Existing sentiment and emotion benchmarks capture parts of affective space but do not combine target-directed VAD attribution with an explicit scorer contract and a structured reporting format. The 'Affective Passport' is a reporting format inspired by communication passports, which present information in an easy-to-follow manner.

## [SciRet: Compute-Aware Study of Retrieval & Reranking for Scientific RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03860) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SciRet evaluates a fixed scientific RAG pipeline on CORD-19 across three corpus scales, finding hybrid retrieval robust (Recall@10=1.000 at 1K and 15K) and MS MARCO-trained rerankers harmful due to domain mismatch. This study provides practical insights for building scientific RAG systems, highlighting the robustness of hybrid retrieval and the negative impact of domain mismatch in rerankers, which can guide system design and model selection. The pipeline uses sentence-window chunking, BM25, BGE-M3 dense retrieval, reciprocal rank fusion, optional cross-encoder reranking, and grounded answer generation. Retrieval evaluation uses pseudo-relevance labels from the hybrid system, so results are treated as controlled comparative evidence, not benchmark claims.

## [Compound and Parallel Tropical Convolutions Boost TCNN Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06881) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces two novel tropical convolution operators, compound tropical convolution (cTCNN) and parallel tropical convolution (pTCNN), which combine min-plus and max-plus operations within a single layer to enhance representational capacity while maintaining low computational cost. The authors provide an open-source PyTorch-compatible implementation with optimized GPU kernels using TileLang. This work addresses the accuracy-efficiency tradeoff in tropical CNNs, making them more viable for deployment on resource-constrained devices. By achieving competitive performance against standard CNNs with significantly fewer multiplications, these variants could enable efficient deep learning in edge computing and real-time applications. The proposed cTCNN and pTCNN layers were evaluated on image classification and semantic segmentation benchmarks, showing competitive performance while reducing multiplications. Hybrid models that integrate both tropical and conventional convolutions further improve the accuracy-efficiency balance. The implementation is open-source and uses TileLang for optimized GPU kernels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Convolutional neural networks (CNNs) rely heavily on multiplication operations, which are computationally expensive. Tropical convolutional neural networks (TCNNs) replace multiplications with min-plus or max-plus operations, which are cheaper but often reduce accuracy. This paper extends TCNNs by combining both operations to improve representational capacity without significant computational overhead.

## [DIB-OD: Robust Heterogeneous Graph Adaptation via Decoupled Info Bottleneck and Online Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10882) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces DIB-OD, a novel framework that combines a decoupled information bottleneck with online distillation to preserve invariant knowledge during heterogeneous graph adaptation. It demonstrates consistent improvements over baselines on seven graph-classification datasets spanning chemical, biological, and social-network domains. This work addresses the critical problem of negative transfer and knowledge overwriting in graph pre-training and domain adaptation, which are common in real-world applications. By providing a theoretically grounded and empirically validated method, it offers a solid incremental contribution that could improve the robustness and transferability of graph neural networks across diverse domains. The framework uses a multiview teacher to learn a compressed, task-relevant representation, which is distilled into a transferable core branch and a complementary residual branch. It employs mutual-information objectives and HSIC-based dependence regularization to discourage information overlap, along with a confidence-aware semantic regularizer and a frozen teacher to anchor reliable pretrained information during adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Graph pre-training aims to learn transferable representations from large-scale graph data, but structural and feature shifts between source and target domains can cause negative transfer. The information bottleneck method, introduced by Tishby et al., provides a principled way to compress representations while preserving task-relevant information. Online distillation is a knowledge distillation approach where teacher and student models are trained simultaneously, often without a fixed teacher. HSIC is a kernel-based statistical measure of dependence used to regularize representations.

## [Minmax-CF: Traceable Single-Cell Data Distillation via Discrete Min-Max Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19426) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Minmax-CF, a novel method for single-cell data distillation that selects real cells and genes via discrete min-max optimization, retaining exact source-cell indices and original gene symbols. It achieves 95.3% of the Full-reference macro-F1 on average across five benchmarks and outperforms synthetic baselines in 24 of 25 comparisons. This work addresses the critical issue of traceability in single-cell data distillation, enabling source-level inspection and biological interpretation, which is essential for regulatory compliance and scientific reproducibility. It offers a more reliable alternative to synthetic-cell-based methods, potentially improving downstream analyses in bioinformatics. Minmax-CF formulates compression as a discrete min-max selection problem over characteristic-function directions, using entropy-regularized maximization and greedy minimization to rank cells and genes. It alternates cell and gene selection under explicit axis-specific budgets, and retained cells can be projected onto independently computed embeddings for biological interpretation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Single-cell datasets are large and expensive to store and reuse, and data distillation aims to create smaller training sets. Traditional distillation methods often generate synthetic cells that lack direct correspondence to original cells, hindering traceability. Characteristic functions in probability theory describe distributions and are used here to guide selection, while min-max optimization seeks to minimize the worst-case error.

## [PAWNI: Eight-Agent System Improves Prompt Formulation via Guided Q&A](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01366) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces PAWNI (Prompt Architecture Wizard using Neural Intelligence), an eight-agent conversational system that transforms unstructured user queries into structured prompts through guided question-and-answer dialogue. It also proposes a three-tier framework of 18 prompt elements across Essential, Enhancement, and Elevation categories. This work shifts the focus from optimizing LLM responses to optimizing the question itself, which could significantly improve human-AI collaboration on complex tasks. If validated, it offers a practical approach to reduce context degradation and cognitive load in multi-turn prompting, benefiting a wide range of LLM users. In an exploratory within-subjects study (N=4) across four complex tasks, participants using PAWNI produced more structurally complete prompts (42% to 91% of assessed elements), rated LLM outputs higher, and reported lower workload (NASA-TLX 39.6 vs. 21.7). Every participant reached satisfactory output in a single turn, compared to 1-12 turns unaided, though effect sizes are unstable due to the small sample size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 5, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are powerful but their output quality heavily depends on the prompts provided by users. Iterative multi-turn prompting often leads to context degradation and diminishing cognitive returns, as users struggle to refine their queries. PAWNI addresses this by front-loading intent clarification through a conversational interface, using a self-evolving knowledge base to guide the question-and-answer process. The three-tier prompt element framework categorizes essential, enhancement, and elevation elements to structure prompts effectively.
